ASSISTANCE AGREEMENT 


THIS ASSISTANCE AGREEMENT (“Agree- 
ment”), dated July 12, 1984, is entered into by and be- 
tween the WINSTAR CORPORATION, Roseville, 
Minnesota, a Minnesota corporation (“WINSTAR”), 
UNITED FEDERAL SAVINGS BANK, Windom, 
Minnesota, a de novo federal stock savings bank, 
which will be a wholly-owned subsidiary of WIN- 
STAR (“RESULTING ASSOCIATION”), and the 
FEDERAL SAVINGS AND LOAN INSURANCE 
CORPORATION, a corporate instrumentality and 
agency of the United States (“CORPORATION”), 


RECITALS 


A. The CORPORATION insures the accounts of 
WINDOM FEDERAL SAVINGS AND LOAN AS- 
SOCIATION, Windom, Minnesota (“MERGING AS- 
SOCIATION”), and will insure the accounts of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION, in accordance with 
$ 403 of the National Housing Act, as amended, 12 
U.S.C. § 1725 (1982). 

B. The Federal Home Loan Bank Board (“Bank 
Board”), in Resolution No. 84-363, dated July 6, 1984, 
approved (1) the chartering of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION as a de novo federal stock savings 
bank, the accounts of which will be insured by the 
CORPORATION, (2) a proposal by WINSTAR to 
acquire the MERGING ASSOCIATION by means of 
the merger of the MERGING ASSOCIATION into 
the RESULTING ASSOCIATION (“Merger”), (3) 
WINSTAR’s acquisition of the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION’s capital stock, which WINSTAR will 
purchase for two million dollars, and (4) the MERG- 
ING ASSOCIATION’s supervisory conversion to 


92 


stock form and merger with the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION, 

C. The MERGING ASSOCIATION has entered 
into a Combination Agreement and Plan of Merger 
(“Merger Agreement”) with WINSTAR, pursuant to 
which WINSTAR has agreed to cause the RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION to consummate the Merger 
pursuant to such agreement, which also provides that 
the RESULTING ASSOCIATION will succeed to all 
rights, obligations, duties, and liabilities of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION, and all assets and 
property of every kind and character belonging to the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION will be vested in and 
become the property of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION. 

D. A condition precedent to the obligation of the 
parties to consummate such merger is that the 
CORPORATION, the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, 
and WINSTAR enter into an agreement in the form 
of this Agreement, which will provide financial 
assistance and indemnification to the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION, in order to facilitate the Merger as 
approved by the Bank Board. 

E. The CORPORATION has decided, pursuant to 
§ 406(f) of the National Housing Act, as amended, 12 
U.S.C. § 1729(f) (1982), to provide financial assistance 
and indemnification as set forth in this Agreement, 
having determined that the MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION is in danger of default and that the amount of 
such assistance and indemnification would be less 
than the losses the CORPORATION would sustain 
upon the liquidation of the MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION through a receivership accompanied by the 
payment of insurance of accounts. 

F. In consideration of the mutual promises con- 
tained in this Agreement, the parties enter into the 
following agreement. 


93 
AGREEMENT 


$1 Definitions. For the purpose of this Agreement 
the following terms have the indicated sn 
. ty “Bank Board”: The Federal Home Loan Bank 

(b) “Claim”: Any claim, demand, cause of action, 
or judgment. 

(ec) “Control” and “Controls”: These terms have 
the meaning indicated in § 583.26 of the Regulations 
for Savings and Loar Holding Companies. 

(d) “Effective Date”: The date and time at which 
the Merger of the MERGING ASSOCIATION into 
the RESULTING ASSOCIATION becomes effective 
pursuant to the terms of the Merger Agreement and 
applicable law. 

(e) “Financial Report”: The Quarterly Report 
required of insured institutions by the Bank Board or 
any other periodic report required of insured 
institutions. 

(f) “Insurance Regulations”: The Rules and 
Regulations for the CORPORATION. 

(g) Liquidation”: The release, compromise, satis- 
faction, settlement, reduction to judgment, or assign- 
ment to other than the CORPORATION of any 
Claim. 

(h) “Management Standard”: The standard of 
prudent business practice generally employed by 
financial institutions in Minnesota in administering 
their assets and liabilities. 

(i) “Merger”: The supervisory merger of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION into the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION under the Merger Agreement. 

(j) “Merging Association Claim”: Any unrealized 
Claim of the MERGING ASSOCIATION or the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION, as its successor, 


——  —— 


Ea O—eEEeEITr 


94 


against (1) a person serving on or prior to the 
Effective Date as a director, officer, employee, or 
agent of the MERGING ASSOCIATION or any of its 
subsidiaries, provided that an action or inaction of 
such person on or prior to the Effective Date gave 
rise to such Claim; (2) an insurer who may be liable 
for the indemnification of the loss caused by the 
action or inaction of such person; (3) any administra- 
tor, executor, personal representative, heir, or assign 
of any such person; or (4) any other person or 
persons, partnership, corporation, or legal entity 
(“third party”), and any administrator, executor, 
personal representative, heir, or assign of such third 
party, provided that the Claim against such third 
party is related to a Claim against a party referred to 
under (1), (2), or (3) above. 

(k) “Net Proceeds Received”: As used in reference 
to the amount received upon the Liquidation of a 
Claim, the amount computed by deducting from the 
gross proceeds received all reasonable expenses in 
the form of payments to third parties, incurred or 
paid by the RESULTING ASSOCIATION in connec- 
tion with such Liquidation, provided such expenses 
have not been reimbursed otherwise. Such “reason- 
able expenses” may include, without being limited to, 
attorneys’ and accountants’ fees, travel expenses, 
court costs and related litigation expenses, and such 
other actual costs that are directly related to the 
Liquidation of the Claim. 

(l) “Net Worth”: Regulatory Net Worth as 
defined in § 561.13 of the Insurance Regulations and 
as computed in accordance with the accounting 
principles required by this Agreement. 

(m) “Quarter”: A calendar quarter, provided that 
the first Quarter during the term of this Agreement 
shall be deemed to include the period, if any, from the 


Effective Date to the last day preceding the first day 


of the first full calendar quarter following the 
Effective Date. " 


§ 2 Conditions. 


(a) The obligations of the parties are subject to 
the Merger becoming effective no later than 30 days 
after the date of this Agreement, unless such date is 
extended in writing by the Principal or other 
Supervisory Agent of the Bank Board, Des Moines, 
lowa (“Supervisory Agent”). 

(b) The CORPORATION’s obligations under this 
Agreement are also conditioned upon receipt of the 
following: 

(1) An opinion from independent counsel for 
the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, in form and 
substance satisfactory to the CORPORATION and its 
counsel, to the effect that: 

(A) The Merger has been duly authorized by 
the directors of the MERGING ASSOCIATION, 
WINSTAR, and the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
and effected in compliance with the Charter and 
Bylaws of the MERGING ASSOCIATION, 
WINSTAR, and the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
and with applicable law; 

(B) This Agreement has been duly author- 
ized, executed, and delivered, and constitutes a valid 
and binding obligation of WINSTAR and the RE- 
SULTING ASSOCIATION, enforceable in accordance 
with its terms; 

(2) A certified copy of the corporate resolutions 
of WINSTAR and the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
authorizing execution and delivery of the Merger 
Agreement and this Agreement; 

(3) A certified copy of the corporate resolution 
of the MERGING ASSOCIATION authorizing execu- 
tion and delivery of the Merger Agreement; 


96 


(4) Certification by the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION’s Secretary, after completion of the sale of 
its capital stock, stating that the capital stock has 
been sold, the price at which it was sold, the date of 
the sale, and the net proceeds received; 

(5) A copy of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION’s stock certificate, which shall include a 
statement to the effect that the shares are restricted 
as to transfer for a period of three years from the 
date of issuance, except with the prior written 
consent of the CORPORATION; 

(6) An opinion of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION’s independent counsel that all applicable se- 
curities law requirements have been met in connec- 
tion with the sale of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION’s stock. 

(c) The CORPORATION’s obligations under this 
Agreement are further conditioned upon 
WINSTAR’s: 

(1) Maintaining, for so long as WINSTAR 
directly or indirectly controls the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION, but in no event for less than three 
years, the RESULTING ASSOCIATION’s Net 
Worth at an amount not less than the minimum 
amount prescribed in § 563.13 of the Insurance 
Regulations for institutions insured twenty years or 
longer; 

(2) Causing the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
to perform its obligations under this Agreement; and 

(3) Guaranteeing the satisfaction of any obliga- 
tion the RESULTING ASSOCIATION assumes un- 
der this Agreement. 

$3 Audit. As soon as is practicable after the 
Effective Date, the CORPORATION, at its own 
expense but with the assistance of the RESULTING 


97 


ASSOCIATION’s personnel, whose salaries and ex- 
penses shall be paid by the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION without reimbursement from the CORPORA- 
TION, shall prepare an audit of the MERGING 
ASSOCIATION as of the Effective Date in accord- 
ance with generally accepted auditing standards, as 
modified by the CORPORATION. The scope of the 
audit shall be limited to that which the CORPORA- 
TION determines to be necessary to implement this 
Agreement and shall not include a revaluation of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION’s assets, except that 
valuation reserves established by the MERGING 
ASSOCIATION since June 30, 1983, other than those 
established at the request of the Supervisory Agent, 
shall be revalued if the CORPORATION requests 
such revaluation. Upon its completion and approval 
by the CORPORATION, a copy of the audit, which 
shall be prepared in such form as the CORPORA- 
TION shall determine, shall be furnished to the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 
§ 4 Contributions. 

(a) Cash Contribution. The CORPORATION 
shall make a cash contribution to the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION equal to $1,950,000 plus a sum equal 
to the MERGING ASSOCIATION’s aggregate nega- 
tive Net Worth as of the Effective Date (“Net Worth 
Contribution”) as follows: 

(1) As soon as possible following the Effective 
Date, but in any event within ten business days after 
the Effective Date, the CORPORATION shall trans- 
mit to the RESULTING ASSOCIATION a cash 
contribution equal to (A) $1,950,000, plus (B) a sum 
equal to the CORPORATION ’s estimate of the Net 
Worth Contribution, which estimate will be the sum 
of negative net worth shown on the most recent 
Financial Report for the MERGING ASSOCIATION 


filed with the Bank Board prior to the Effective Date, 
plus the estimated loss for the period following that 
of the last filed Financial Report, calculated by 
multiplying the average per diem loss, determined 
from the last Financial Report filed before the 
Effective Date, by the number of days between the 
last day covered by such Financial Report and the 
Effective Date (“Estimated Net Worth 
Contribution”). 

(2) The amount of the Estimated Net Worth 
Contribution shall be adjusted following the Audit 
required by § 3 of this Agreement and, to the extent 
that the MERGING ASSOCIATION’s audited Net 
Worth is greater than the Estimated Net Worth 
Contribution, the CORPORATION shall pay a cash 
sum equal to the amount of such difference to the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION and, to the extent the 
audited Net Worth is lesser than the Estimated Net 
Worth Contribution, the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION shall pay a cash sum equal to such difference to 
the CORPORATION. Any adjustment payment due 
from the CORPORATION to the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION, as a result of the audit, shall be 
remitted by the CORPORATION within ten business 
days of acceptance by the CORPORATION of the 
final audit report. Further, the CORPORATION will 
utilize its best efforts to obtain, review and accept the 
final audit report within 120 days of the Effective 
Date. Any adjustment payment due to the CORPO- 
RATION from the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, as 
a result of the audit, shall be remitted by the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION within ten business 
days after notification by the CORPORATION that 
such a payment is due. 

For purposes of periodic financial reports to the 
Bank Board, all cash contributions, other than Net 


99 


Worth Contributions, made under this § 4(a) shall be 
directly credited to the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION’s net worth; provided, however, that such 
accounting treatment of the contributions made under 
this § 4(a) is not hereby authorized for any filings or 
other reports the RESULTING ASSOCIATION may 
make to the Bank Board or others, including, but not 
limited to, financial statements required to be 
prepared in accordance with generally accepted 
accounting principles as in effect at the time of their 
submission. In the event that a court of competent 
jurisdiction directs the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
to divest itself of the assets and liabilities of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION, the cash contributions 
made pursuant to this § 4(a) shall be repaid to the 
as by the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 

(b) Indemnification Contributions. Within 30 
days after receipt of each of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION’s quarterly reports required pur- 
suant to § 8(a) of this Agreement, and within 90 days 
after receipt of the final accounting required pursuant 
to § 8(b) of this Agreement, the CORPORATION 
shall contribute to the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
any amounts set forth on such report that are owed 
to the RESULTING ASSOCIATION pursuant to 
$$ 5 and 6. 

(c) Assignment Contributions. In the event that 
the CORPORATION exercises its right to the 
assignment of a Merging Association Claim pursuant 
to § 6(c), the amount of any payment made by the 
CORPORATION in excess of the fair market value of 
such Claim shall be deemed to be a contribution by 
the CORPORATION. Fair market value may be 
determined by agreement of the CORPORATION 
and the RESULTING ASSOCIATION or by an 


100 


appraisal by an independent appraiser approved by 
the CORPORATION, the cost of which appraisal 
shall be paid by the CORPORATION. 

(d) Adjustments. Any contribution or payment by 
the CORPORATION or any accounting or report 
furnished by the RESULTING ASSOCIATION un- 
der this Agreement shall be subject to adjustment if 
it subsequently is determined that such contribution, 
payment, accounting, or report was based upon error 
or was not computed in accordance with the account- 
ing principles prescribed by this Agreement. 

$5 Indemnification for Undisclosed Liabilities, 
Claims Against the MERGING ASSOCIATION, and 
Litigation Challenging the Merger. 

(a) Indemnifications. To the extent not otherwise 
reimbursed or reimbursable by the CORPORATION 
or under any insurance policy, bond or other 
provision for funding, provided that the RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION complies with the covenants set 
forth in § $, the CORPORATION will indemnify the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION for, and pay as pro- 
vided in § 4(b), the amounts paid by the RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION to satisfy, settle, or compromise 
the following liabilities, Claims, and litigation and the 
reasonable costs and expenses of related litigation, 
including attorneys’ and accountants’ fees, travel 
expenses, court costs and related litigation expenses, 
and such other actual costs, including consideration 
by the CORPORATION of the payment of actual out 
of pocket expenses of employees of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION, as may be incurred in connection 
with such satisfaction, settlement or compromise: 

(1) Undisclosed Liabilities. Any liability of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION or any subsidiary corpo- 
ration that (A) was not disclosed in or reflected on 
the MERGING ASSOCIATION’s or a subsidiary 


101 


corporation’s books and records prior to the Effective 
Date and (B) under generally accepted accounting 
principles as of the Effective Date and consistently 
applied, is of the type that should have been so 
disclosed or reflected; provided that for the purposes 
of this § 5(a)(1), the term “liability” shall not be 
construed to include any inadequacy in or the lack of 
a valuation allowance or specific reserve; 

(2) Unreserved for Ciaims. Any Claim against 
the MERGING ASSOCIATION or any subsidiary 
corporation, or the RESULTING ASSOCIATION as 
the successor to the MERGING ASSOCIATION, 
based upon an action or failure to act of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION or any subsidiary corpo- 
ration prior to the Effective Date, whether filed 
before or after such date, to the extent that such 
Claim (A) was not adequately reserved for or 
provision made for its payment on the MERGING 
ASSOCIATION’s or a subsidiary corporation’s books 
and records prior to the Effective Date, and (B) is not 
fully reimbursed or reimbursable under any insurance 
policy, bond, or other provision for funding; and 

(3) Challenges to the Merger. Except for an 
action by the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, any 
litigation brought within a period of one year 
following the Effective Date to challenge or set aside 
the Merger or this Agreement in which an essential 
element is a claim that an action or failure to act by 
the Bank Board or the CORPORATION was not in 
accordance with the Bank Board’s or the CORPORA- 
TION’s enabling legislation and, in the event that a 
court of competent jurisdiction directs the RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION to divest itself of the assets or 
liabilities of the MERGING ASSOCIATION, the 
reasonable costs and expenses of such divestiture, 
provided that such damages are directly attributable 


102 


to such action or failure to act by the Bank Board or 
the CORPORATION. 

(b) Pursuit of Matters. The RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION shall use its best efforts to reduce or 
minimize the CORPORATION’s indemnity payments 
under this § 5 by the pursuit of counterclaims, offsets, 
insurance settlements, and Claims related to the 
liabilities indemnified for and shall promptly notify 
the CORPORATION of any evidence it discovers 
indicating a basis for such pursuit. The CORPORA- 
TION shall indemnify the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION for all reasonable costs and expenses related to 
such pursuit, including, but not limited to, attorneys’ 
and accountants’ fees, travel expenses, court costs 
and related litigation expenses, and such other actual 
costs as may be incurred by the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION in complying with this § 5(b), 
provided that the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
complies with the covenants set forth in § 9. The 
CORPORATION will consider payment of actual out 
of pocket expenses of employees of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION when incurred in the pursuit of 
counterclaims, offsets, insurance settlements and 
claims. All sums recovered from such related coun- 
terclaims, offsets, insurance settlements, and related 
Claims shall be paid over to the CORPORATION to 
the extent that the CORPORATION has made 
contributions pursuant to this Agreement. 

(c) Payments as Contributions. All payments 
made by the CORPORATION to the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION under this §5 shall be considered 
contributions under this Agreement. 

(d) Limitations. The CORPORATION’s aggre- 
gate contributions with respect to the liabilities of a 
subsidiary corporation under § 5(aX1) of this Agree- 
ment and Claims against subsidiary corporations 


103 


under § 5(a)(2) shall be limited to the amount of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION’s investment in that 
subsidiary corporation. 

§ 6 Merging Association Claims. 

(a) The RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall use 
its best efforts in pursuing and filing actions on all 
practicably recoverable Merging Association Claims 
which accrue to the RESULTING ASSOCIATION as 
successor in interest to the MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION, whether discovered before or after the 
Effective Date. 

‘b) Provided that the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION complies with the covenants set forth in § 9, 
the CORPORATION shall indemnify the RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION for the reasonable costs and 
expenses incurred by it in connection with the 
prosecution of Merging Association Claims, including, 
but not limited to, attorneys’ and accountants’ fees, 
travel expenses, court costs and related litigation 
expenses, and such other actual costs as may be 
incurred in connection with such prosecution, but only 
to the extent that such costs and expenses are not 
otherwise reimbursed or reimbursable. The CORPO- 
RATION will consider payment of reasonable actual 
out of pocket expenses of employees of the RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION when incurred in the prosecu- 
tion of Merging Association claims. Any amount paid 
by the CORPORATION pursuant to this subsection 
shall be considered to be a contribution by the COR- 
PORATION to the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 

(c) At any time during the term of this 
Agreement and until 90 days after the date on which 
this Agreement terminates, the CORPORATION 
shall have the right, upon its request, to obtain from 
the RESULTING ASSOCIATION an assignment of 


104 


any Merging Association Claim. If a Merging Associa- 
tion Claim had a value reflected on the books of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION immediately prior to the 
Effective Date, the CORPORATION shall pay for 
such assignment that value, as adjusted or deter- 
mined in accordance with the accounting principles as 
required by this Agreement, within 10 business days 
after the assignment is completed. In order to 
facilitate the assignment of a Merging Association 
Claim, the RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall, at 
the CORPORATION’s request: 

(1) Execute and deliver such instruments of 
assignment or transfer and make endorsements in 
such form and manner, and at such time, as the 
CORPORATION shall require; and 

(2) Execute and deliver all other instruments 
that the RESULTING ASSOCIATION possesses and 
the CORPORATION deems necessary or requisite to 
complete such assignments or transfers. 

(d) If the RESULTING ASSOCIATION has 
recovered on a Merging Association Claim, the Net 
Proceeds Received upon the Liquidation of such 
Merging Association Claim in excess of the value, if 
any, of such claim reflected on the books of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION prior to the Effective 
Date shall be paid over to the CORPORATION to 
the extent that it has made contributions pursuant to 
this Agreement. 

(e) If the CORPORATION has recovered on a 
Merging Association Claim that was assigned to the 
CORPORATION, the Net Proceeds Received upon 
the Liquidation of such Claim shall be retained by the 
CORPORATION to the extent of the amount, if any, 
paid by the CORPORATION for the assignment of 
such Claim and to the extent of any other contribu- 
tions made pursuant to this Agreement. 


105 


(f) Any recoveries on a Merging Association 
Claim exceeding the amount to be retained by or 
reimbursed to the CORPORATION pursuant to 
§ 6(d) or § 6(e) shall be paid to or retained by the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 

(g) The RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall 
promptly notify the CORPORATION of any evidence 
that it discovers indicating a basis for a Merging 
Association Claim. 

§ 7 Tax Benefits. 

Any federal or state income tax deduction, credit, 
loss carryovers, or other tax benefit, which now or 
hereafter inures to the benefit of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION or WINSTAR as a result of this 
Merger, may be retained by the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION or WINSTAR respectively. How- 
ever, should the RESULTING ASSOCIATION or 
WINSTAR receive no tax deductions, credits, loss 
carryovers, or other benefits or be liable for taxes as 
a result of the Merger, the CORPORATION has no 
obligation to indemnify or provide additional contribu- 
tions to either the RESULTING ASSOCIATION or 
WINSTAR. 

§8 Reports and Records. 

(a) Quarterly Reports. Within 45 days following 
the end of each Quarter in which the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION receives notice of any Claim or 
liability that may cause a payment by or to the 
CORPORATION pursuant to §§5 or 6, the RE- 
SULTING ASSOCIATION shall furnish a report to 
the CORPORATION, in a format approved by the 
CORPORATION and supported by such data as may 
be necessary to verify the reporting, that shall 
include: 

(1) Status reports on such Claims and 
liabilities; 


106 


(2) Payments made by and due from the 
CORPORATION; and 

(3) Payments made by and due from the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 

(b) Final Accounting. Within 90 days following 
the date this Agreement terminates, or such longer 
time as the CORPORATION may permit, the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall render to the 
CORPORATION a final accounting of the matters 
covered by this Agreement, which accounting may be 


prepared by the RESULTING ASSOCIATION’s. 


staff and shall include such information and be in such 
form as the CORPORATION shall request. 

(c) Access to Books and Records. The RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION and WINSTAR shall make 
available to the CORPORATION all books, papers, 
records, and information of any kind relating to any 
or all matters subject to the provisions of this 
Agreement, including any material or information in 
the possession of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, 
WINSTAR, or any of their affiliates or subsidiaries. 

(d) Examinations. The CORPORATION may 
audit or examine the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
and WINSTAR with respect to transactions affecting 
the implementation of this Agreement at any time or 
times chosen by the CORPORATION. Such audits 
shall be conducted by the CORPORATION at its own 
expense but with the assistance of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION’s and WINSTAR’s personnel, whose 
salaries and expenses shall be paid by the RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION and WINSTAR, respectively, 
without reimbursement from the CORPORATION. 

§9 Covenants of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION. If the RESULTING ASSOCIATION fails to 
comply with the following covenants concerning the 
pursuit of a Merging Association Claim, the defense 


107 


of a liability, Claim, or litigation described in § 5(a), 
or the pursuit of a counterclaim, offset, insurance 
settlement, or related Claim as required under § 5(b), 
all such items being referred to collectively in this 
section as “matters,” the CORPORATION may 
disallow a related filing of the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION for indemnification. 

(a) Management Standard. The RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION shall use its best efforts to minimize 
losses and maximize gains and recoveries in dealing 
with all matters that may be the basis for, or reduce 
the amount of any indemnification or contribution 
provided by the CORPORATION under this Agree- 
ment. The standard to be applied to determine 
whether the RESULTING ASSOCIATION uses its 
best efforts is the Management Standard defined in 
§ 1(g) above. 

(b) Conduct of Litigation. With respect to all 
matters described in the introductory paragraph to 
this § 9, the RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall: 

(1) Use its best efforts to keep the CORPORA- 
TION fully informed of their status; 

(2) Observe the right of the CORPORATION 
to monitor and direct their defense or prosecution, 
including the right to direct the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION to file a petition to remove an action 
to the appropriate court or to appeal any determina- 
tion of any court, provided that the CORPORATION 
shall bear the cost of removal and of monitoring and 
directing such defense or prosecution; 

(3) Authorize the CORPORATION, upon the 
CORPORATION’s election and at the CORPORA- 
TION’s expense and with its own attorneys, to defend 
against, or to prosecute in the name of the MERG- 
ING ASSOCIATION, the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION, or the CORPORATION, as the case may be, 


108 


any such matter and cooperate fully with the 
CORPORATION in such defense or prosecution, 
including, but not limited to, the furnishing to the 
CORPORATION of all applicable books, records, and 
other information in the possession or within the 
control of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, 
WINSTAR, or any of their affiliates or subsidiaries; 
(4) At the request of the CORPORATION, 
assign to the CORPORATION and subrogate the 
CORPORATION to any right, title, or interest of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION in any such matter, 
any defense related to such matter, and the proceeds 
recovered from such matter, and, in the event of such 
assignment, cooperate fully with the CORPORATION 
in the defense or prosecution of any such matter, 
including, but not limited to, the furnishing to the 
CORPORATION of all books, records, and other 
information in the possession or within the control of 
the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, WINSTAR, or 
any of their affiliates or subsidiaries; and 
(5) Obtain the CORPORATION ’s written con- 
sent before (A) prosecuting any such matter by court 
action, (B) entering into any binding commitment 
with respect to the satisfaction, settlement, or 
compromise of any such matter, or (C) making any 
payment in connection therewith, unless such commit- 
ment or payment is for an amount not in excess of 
twenty-five thousand dollars ($25,000) or is made 
pursuant to an adjudication by a court of competent 
jurisdiction which, with the permission of the COR- 
PORATION, the RESULTING ASSOCIATION has 
not appealed. 
$10 Accounting Principles. Except as otherwise 
provided, any computations made for the purposes of 
this Agreement shall be governed by generally 
accepted accounting principles as applied on a going 


109 


concern basis in the savings and loan industry, except 
that where such principles conflict with the terms of 
this Agreement, applicable regulations of the Bank 
Board or the CORPORATION, or any resolution or 
action of the Bank Board approving or adopted 
concurrently with this Agreement, then this Agree- 
ment, such regulations, or such resolution or action 
shall govern. In the case of any ambiguity in the 
interpretation or construction of any provision of this 
Agreement, such ambiguity shall be resolved in a 
manner consistent with such regulations and the 
Bank Board’s resolution or action. If there is a 
conflict between such regulations and the Bank 
Board’s resolution or action, the Bank Board’s 
resolution or action shall govern. For the purposes of 
this section, the governing regulations and the 
accounting principles shall be those in effect on the 
Effective Date or as subsequently clarified, inter- 
preted, or amended by the Bank Board or the 
Financial Accounting Standards Board (“FASB”), 
respectively, or any successor organization to either. 
If there is a conflict between what is required by the 
FASB and what is required by the Bank Board, the 
Bank Board’s interpretation and requirements shall 
govern. 

$11 Representations and Warranties. All repre- 
sentations and warranties made to WINSTAR or the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION in the Merger Agree- 
ment or otherwise made to WINSTAR or the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION in connection with the 
Merger shall also be deemed to have been made to, 
and shall inure to the benefit of, the CORPORA- 
TION. The CORPORATION shall not, however, have 
the right to terminate or rescind this Agreement 
because of any breach of any such warranty or 
representation without the written consent of the 


110 


RESULTING ASSOCIATION and shall not assert 
against the RESULTING ASSOCIATION any Claim 
or right arising from the breach of such representa- 
tions and warranties by the MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION or its officers, directors, or employees.

§ 13 Termination of Agreement. 

(a) Except as otherwise specifically provided in 
this Agreement, this Agreement shall terminate two 
years following the Effective Date or on such other 
date to which the parties or their successors agree in 
writing. However, if a receiver is appointed for the 
purpose of liquidating the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION, or the RESULTING ASSOCIATION ceases 


111 


to be an association the accounts of which are insured 
by the CORPORATION, this Agreement and all of 
the CORPORATION’s obligations under it shall 
terminate upon the effective date of such occurrence. 
(b) Notwithstanding termination of this Agree- 
ment as described in § 13(a), provided that the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION continues to observe 
after the termination date all conditions, covenants, 
agreements of cooperation, and agreements for the 
use of best efforts set forth in this Agreement, the 
CORPORATION shall indemnify the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION for (1) any loss arising from lia- 
bilities and Claims described in § 5(a), including the 
reasonable costs and expenses of related litigation, (2) 
the reasonable costs and expenses of pursuing the 
matters described in § 5(b), and (3) the reasonable 
costs and expenses incurred by the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION in the prosecution of any Merging 
Association Claim pursuant to § 6, provided that any 
such liability, Claim, matter, or Merging Association 
Claim is in litigation on the date that this Agreement 
terminates, and provided further that ay recovery 
by the RESULTING ASSOCIATION on any matter 
described in § 5(b) or any Merging Association Claim 
shall be paid over to the CORPORATION, subject to 
the limitations of § 5(b) and § 6(e). 
$14 Rights and Forbearance. The rights, powers, 
and remedies given to the parties by this Agreement 
shall be in addition to all rights, powers, and remedies 
given by any applicable statute or rule of law. Any 
forbearance, failure, or delay by any party in 
exercising or partially exercising any such right, 
power, or remedy shall not preclude its further 
exercise. 
§ 15 Governing Law. This Agreement and the 
parties’ rights and obligations under it shall be 


112 


governed by the law of the State of Minnesota to the 
extent that Federal law does not control. Nothing in 
this Agreement shall require any unlawful action or 
inaction by either party. 

§$ 16 Entire Agreement, Severability. 

(a) This Agreement, together with any inter- 
pretation or understanding agreed to in writing by 
the parties, constitutes the entire agreement between 
the parties and supersedes all prior agreements and 
understandings of the parties in connection with it, 
excepting only the Merger Agreement and any 
resolutions or letters issued contemporaneously with 
this Agreement by the Bank Board or the CORPO- 
RATION, provided, however, that in the event of any 
conflict, variance, or inconsistency between this 
Agreement and the Merger Agreement the provisions 
of this Agreement shall govern and be binding on all 
parties insofar as the rights, privileges, duties, 
obligations, and liabilities of the CORPORATION are 
concerned. 

(b) If any provision of this Agreement is invalid 
or unenforceable, all of the remaining provisions of 
this Agreement shall nevetheless remain in full force 
and effect and shall be binding upon the CORPORA- 
TION, the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, and 
WINSTAR.

(a) This Agreement may be executed in any

execute this Agreement by signing any such 
counterpart.

ment shall be binding unless executed in writing by

(c) Section headings are not to be considered part

113 


reference, and shall not affect the meaning or

provisions. —

provisions of this Agreement shall be binding upon 
and inure to the benefit of the parties and their 
respective transferees, succesors, and assigns, but 
this Agreement may not be assigned to any party nor 
may any rights under it be transferred to or vested in 
any other party, through merger, consolidation, or 
otherwise, without the prior written consent of the 
CORPORATION. 

$19 Sole Benefit. It is the intention of the parties 
that this Agreement, the assumption of obligations 
and statements of responsibilities under it, and all of 
its conditions and provisions are for the sole benefit 
of the CORPORATION and the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION and for the benefit of no other person or 
entity. Nothing expressed or referred to in this 
Agreement is intended or shall be construed to give 
any person other than the CORPORATION or the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION any legal or equitable 
right, remedy, or claim under, or in respect to, this 
Agreement or any of its provisions. 

§ 20 Continuing Cooperation. It is the purpose of 
this Agreement to provide a means by which the 
failure of the MERGING ASSOCIATION may be 
prevented, the savers and other creditors of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION may be protected against 
losses, the thrift and economical home financing needs 
of the community in which the MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION’s home office is located may be served, the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION and WINSTAR may 
receive the benefits and assume the risks contracted 
for, and expenses to the CORPORATION may be 
reduced. WINSTAR, the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION, and the CORPORATION therefore agree that 


114 


they shall in good faith, and using their best efforts, 
cooperate with one another to carry out the purposes 
of this Agreement as described in this section.

COMBINATION AGREEMENT AND 
PLAN OF MERGER 


THIS COMBINATION AGREEMENT AND PLAN 
OF MERGER is the contract of Windom Federal 
Savings and Loan Association, Windom, Minnesota, a 
federal mutual savings and loan association (“WIN- 
DOM” or “MERGING ASSOCIATION”) and United 
Federal Savings Bank, Windom, Minnesota, a federal 
stock savings bank (“SAVINGS BANK” or “RE- 
SULTING ASSOCIATION”). 


RECITALS 


1. The accounts of WINDOM and SAVINGS BANK 
are insured by the Federal Savings and Loan 
Insurance Corporation. 

2. This merger, the supervisory conversion of 
WINDOM to the stock form of ownership, and the 
resulting acquisition of control of WINDOM are 
authorized by the provisions of the Home Owners 
Loan Act of 1933, as amended (“HOLA”), the National 
Housing Act, as amended, (the Act), and the rules and 
regulations promulgated under and pursuant to the 
HOLA and the Act by the Federal Home Loan Bank 
Board (“BANK BOARD”). 

3. It is deemed to be in the best interests of the 
members of WINDOM that WINDOM and SAVINGS 
BANK effect a merger upon the terms and conditions 
stated in this Agreement (the transaction provided for 
in this agreement being referred to as the “Merger”). 

4. It is believed that this merger will enable the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION to maintain and im- 
prove WINDOM’s savings, mortgage and other serv- 
ices, and improve the financial condition of WINDOM. 


116 


5. The following agreement is entered into in 
consideration of the mutual promises contained in this 
Agreement. 


AGREEMENT 


$1 Merger. WINDOM shall merge with and into 
SAVINGS BANK. 

§2 RESULTING ASSOCIATION. The RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION shall be United Federal Savings 
Bank, Windom, Minnesota. 

§$ 3 Name of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. The 
name of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall be 
United Federal Savings Bank. 

$4 Home Office of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION. The home office of the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION shail be located at 930 Fourth Avenue, 
Windom, Minnesota. 

$5 Charter and Bylaws of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION. After the merger the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION shall operate under the present 
charter and bylaws of SAVINGS BANK which are 
attached as Exhibit Al and A2 respectively and 
constitute part of this Merger Agreement. 

§ 6 Directors of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 
The number of directors of the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION shall be six (6). The names and residence 
addresses of those persons who shall serve as directors 
of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION are listed in the 
attached Exhibit B, which constitutes a part of this 
Merger Agreement. 

§ 7 Officers. The officers of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION shall be the officers of SAVINGS 
BANK. 


§ 8 Branch Offices of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 


TION. After the merger the branch offices of the 


117 


RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall be the present 
branch offices of WINDOM. The addresses of the 
offices that will be branch offices of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION are listed on the attached Exhibit C, 
which constitutes a part of the Merger Agreement. 
§ 9 Accounts. Upon the effective date of the merger, 
each accountholder of the MERGING ASSOCIATION 
shall receive, without payment, a withdrawable ac- 
count or accounts in the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION equal in withdrawal value to the account or 
accounts held in the MERGING ASSOCIATION on 
such date, featuring the same rate, maturity, and other 
terms. Accounts of WINDOM shall continue as 
accounts of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION but 
accountholders of WINDOM shall not, due to having 
accounts in WINDOM, have any equity interest in the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION. Accounts of the RE- 
SULTING ASSOCIATION shall be issued on the 
same bases as accounts issued presently by WINDOM. 
§ 10 Transfer of Assets upon Merger. Upon the 
effective date of the merger, all of the assets and 
property of every kind or character, real, personal, and 
mixed, tangible and intangible, choses in action, rights, 
and credits then owned by the MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION, or that would inure to it at any time thereafter, 
shall immediately by operation of law and without any 
conveyance or transfer and without further act or deed 
by vested in and become the property of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION, which shall have, hold, 
and enjoy the same in its own right as fully and to the 
same.extent as the same were possessed, held, and 
enjoyed by the MERGING ASSOCIATION prior to 
the merger. The RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall 
be deemed to be and shall be a continuation of the 
entity and identity of WINDOM. All of the rights and 
obligations of the MERGING ASSOCIATION shall 


118 


remain unimpaired, and the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION shall, on the effective date of the merger, 
succeed to all of such rights and obligations, and duties 
connected with them. 

§ 11 Effective Date of Merger. This Merger Agree- 
ment is effective only when approved by the BANK 
BOARD. The effective date of the merger shall be the 
date on which the merger is approved by the BANK 
BOARD unless another date is specified in such 
approval. 

§ 12 Conduct in Ordinary Course of Business. The 
MERGING ASSOCIATION warrants and represents 
that from the date of this Agreement to the effective 
date of the merger, the MERGING ASSOCIATION 
shall have conducted its business in its ordinary and 
usual course, and shall not have, without the consent 
of the Principal Supervisory Agent for its district, or 
his designee, 

(a) entered into any material transaction other 
than in the ordinary and usual course of business, 
except transactions in connection with this Agreement 
and the transactions expressly permitted by it; 

(b) borrowed any money other than advances from 
a Federal Home Loan Bank; 

(c) encumbered any of its property or other assets 
except as security for advances from a Federal Home 
Loan Bank; 

(d) disposed of, transferred, or contracted to 
dispose of or transfer any property, real or otherwise, 
or other assets, except, in each case, in the ordinary 
and usual course of business; 

(e) entered into any contract or agreement for the 
purchase, sale or lease of goods, materials, equipment, 
supplies, capital assets, real estate or any interest in 
real estate except, in each case, in the ordinary and 
usual course of its business; 


119 


(f) incurred any material obligation or liability or 
had any contingent liability arise except in the 
ordinary and usual course of business; 

(g) except for individual merit increases in 
accordance with standard personnel practices adopted 
prior to the date of this Agreement, and normal 
periodic employee salary and wage increases scheduled 
prior to the date of this Agreement, made or agreed to 
make any increase in the rate of compensation or 
remuneration to any of its directors, officers, 
employees, agents, or other representatives; 

(h) employed any person to serve as an officer, 
director, or senior manager who is not employed as of 
the date of this Agreement; employed any person 
pursuant to an agreement which is not terminable at 
the will of the MERGING ASSOCIATION; or entered 
into any of the following types of contracts with any 
employee, director, or officer, or amended any such 
contract that is presently binding on the MERGING 
ASSOCIATION: collective bargaining agreements, 
pension or profit sharing, bonus, severance pay, 
retirement, fringe benefit or other employee benefit 
plans, or other employment contracts; 

(i) amended or permitted the amendment of its 
charter or bylaws; 

(j) made any material change in accounting 
method; or 

(k) engaged in transactions described in 
$§ 563.17-4 and 563.17-5 of the Rules and Regulations 
for the Federal Savings and Loan Insurance 
Corporation. 

$13 Further Actions. All of the transactions 
contemplated by this Agreement have been or will be 
authorized by all necessary corporate actions by 
WINDOM, and SAVINGS BANK. WINDOM, and 
SAVINGS BANK, by their proper officers, shall 


120 


execute and deliver all instruments, certificates, and 
uther documents as may be necessary or incidental to 
the performance of this Agreement. 

§ 14 Counterparts, Modifications, Headings. 

(a) This Agreement may be executed in any 
number of counterparts, each of which shall constitute 
one and the same instrument, and any party may 
execute this Agreement by signing any such 
counterpart. 

(b) No amendment or modification of this Agree- 
ment shall be binding unless executed in writing by 
the parties or their successors. 

(c) Section headings are not to be considered part 
of this Agreement, are solely for convenience of 
reference, and shall not affect the meaning or 
interpretation of this Agreement or any of its 
provisions. 

§ 15 Entire Agreement, Severability. 

(a) This Agreement, together with any interpreta- 
tion or understanding agreed to in writing by the 
parties, constitutes the entire agreement between the 
parties and supersedes all prior agreements and 
understanding of the parties in connection with it. 

(b) If any provision of this Agreement is invalid or 
unenforceable, then to the extent possible, all of the 
remaining provisions of the Agreement shall remain in 
full force and effect and shall be binding upon the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION and the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION. 

§ 16 Governing Law. This Agreement and the rights 
and obligations under this Agreement shall be gov- 
erned by the law of the State of Minnesota. to the 
extent that Federal law does not control. Nothing in 
this Agreement shall require any unlawful action or 
inaction by either party. 

§$ 17 Sole Benefit. Nothing expressed or referred to 
in this Agreement is intended or shall be construed to 


121 


give any person other than the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION or the MERGING ASSOCIATION any 
legal or equitable right, remedy, or claim under, or in 
respect to, this Agreement or any of its provisions, it 
being the intention of the parties that this Agreement, 
the assumption of obligations and statements of 
responsibilities under it, and all of its conditions and 
provisions are for the sole benefit of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION and the MERGING ASSOCIATION 
and for the benefit of no other person. 

§ 18 Acknowledgement. The MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION and SAVINGS BANK, by the execution of this 
Agreement, acknowledge and affirm that their boards 
of directors, by at least a two-thirds vote of each 
board, have approved this Agreement and the merger, 
authorized the execution of this Agreement, em- 
powered the signatories to execute this Agreement, 
and authorized the filing of this Agreement with the 
BANK BOARD. 
$ 19 Assistance Agreement. This Merger Agreement 
is conditioned upon the execution of an Assistance 
Agreement between the FEDERAL SAVINGS AND

company the Winstar Corporation, Roseville, 
Minne. ota.

Dear Sirs: 


In connection with the merger of Windom Federal 
Savings and Loan Association, Windom, Minnesota 
(“Merging Association”), into United Federal Savings 
Bank (“Savings Bank”), this is to confirm the 
understanding that: 


1. For purposes of reporting to the Board, Savings 
Bank may show, as a direct credit to its net 
worth, the cash contribution made by the FSLIC 
pursuant to Section 4(a)(1)(A) of the Assistance 
Agreement to be entered into among Winstar 
Corporation, Savings Bank, and the Federal 
Savings and Loan Insurance Corporation 
(“FSLIC”), 


2. For purposes of reporting to the Board, the 
value of any intangible assets resulting from 
accounting for the merger in accordance with the 
purchase method may be amortized by S#vings 
Bank over a period not to exceed 35 years by 
the straight-line method; 


3. For purposes of reporting to the Board, any 
discount arising from the adjustment of Savings 
Bank’s mortgage loan portfolio to its fair value 
shall be credited to income by the level yield 
method assuming contractually required pay- 
ments will be received for 15 years, and the 


SO a tee 


124 


remaining balance will be repaid at the end of 
the 15th year or by other methods of applying 
the level yield method as described in R 
memoranda or other accounting documents ac- 
ceptable under generally accepted accounting 
principles; and 


4. The limitation of 50%, on Savings Bank’s net 
income available for payment of dividends, as set 
forth in the Dividend Limitation Stipulation and 
Bank Board Resolution No. 84-363, is waived for 
its fiscal years beginning on July 1, 1985, July 1, 
1986, and July 1, 1987. During Savings Bank’s 
fiscal year beginning on July 1, 1985, it may pay 
as dividends up to 60% of its net income. During 
Savings Bank’s fiscal year beginning on July 1, 
1986, it may pay as dividends up to 70% of its 
net income. During Savings Bank’s fiscal year 
beginning on July 1, 1987, it may pay as 
dividends up to 60% of its net income. But in no 
event may Savings Bank by the payment of 
dividends reduce its net worth below the level 
required by § 563.13 of the Insurance Regula- 
tions for institutions insured for twenty years or 
longer. 


The accounting forbearances in the preceding 
paragraphs 1 through 3 are not acceptable in any 
financial statements which are required to be pre- 
pared in accordance with generally accepted account- 
ing principles. 


The forbearances or waivers extended by this 
letter do not relieve Savings Bank of its continuing 
obligations to maintain records of its reserve and net 
worth condition and to report its financial condition in 
accordance with applicable regulatory requirements. 


124a 


This letter does not and shall not be construed to 
constitute forbearance or waiver by the Board or the 
FSLIC with respect to any regulatory or other 
requirements other than those encompassed within 
the preceding paragraphs 1 through 4. Other than 
actions to enforce the regulatory requirements 
waived in accordance with paragraphs 1 through 4 
and the statutory provisions authorizing imposition of 
the waived requirements, insofar as such require- 
ments are waived, the Board and the FSLIC 
expressly reserve all of their statutory rights and 
powers with respect to Savings Bank, including, 
without limitation, those under Section 5 of the Home 
Owners’ Loan Act of 1933, as amended, and Sections 


406 and 407 of the National Housing Act, as 
Amended.

AFFIDAVIT OF E. TED YOCH 
IN SUPPORT OF PLAINTIFF’S MOTION 
FOR SUMMARY JUDGMENT AS TO LIABILITY 


Before me, a notary public for the State of 
Minnesota, on this date personally appeared E. Ted 
Yoch, being by me first duly sworn, upon his oath 
deposes and says: 

1. | am Chief Executive Officer and Chairman of 
the Board of Directors of Winstar Corporation 
(“Winstar”). I am also Chairman of the Board of 
Directors of United Federal Savings Bank (“United”). 

2.1 submit this affidavit in support of the 
Plaintiffs’ Motion for Summary Judgment on the 
issues of defendant’s liability. I am familiar with the 
matters set forth herein from my own personal 
knowledge or from a review of materials in the files 
and possession of Winstar or United. 

3. Winstar was formed as a Minnesota corporation 
in 1984 for the purpose of acquiring control of 


126 


Windom Federal Savings and Loan Association 


(“Windom”), an insolvent institution at the time. 
United is a federal stock savings bank chartered in 
1984, all of the stock of which is owned by Winstar. 

4. In 1983, the organizers of Winstar, including 
myself, began negotiating with the Federal Home 
Loan Bank Board (“FHLBB”) and the Federal 
Savings and Loan Insurance Corporation (“FSLIC”) 
regarding the proposed acquisition of Windom. Win- 
dom was on the brink of financial ruin in 1983. 
Exhibit A is a true copy of a Consent Resolution 
adopted by Windom’s Board of Directors on March 
11, 1983, which was obtained from United’s files. 

5. Exhibit B is a true copy of a report to Windom’s 
Board of Director from the accounting firm of 
McGladrey Hendrickson & Co. dated September 16, 
1983, which was obtained from United’s files. 

6. Throughout the entire bidding and negotiating 
process between Winstar and FHLBB and FSLIC, it 
was understood by all parties that the entity that 
acquired Windom would be able to record as 
supervisory goodwill the amount by which the market 
value of Windom’s liabilities exceeded the market 
value of its assets, and to amortize that goodwill over 
a long period of time, such as 30 to 40 years. 

7. Ultimately, FHLBB and FSLIC agreed that 
FSLIC would provide to United a cash contribution 
of $1,950,000.00, plus a sum equal to Windom’s 
aggregate negative net worth (the amount by which 
Windom’s liabilities exceeded its assets, measured at 


book value). In return, Winstar agreed to form — 


United and to invest $2 million in cash in United 
through the purchase of all of United’s common stock. 

8. After an agreement in principle was reached, the 
organizers of Winstar incorporated Winstar under the 
laws of the State of Minnesota on March 1, 1984. 


127 


Exhibit C is a true copy of a certificate from the 
State of Minnesota Depariment of State certifying 
that Winstar is a legally organized corporation under 
the laws of Minnesota. 

9. In accordance with its agreement with FSLIC 
and FHLBB, Winstar formed United. Exhibit D is a 
true copy of the Plan of Organization for United. 

10. United entered into a Combination Agreement 
and Plan of Merger with Windom setting out the 
terms of the acquisition. Exhibit E is a true copy of 
the Combination Agreement and Plan of Merger. 

11. At the time of the merger between United and 
Windom, FSLIC entered into an Assistance Agree- 
ment with Winstar and United. Exhibit F is a true 
copy of the Assistance Agreement. 

12. FHLBB passed a Resolution approving the 
supervisory merger. Exhibit G is a true copy of 
FHLBB Resolution No. 84-363 approving the merger. 

13. FHLBB delegated certain authority pertaining 
to the Assistance Agreement in Resolution No. 
84-364, a true copy of which is attached as Exhibit H. 
14. FHLBB issued Resolution No. 84-365, further 
indicating its approval of the merger. A true copy of 
this Resolution is attached as Exhibit I. 

15. In a separate forbearance letter, FHLBB set 
forth its commitment regarding the regulatory ac- 
counting treatment pertaining to the merger. Exhibit 
J is a true copy of the forbearance letter. 

16. In accordance with the agreements and docu- 
ments listed above, the supervisory merger between 
United *»d Windom was consummated on July 12, 
1984. " wstar infused $2 million into United, receiv- 
ing al. of the common stock of United. United merged 
with Windom, and the resulting institution was 
known as United. 

17. Under the Assistance Agreement FSLIC con- 
tributed $1,950,000.00 in cash to United. FSLIC 


128 


ultimately contributed $3,694,499.00 as its contribu- 
tion based on Windom’s aggregate negative net worth 
under § 4 of the Assistance Agreement. 

18. Exhibit K is a true copy of a letter and Notes 
to Consolidated Statement of Financial Condition 
dated September 7, 1984 that was provided to 
United’s Board of Directurs by the accounting firm of 
McGladrey, Hendrickson & Pullen. 

19. In reliance upon FSLIC’s and FHLBB’s ex- 
press agreements concerning the use of purchase 
method accounting, the assets and liabilities of 
Windom were adjusted to fair market value at the 
time of the supervisory merger, and the resulting 
excess of liabilities over assets was recorded on 
United’s books as supervisory goodwill to be included 
in capital and amortized on a straight-line basis over 
a period of 35 years. The amount of supervisory 
goodwill ultimately recorded by United as a result of 
the acquisition of Windom was $10,654,419.00. United 
also included the FSLIC cash contribution of 
$1,950,000.00 as capital. In financial reports filed with 
FSLIC and FHLBB since the supervisory merger, 
United has consistently booked supervisory goodwill 
as an asset included in capital for regulatory 
purposes, in accordance with, and in reliance upon, 
FSLIC’s and FHLBB’s express agreements. United 
has also consistently amortized the supervisory 
goodwill over a period of 35 years on a straight-line 
basis, in accordance with the agreements. Prior to the 
enactment of the Financial Institution Reform, Re- 
covery and Enforcement Act of 1989 (“FIRREA”), 
neither FSLIC nor FHLBB ever objected to United’s 
treatment of supervisory goodwill. 

20. After the merger with Windom, United’s 
tangible net worth (i.e., exclusive of supervisory 
goodwill), was approximately negative $6.7 million. 


129 


Clearly, therefore, the purchase method of accounting 
for the proposed merger was integral to Winstar and 
United. Had Winstar and United not been permitted 
to record on United’s books supervisory goodwill in 
the amount by which Windom’s liabilities exceeded 
the fair market value of its assets, the acquisition of 
Windom on the remaining financial terms agreed 
upon would have rendered United insolvent and 
subject to immediate liquidation by FSLIC and 
FHLBB. Winstar and United would not have entered 
into such a transaction. Nor would they have made a 
proposal or entered into a transaction for the 
acquisition of Windom that did not include a lengthy 
amortization period in which to return Windom to 
profitability and to gradually replace supervisory 
goodwill with tangible assets. Thus, Winstar’s willing- 
ness to form United, United’s willingness to acquire 
Windom on the financial terms proposed, and 
Winstar’s willingness to invest $2 million in United, 
were all absolutely conditioned on FHLBB’s and 
FSLIC’s agreement to permit United to report 
supervisory goodwill as an asset included in capital 
for regulatory purposes and to amortize that super- 
visory goodwill over a 35-year period. Absent the 
specified accounting treatment, Winstar and United 
either would have insisted upon receiving more than 
$5,644,500.00 in tangible financial assistance from 
FSLIC or would have withdrawn the offer. 

21. In order to maintain United’s tangible capital at 
3 percent of liabilities, the regulatory minimum 
prevailing at the time of the supervisory merger, 
FSLIC would have had to contribute to United 
approximately $15.8 million in cash or other tangible 
financial assistance. 

22. In 1988, Winstar purchased additional common 
stock from United for an additional $800,000.00. This 


130 


investment was made solely for the purpose of 
boosting United’s capital, as Winstar already held 100 
percent of United’s common stock. Winstar would not 
have made this investment in the absence of 
FHLBB’s and FSLIC’s agreement to the specified 
accounting treatment for goodwill, or if Winstar or 
United had foreseen that the government would 
renege on that agreement. 

23. Winstar and United have complied in good faith 
with all the terms and conditions of the agreements 
relating to the supervisory merger and have other- 
wise fully performed their undertakings in connection 
therewith. 

24. As of June 30, 1989, the end of the last 
reporting period before the enactment of FIRREA, 
United reported supervisory goodwill in the amount 
of approximately $9.1 million. Thus, United had 
written off over $1.5 million of the goodwill it had 
acquired in its 1984 merger with Windom. Prior to 
FIRREA, all of the $9.1 million of supervisory 
goodwill was includable in United’s regulatory capitai. 
FIRREA excludes all of this goodwill for purposes of 
satisfying United’s tangible capital requirement. FIR- 
REA and the capital regulation promulgated by the 
Office of Thrift Supervision exclude all but $2.7 
million of this goodwill for purposes of satisfying 
United’s leverage limit and risk-based capital require- 
ment. These exclusions violate Winstar’s and United’s 
contract with FHLBB and FSLIC. 

25. As of September 30, 1989, it would have cost 
the government less to liquidate United than it would 
have cost FHLBB and/or FSLIC to liquidate Windom 
before or at the time of Windom’s merger into 
United. 

I hereby certify that the foregoing statements are 
true and correct, to the best of my knowledge and 
belief.
