FEDERAL HOME LOAN BANK BOARD 
OFFICE OF EXAMINATIONS AND 
SUPERVISION 


MEMORANDUM 


SP-37a 
March 7, 1986 


TO: Principal Supervisory Agents 
FROM: Francis M. Passarealli 


Requests for 
Forbearances and 
Exceptions to 
Filing Require- 
ments Regarding 
Supervisory 
Institutions 


Editor’s Note: This memorandum constitutes a revi- 
sion of SP-37 made in order to accom- 
modate amendments to the net worth 
regulation and to make the language 
contained herein consistent with the 
direct investment regulation and R 
Memorandum 34 (“Level Yield Method 
of Amortization”). SP-37 is hereby 
rendered obsolete and is rescinded. 


The purpose of the instructions in the attached 
material is to expedite the processing of supervisory 
or assisted mergers and acquisitions and to diminish 
the inordinate amount of staff time presently necessi- 
tated to negotiate the terms of forbearance letters 
with potential acquirers of institutions. Washington 


181 


Staff will use these guidelines and the standard 
forbearance language contained herein. In the interest 
of consistency and fairness, Supervisory Agents should 
also use these guidelines where they have a general 
authorization or delegation to grant forbearance. 
Potential acquirers should be encouraged to limit 
forbearance requests to the standardized statements 
provided in Section I of the memo. They should be 
advised that requests for forbearances beyond these 
outlined in Section I will, at a minimum, substantially 
increase the processing time. Requests for for- 
bearances outlined in Section II will likely be rejected 
unless they are shown to be key factors in helping to 
solve exceptionally difficult supervisory cases. For- 
bearances outlined in Section III will not be granted. 


The memorandum is grouped into self-explanatory 
subjects. The first three deal with forbearances in 
connection with supervisory mergers, including mer- 
gers into a subsidiary of a holding company, and 
acquisitions of a problem institution either by a 
holding company or individual(s) without a merger. 
They are: 


I. Stanard forbearances that will be granted. 
II. Forbearance that may be granted on a case-by- 
case basis if circumstances so justify. 
III. Forbearances that will not be granted. 


The last two subjects cover miscellaneous require- 
ments. They are: 


IV. Requests to provide reduced information. 


V. Required concluding paragraphs to all for- 
bearance letters. 


It should be especially stressed that requests for 
forbearances, beyond those outlined in Section I, must 


182 


be convincingly justified. Blanket requests for for- 
bearances (i.e., all available forbearances) are not 
acceptable. The basic purpose in granting forbearances 
is to give realistic incentives to potential acquires of 
problem institutions. The incentives, however, must be 
limited to those actually necessary for an acquirer to 
complete a supervisory merger or acquisition. 


This SP memorandum does not cover forbearances 
appropriate for acquisition of a problem institution by 
a holding company that is an insured institution. 


Francis M. Passarelli 
Acting Director 


Attachment 
ce: Professional Staff—Examinations and Supervision 


Office of the FSLIC 
Office of the General Counsel 


183 


I. Standard forbearances that will be granted in 
connection with supervisory mergers, including 
mergers with a subsidiary of a holding company, 
and acquisitions of a problem institution by a 
holding company or individual(s) without a merger: 


Net Worth Requirements 
Supervisory Mergers 

A-1. Section 563.13 (Regulatory Net Worth Re- 
quirement) of the Rules and Regulations for 
Insurance of Accounts provides in subsection 
(b)(3)(iii) for the possible exclusion or modera- 
tion of the base, amortization, contingency and 
growth factors of (disappearing institution) by 
(resulting institution) in computing the net 
worth requirements of this section provided 
the merger was instituted for supervisory 
reasons. Therefore, the Federal Savings and 
Loan Insurance Corporation will forbear, for a 
period not to exceed (five) years following 
consummation of the merger, from exercising 
its authority, under Section 563.13 of the Rules 
and Regulations for Insurance of Accounts, for 
any failure of (resulting institution), to meet 
the net worth requirements of Section 563.13 
arising solely from (1)(a) any increase in the 
contingency factor attributable to the assets of 
(disappearing institution(s)) existing at the 
Effective Date(s), and, (b) any increase in the 
net growth of (resulting institution) as of the 
Effective Date(s) by reason of (resulting 
institution’s) assumption of (disappearing insti- 
tution(s)) liabilities; or (2) (resulting institu- 
lion’s) assumption of the net worth defi- 
ciency(ies) of disappearing institution(s) as of 
the Effective Date(s). 


184 


Acquisitions by a Holding Company or Individual(s) 


A-2. Section 563.13 (Regulatory Net Worth Re- 
quirement) of the Rules and Regulations for 
Insurance of Accounts provides no exceptions 
in the case of acquisitions which permit the 
exclusion of scheduled items of an institution in 
computing the net worth requirements of this 
section. However, in view of the fact that the 
acquisition was instituted for supervisory rea- 
sons, the Federal Savings and Loan Insurance 
Corporation will forbear, for a period not to 
exceed (five) years following consummation of 
the acquisition, from exercising its authority 
under Section 563.13 of the Rules and Regula- 
tions for Insurance of Accounts, for any failure 
of (acquired institution) to meet the net worth 
requirements of Section 563.13 arising solely 
from an increase in the contingency factor 
attributable to (acquired institution) at the 
date of acquisition. 


Exclusion of Scheduled Items 
Supervisory Mergers 


B. The assets acquired by (resulting institution) 
from (disappearing institution(s)) will be 
deemed to have been acquired in a merger 
instituted for supervisory reasons. Therefore, 
pursuant to any section of the regulations 
administered by the Federal Home Loan Bank 
Board where scheduled items are referred to 
as “assets acquired in a merger instituted for 
supervisory reason”, scheduled items attribu- 
table to (disappearing institution(s)) shall be 
excluded from the computation of scheduled 


185 


items in determining whether (resulting insti- 
tution) meets the requirements of such sec- 
tions following the effective merger date(s). 


II. Forbearances that may be granted in connection 
with supervisory mergers, including mergers into a 
subsidiary of a holding company, and acquisitions 
of a problem institution, either by a_ holding 
company or individual(s), without a merger, if 
circumstances so justify: 


Regulations Which Reference Reserve and Net Worth 
Requirements 
Supervisory Mergers 


A. For a period of five years following the Effective 
Date(s), the Board will waive or forbear from 
enforcement of the reserve or net worth require- 
ments as stated in Insurance Regulation 563.13 
provided that the Board may lawfully waive or 
forbear with respect to such requirements, and 
provided further that failure to meet the require- 
ments is due to (1)(a) operating losses on acquired 
assets; (b) capital losses sustained by (resulting 
institution) upon disposition of acquired assets; (c) 
acquired assets that are or become scheduled 
items; and (d) the assumption of (disappearing 
institution’s) _ liabilities, including averaged lia- 
bilities; or (2) (resulting institution’s) assumption of 
(disappearing institution(s’)) net worth defi- 
ciency(ies) as of the Effective Date(s). 


This forbearance generally will be granted where a 
request demonstrates that without such a for- 
bearance, the resulting institution in a 


186 


supervisory merger would be unable to meet, or 
only marginally meet, the scheduled items and net 
worth limitations and requirements of the Board’s 
Regulations. 


Liquidity Deficiencies 
Supervisory Mergers 
B-1. For a period of one year following the 
Effective Date(s), in determining whether a 
penalty shall be assessed against or paid by 
(resulting institution) pursuant to Section 
523.12 (Deficiencies and Penalties) of the 
Rules and Regulations for the Federal Home 
Loar Bank System (“Bank Regulations”), the 
liquidity requirements of Section 523.11 (Liq- 
uidity Requirements) of the Bank Regulations 
as applied to (resulting institution) shall be 
reduced by (1) the amount of (disappearing 
institution(s’) liquidity deficiencies existing at 
the Effective Date(s) and (2) any aggregate 
net withdrawals (excess of withdrawals over 
cash savings received) from (disappearing 
institution(s’)) offices after the Effective 
Date(s). 


Acquisitions by a Holding Company or Individual(s) 


B-2. For a period of one year following the 
Effective Date, in determining whether a 
penalty shall be assessed against or paid by 
(institution) pursuant to Section 523.12 (Defi- 
ciencies and Penalties) of the Rules and 
Regulations for the Federal Home Loan Bank 
System (“Bank Regulations”), the liquidity 
requirements of Section 523.11 (Liquidity 
Requirements) of the Bank Regulations 


187 


as applied to (institution) shall be reduced by (1) 
the amount of (acquired institution’s) liquidity 
deficiencies existing at the Effective Date and (2) 
any aggregate net withdrawals (excess of with- 
drawal over cash savings received) from (acquired 
institution’s) offices after the Effective Date. 


References to disappearing institution(s) in items C-D 
below, include state-chartered associations not pre- 
viously subject to Federal law, or Federally chartered 
institutions previously in violtion of Federal law. 


Limitation on Service Corporation Investment 


C. In determining (resulting institution’s) compliance 
with Section 545.74 (Investments in Service Corpo- 
rations) of the Federal Regulations, the value of 
any service corporation acquired by (resulting 
institution) or by a subsidiary of (resulting institu- 
tion) in connection with the merger shall not, for a 
period of five years following the Effective Date, be 
deemed to be an investment by (resulting institu- 
tion) in that subsidiary. 


This forbearance generally will be granted 
where a request demonstrates that without 
such a forbearance the resulting association, in 
a supervisory merger, would be unable to 
meet, or only marginally meet, the investment 
in service corporation limitations of FR 545.74. 
This waiver will be confined to situations 
where the disappearing institution(s) had in- 
vestments in service corporations in excess of 
that authorized for Federal institutions. 


188 189 
Phase-out of Operations Inconsistent with HOLA 


C. (Resulting institution) shall be given a reasonable 


Building Investment

period of time, but not to exceed ( ) (months/ 
years), to phase out, dispose of or discontinue in an 
orderly manner, any acquired investments, ac- 
tivities, or operations inconsistent with the Home 
Owners’ Loan Act of 1933 as amended (HOLA), 
provided that such action is taken at the earliest 
possible date without material loss to (resulting 
institution). 


This forbearance generally will be granted where a 
request from an acquiring Federal association 
demonstrates that the disappearing association(s) in 
a supervisory merger had investments and/or 
engaged in activities which are not authorized by 
the HOLA.

exceed ( ) years, be deemed in violation of any of 
the provisions of Section 545.74 (Service Corpora-

Savings and Loan System by reason that any 
activities of a service corporation acquired as part 
of the merger(s), at or before the effective date(s), 
are not approved or authorized by the Home 
Owners’ Loan Act of 1933, as amended. 


This forbearance geneally will be granted 
where a request from an acquiring Federal 
association demonstrates that the disappearing 
association(s) in a supervisory merger had a 
service corporation or subsidiary which 
engaged in activities not authorized by FR 
545.74 or by the HOLA.

the resulting Federal association would be unable 
to meet, or only marginally meet, the investment 
limitations contained in FR 545.77. 


Holding Company Net Worth Requirements 
Supervisory Mergers 


F-1, For the purpose of calculating any holding 


company net worth maintenance requirements of 
(Holding Company) for (resulting institution), the 
Board will exclude, for a period of five years, the 
asset and liability balances of (disappearing 
institution(s)). 


This forbearance is applicable to cases where a 
holding company has a net worth maintenance 
requirement for a subsidiary institution which 
is merging with a supervisory case. This 
forebearance will be confined to supervisory 
mergers where the acquired association is a 
very serious supervisory problem. 


Acquisitions by a Holding Company or Individual(s) 
F-2. There is no standard forbearance language ex- 


empting acquiring holding companies or individ- 
uals from maintaining net worth. This is because 


190 


it is OES policy to mandate that new acquirers 
satisfy the regulatory net worth requiremeat 
with respect to insured subsidiaries. Only in the 
most unusual circumstanes will any modification 
of this requirement be considered. 


Divided Limitations 
Supervisory Mergers and 


Acquisitions by a Holding Company or Individual(s) 


G. The Board may eliminate the 50% of net income 


dividend limitation for (resulting or acquired insti- 
tution) which is controlled by (holding company). 
The limitation may be waived only where dividends 
are going to service holding company debt. 


This forbearance will rarely be granted, and 
when granted, will be confined to supervisory 
mergers or acquisitions where the acquired 
institution is a very serious supervisory problem. 
Any request for elimination of the 50% of net 
income dividend limitation must be supported by 
convincing evidence that the acquiring holding 
company is strongly capitalized and will agree to 
maintain the net worth of the subsidiary insured 
institution at the required regulatory level as 
stated in Section 563.13. 


(It should be noted that eligible dividends are 
calculated based on net income from the time that 
the holding company acquired stock of an institu- 
tion. “Acquired” means the date of the consumma- 
tion of the purchase of the association’s stock by the 
holding company. Preacquisition earnings may not 
be distributed.)

Acquisitions by a Holding Company or Individual(s) 
1-2 In connection with acquisitions by a_ holding 


company or individual(s), or any other entity, the 
use of push-down accounting will be permitted to 
the extent authorized by GES Memorandum R-55, 
dated January 17, 1983, or as may be subsequently 
amended. As stated in R-55, appropriate evidence 
must be supplied by an independent public 
accountant that both the establishment of a new 
basis of accounting at the time of the acquisition 
and the current use of push-down accounting are 
in conformity with GAAP. 


Where push-down accounting in authorized, the 
institution may request forbearances identified 
below as HC/I-1, HC/I-2, HC/I-3 and HC/I-4, or 
any combination of these forbearances. For- 
bearance HC/I-1 serves as a prerequisite to the 
granting of forbearances HC/I-2, HC/I-3, and HC/ 
1-4, and, therefore, of necessity, must be part of 
any request for accounting forbearances. 


The selection of any of these forbearances requires 
the addition of a subsection from HC/I-5. The 
matching is as follows:

OTHER ASSETS (Cont’d) 


Deferred Net Losses (Gains) on: — Lines A520 and 
A530 


If gains exceed losses (resulting in a net credit), 
enclose the net amount in brackets [ ]. 


Line A520 — Loans Sold 


Record the net of all deferred losses and gains on 
the sale of loans as required by Regulation 
563¢.14. 


Line A530 - Other Assets Sold 


Record on this line the net of all deferred losses 
and gains on the sale of other assets as defined 
by Regulation 563c.14. This includes such items 
as: (1) redeemable ground rent leases; (2) 
mortgage-related securities; (3) preferred stock 
that at the time of issuance provides for 
redemption on a fixed date in a fixed dollar 
amount or for redemption pursuant to a fixed 
schedule of periodic payments and has a remain- 
ing term to maturity of at least five years; and 
(4) debt securities that do not qualify as liquid 
assets under Regulation 523.10(g) because of 
their maturities or that have remaining terms to 
maturity of at least five years. Do not report 
deferred profits on sale of real estate that should 
be reported on Line A580 (Other Assets). 


Intangible Assets:- Lines A542 and A544 


Line A542 — Purchased Loan Servicing 


Report on this line the unamortized cost of 
purchased loan servicing rights. The amortization 


203 


of purchased loan servicing rights is deducted 
from Line D090 (Loan Servicing Fees). 


Line A544 - Goodwill and Other 


Report on this line the total amount of unamor- 
tized goodwill and intangibles other than pur- 
chased loan servicing reportable on Line A542. 
Include goodwill and other identified intangibles 
that originated from the application of purchase 
or pushdown accounting. For pushdown account- 
ing, see Bank Board memorandum R-55a. The 
amortization of goodwill and other intangibles is 
reported on Line E110 (Amortization of Good- 
will). One-time writeoffs of goodwill are reported 
on E310 (Other Nonoperating Expense). 


REGULATORY CAPITAL EXCLUDING GEN- 
aa LOAN-LOSS RESERVES - Lines C012 thru 
115 


General Loan-Loss Reserves are discussed in the 
instructions for Line C115 (Retained Earnings). 


Preferred Stock: - Lines C012 and C014 


Include on the appropriate line, stock issued by 
the institution that is guaranteed priority over 
common stock in liquidation and in the payment 
of dividends by the institution’s corporate charter 
and that qualifies as regulatory capital as defined 
in Regulations 561.13 and 563.7-5. Exclude non- 
qualifying preferred stock and preferred stock 
issued by a finance subsidiary, reportable on Line 
B100 (Other Borrowings). 


204 


Line C012 — Perpetual 


Report here preferred stock that has no stated 
maturity and cannot be redeemed except at the 
option of the issuer. Perpetual preferred stock 
also includes preferred stock that is convertible 
into common stock. 


Line C014 - Other Qualifying 

Report on this line qualified nonpermanent 
preferred stock (e.g., preferred stock that has a 
stated maturity, a required redemption provision, 
or a redemption that is at the option of the 
holder). 


Line C020 -Permanent, Reserve, or Guaranty 
Stock 


Enter the par value of all outstanding perma- 
nent, reserve, or guaranty stock issued by the 
institution. Contributed capital in excess of par 
value net of the cost of issuing stock is to be 
reported on Line C030 (Contributed Capital). The 
par value of treasury stock (stock reacquired by 
the institution) must be deducted from this line 
to obtain the outstanding stock of the institution 
(that amount issued to others). 


Line C030 - Contributed Capital 

Report paid-in capital in excess of par value net 
of the cost of issuing stock. Generally, paid-in 
capital arises from the sale of capital stock for an 
amount in excess of par value. Deduct from this 
line the amount paid for stock reacquired by the 
institution (treasury stock) in excess of its par 
value. Also includable on this line are (1) capital 
contributions by the stockholders and/or holding 
company not related to the purchase of stock; and 


205 


(2) permanent capital contributions by the FSLIC 
not in the form of the capital instruments 
reportable on Lines C070 thru C090. 


Line C040 - Qualifying Mutual i 
98: Q ying Capital 
Report on this line all outstanding mutual capital 
certificates that quality as equity capital. Non- 
qualified mutual capital certificates must be 
included on Line B180 (Other Liabilities). Refer 
to Regulation 563.7-4. 


Line C050 - Qualifying Subordinated Debentures 
Enter debt claims represented by subordinated 
debentures issued by the institution that qualify 
for inclusion in regulatory capital in accordance 
with Regulation 561.13. Accrued interest payable 
is reportable on Line B110 (Accrued Interest 
Payable). Interest expense on subordinated de- 
bentures is reportable on Line E190 (INTEREST 
CHARGES ON: Subordinated Debentures). 


Lined C060 - Appraised Equity Capital 

Report on this line the excess of appraised value 
over (1) net book value of office land, buildings 
and improvements, and eligible leasehold im- 
provements, owned by the insured institution or 
any of its subsidiaries; and (2) the deferred profit 
from a sale with leaseback of formerly owned 
office properties. The amount entered on this line 
must equal the sum of Lines A430 and A500 
(Appraisal Increment). The appraised value is 
equal to the “one time only” appraisal permitted 
by the Principal Supervisory Agent as provided 
for in Regulation 563.13(c). The portion of 
appraised equity capital attributable to unamor- 
tized deferred profit includes only that allowable 


—— Eo 


206 


under Regulation 563.13(c) and must be adjusted 
to reflect the current level of unamortized 
deferred profits. The amount reported on this 
line must be reduced for sales, other dispositions, 
and the aforementioned amortization of deferred 
profits. 


Line C070 — Net Worth Certificates 


Include on this line Net Worth Certificates 
(NWCs) issued to the FSLIC.

ACQUISITION PROPOSAL 


I. Introductory Statement 


The Statesman Group, Inc., (“Statesman”) head- 
quartered in Des Moines, Lowa, proposes to acquire 
substantially all of the assets and liabilities of First 
Federated Savings Bank, West Palm Beach, Florida; 
First Federal Savings Bank, Waterloo, Iowa; Peoples 
Federal Savings and Loan Association, Waterloo, 
Iowa, and The Perpetual Savings and Loan Associa- 
tion, Waterloo, Lowa, (collectively the ‘ Associations ) 
through a supervisory acquisition assisted by the 
Federal Savings and Loan Insurance Corporation 


(“FSLIC”). 
Il. Description of The Statesman Group, Inc. 


Statesman is a financial services holding company 
which, through its subsidiaries, engages primarily in 
the business of life insurance and, to a lesser — 
in the businesses of investment banking and retal 
brokerage and property-casualty insurance. In a 
tion, in January, 1987, Statesman, through a 60%- 
owned subsidiary, Statesman Mortgage Holding =. 
pany, purchased all of the outstanding stock of a 
mortgage company, Statesman Mortgage ne a 
An organizational chart describing Statesman s sub- 
sidiary and affiliated companies is attached - 
Appendix A. Statesman was incorporated in 1959 ” 
its common stock is traded on a national over-the- 


counter market. 


III. Description of Acquiring Entity 


Statesman intends to establish and incorporate a 
unitary savings and loan holding company which, for 


211 


purpose of identification, shall be referred to herein 
as “Statesman Savings Holding Corp.” or “SSHC.” 
The initial capital infusion into SSHC shall be 
$21,000,000, of which 60% ($12,600,000) shall be 
represented by preferred stock issued by Statesman 
to SSHC in exchange for preferred stock issued by 
SSHC to Statesman. The other 40% ($8,400,000) shail 
be in the form of cash contributed by American Life 
and Casualty Insurance Company (“American Life”), 
a wholly-owned Statesman subsidiary, in exchange for 
voting common stock issued by SSHC to American 
Life. As a result, American Life will have voting 
control of SSHC. 

SSHC will in turn establish and incorporate a 
wholly-owned subsidiary federal savings bank, and for 
purposes of references herein shall be identified as 
“Statesman Bank for Savings, FSB” or “SBS.” SSHC 
will contribute all of the Statesman preferred stock 
and all of the cash acquired from American Life to 
SBS in exchange for SBS common stock, thereby 
capitalizing SBS in the amount of $21,000,000. Thus 
SBS would be the acquiring corporate entity of the 
assets and liabilities of the four associations which are 
the subject of the bid proposal. However, references 
in this bid proposal and the Business Plan may refer 
to Statesman as the acquirer, it being understood 
that Statesman is submitting this bid and intends that 
the acquisition occur in the manner set forth herein. 
It is further understood that FSLIC shall deliver said 


_ assets and liabilities on a “turnkey” basis, with 


FSLIC providing for the combining of the assets and 
liabilities of the Associations prior to the acquisition, 
establishment and incorporation of SSHC and SBS, 
transfer of assets and liabilities to SBS, and recording 
and filing all documents in accordance with various 
state and federal requirements, all at the cost of 
FSLIC. 


212 213 


IV. Bid Proposal amount, as of the date of acquisition, which is 
Statesman proposes to purchase the four associa- equal to the combined deficit of the Associations, 
ions identi in under the following terms and adjusted for: 

er a — . (i). Increase: Write-off of goodwill, other 


1. Subsidiaries of Associations. All subsidiary 
corporations of the four Associations and their 
assets, including mortgage servicing rights, shall 
be included as assets included in this transaction. 
The stock of such subsidiaries (or the assets and 
remaining liabilities in the event of dissolution 
prior to acquisition) shall be transferred to SBS. 
FSLIC will cause and ensure the transfer of all 
such mortgage servicing rights to SBS or a 
subsidiary of Statesman which is duly qualified to 
service such mortgages. It is a condition of this 
proposal that there be no material change (more 
than 15%) in the total dollar volume of the 
mortgage servicing portfolio between September 
30, 1987 and the date of acquisition. 

2. Capital Infusion by Acquirer. In the man- 
ner described in Section III above, Statesman 
will cause SBS to have a capital infusion of 
$21,000,000, consisting of 40% ($8,400,000) cash 
and 60% ($12,600,000) Statesman preferred stock. 

3. Additional Contribution by Statesman. As 
additional consideration for the acquisition of the 
Associations, SBS agrees to assume losses arising 
from disposition and/or maintaining assets other 
than Covered Assets, as defined below, acquired 
in the transaction, which are estimated to be 
approximately $26,000,000. 

4. FSLIC Assistance Required. FSLIC assist- 
ance is required in this transaction. The forms of 
assistance sought and required are as follows: 

a. Cash and Promissory Note. FSLIC will 
contribute cash and a note receivable in an 


intangibles, deferred losses and appraised equity 
capital; 

(ii) Decrease: All valuation allowances (ex- 
cluding allowances for one to four-family dwelling 
units, owned or mortgaged) adjusted for un- 
collectable accrued interest receivable; and 

(ili) Increase: Equity in the adjusted losses 
of Federated Financial Corporation, the wholly- 
owned mortgage banking subsidiary of First 
Federated Savings Bank. 

Thirty percent (30%) of this contribution shall 
be payable to SBS in the form of cash within five 
(5) days of the date of acquisition. Seventy 
percent (70%) of this amount shall be payable in 
the form of a promissory note executed and 
delivered within five (5) days of the date of 
acquisition. It is projected that the principal 
amount of this contribution as at December 31, 
1987 will exceed $115,000,000, and will increase 
between that date and the actual date of 
acquisition. The FSLIC note shall bear variable 
interest, adjusted quarterly, at a rate equal to 
the Eighth District cost of funds plus 200 basis 
points. The note will be amortized over five years 
and principal and interest payments shall be paid 
quarterly and may be prepayable without penalty 
at FSLIC’s option. : 

b. Losses on Covered Assets. FSLIC will pay 
to SBS quarterly cash payments equal to 93 
percent of losses incurred on the disposition of 
Covered Assets during the prior quarter for a 
period of five (5) years from the date of 


214 


acquisition. Covered Assets are defined as 
follows: 

(i) Real Estate Owned, Real Estate Held 
for Investment, and Real Estate in Judgment, 
excluding in all three categories one to four 
family dwelling units. 

(ii) The principal on all mortgage and non- 
mortgage loans which at the date of acquisition 
are classified or ninety days or more delinquent 
and the principal and accrued interest receivable 
on mortgage and non-mortgage loans which 
become classified or ninety days or more delin- 
quent within two years of the acquisition, 
excluding loans secured by first liens or first 
mortgages on one to four-family dwelling units 
and direct or participation loans with a total 
balance of $50,000 or less. (If a loan has an SBS 
participation under a $50,000 balance but the 
total balance exceeds $50,000, the loan will be a 
covered asset provided it is not on a one to four 
family dwelling unit secured by a SBS first 
mortgage.) 

(iii) Investments in and loans to subsidi- 
aries. Losses shall be the difference between the 
carrying (book) value of Covered Assets (plus 
accrued interest if applicable under subparagraph 
(ii) immediately above) before considering re- 
corded loss allowances, and the net sales pro- 
ceeds realized by SBS on the disposition of such 
assets. 

c. Yield Maintenance. FSLIC will provide 
cash payments on a quarterly basis for spread 
and yield maintenance on Covered Assets in an 
amount equal to the Eight District cost of funds 
plus 225 basis points. 

d. Indemnifications. FSLIC will indemnify 
Statesman, its subsidiaries, affiliates, officers, 


215 


directors and employees for a period of five (5) 
years for and against: 

(i) All undisclosed or unrecorded (not 
booked) liabilities of the Associations, their 
subsidiaries and affiliates, in their respective 
September 30, 1987 financial statements; 

(ii) All claims not reserved for and subse- 
quently asserted against the Associations, their 
subsidiaries or affiliates, or Statesman, its sub- 
sidiaries, officers and directors, as the Acquirer 
and successor of the Associations; 

(iii) Litigation brought to challenge or set 
aside the transaction on the basis of any alleged 
illegal action by the Federal Home Loan Bank 
Board or FSLIC; and 

(iv) Any claims based on ERISA rights 
and any unfunded employment and/or severance 
benefit plans, policies, or contracts, other 
employee benefit contracts, employment con- 
tracts, and pension plans, with employees or ex- 
employees of the Associations or any of their 
subsidiaries, which exist at, or existed prior to 
the time of acquisition. 

5. Regulatory Considerations and For- 
bearances. As a part of the proposed acquisition, 
certain regulatory forbearances and considera- 
tions will be granted or procured by FSLIC as 
identified below: 

a. For regulatory purposes, with respect to 
the books and records of SBS, push-down 
accounting shall be used to record the purchase 
of SBS’s capital stock by SSHC. Twenty one 
million dollars of the cash contribution to be 
made to SBS pursuant to an assistance agree- 
ment to be entered into between the FSLIC and 
Statesman is to be a credit to SBS’s regulatory 


216 


capital; therefore, for regulatory accounting pur- 
poses, SBS will book such contribution as a direct 
addition to its net worth. Accordingly, imme- 
diately after the acquisition, the regulatory 
capital of SBS will be $42,000,000 which will 
consist of $21,000,000 in capital infused by SSHC, 
plus $21,000,000 in capital contributed by the 
FSLIC which will be treated as regulatory 
capital. Not later than 30 days following the 
acquisition, SBS shall submit to the Supervisory 
Agent an opinion of independent certified public 
accountants that SBS has accounted for the 
transaction in accordance with generally accepted 
accounting principles (“GAAP”) including the use 
of “push down” accounting, except that as 
provided by the Board, for purposes of reporting 
to the Board, twenty-one million dollars of the 
cash contribution by the FSLIC to SBS pursuant 
to the assistance agreement shall be deemed a 
contribution to regulatory net worth and will be 
booked as a direct credit to SBS. 

b. The FSLIC will permit Statesman and 
SSHC to enter into a capital maintenance 
agreement for SBS that is limited to: 

(i) an agreement to cause the regulatory 
capital of SBS to be maintained at a level 
consistent with that required by 12 C.F.R. 
563.13(b) as now or hereinafter in effect, and will 
infuse sufficient capital, in the same ratio and 
form as the initial contribution as set forth in 
Section IV, paragraph 2 above, to effect com- 
pliance with such requirement, provided that the 
aggregate amount of the combined obligation of 
Statesman, SSHC and American Life shall not 
exceed an amount equal to $4 million. 

(ii) an agreement that in the event SBS’s 
ratio of regulatory capital to total liabilities at 


217 


the end of any given calendar quarter falls below 
2 percent, and Statesman and SSHC are unwill- 
ing and/or unable to infuse sufficient additional 
equity capital to restore SBS’s ratio of regulatory 
capital to total liabilities to 2%, regardless of the 
amount of equity capital previously infused into 
SBS pursuant to the aforementioned agreement, 
the FSLIC will have the right to vote the 
securities acquired by Statesman and SSHC, 
remove SBS’s board of directors, and/or dispose 
of any or all of the voting securities of SBS 
owned by Statesman, American Life or SSHC. 

This proposed agreement is intended to satisfy 
two of Statesman’s concerns. First, as a diver- 
sified company, Statesman is concerned to ensure 
that its debt rating is not downgraded as a result 
of the company becoming subject to an unlimited 
contingent obligation. Second, as a matter of 
business judgment, Statesman is equally con- 
cerned by the prospect of becoming subject to an 
unlimited capital guarantee. This proposed capital 
agreement is based upon the agreements used in 
certain acquisitions of healthy thrifts by diver- 
sified aeyuirers, such as Ford, Fuqua, and 
Temple-Inland and is modified to reflect the fact 
that this is a supervisory transaction. 

(ce) The FSLIC will give permanent and contin- 
uing approval to the issuance of debt by 
Statesman and its affiliates (or waive the debt 
requirement under section 408(m) of the National 
Housing Act), so long as the regulatory capital of 
SBS meets the amount required at the time the 
debt is issued. At the time Statesman (with 
approximately one billion dollars in assets), 
acquires SBS (with approximately $700 million in 
assets), it is anticipated that Statesman will 


218 


qualify as a diversified savings and loan holding 
company and will not be subject to the debt 
limitations of 12 U.S.C. 1730a or 12 C.F.R. 584. 
However, as demonstrates « the Business Plan, 
Statesman plans to transform SBS into a profita- 
ble and growing institution. The successful opera- 
tion and growth of SBS could cause Statesman to 
become a nondiversified institution subject to the 
debt restrictions. Due to the peculiarities of the 
insurance industry and the wide diversification of 
Statesman’s financial service activities, States- 
man’s debt requirements are highly unpredictable 
and variable, and the standard prior-approval 
procedure would therefore pose a significant 
hardship. 

A waiver or prior approval for Statesman’s 
debt would not compromise the regulatory objec- 
tives of the National Housing Act or the FSLIC. 
The purpose of the debt limitation is to ensure 
that savings and loan holding companies do not 
become over leveraged and seek to drain assets 
from a subsidiary insured institution. Since as 
part of this acquisition Statesman will agree to 
maintain the capital of the acquired institution at 
certain levels, and Statesman has not requested a 
forbearance from the standard limitation of 
dividends to 50% of net earnings, the FSLIC will 
have no need to rely upon the cumbersome pro- 
cedures for debt approval in order to protect the 
capital of SBS. Furthermore, Statesman operates 
in the highly regulated and conservative insur- 
ance industry where regulators impose capital 
requirements on its subsidiaries. (Statesman’s 
broker-dealer subsidiary is likewise subject to 
capital requirements). Consequently, there is no 
regulatory purpose to be served by requiring 
such prior approval for debt. 


219 


d. The FSLIC will authorize the acquisition 
under section 408(m) of the National Housing 
Act, 12 U.S.C. 1730a, will determine under such 
authority that Statesman and its affiliates are 
exempt from the operation of section 408(p) of 
the National Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 1730(p), with 
respect to the cross-marketing of products or 
services between SBS and Statesman, its subsidi- 
aries or its affiliates, and will confirm that such 
sales, as described herein, are consistent with 
section 408(d) of the National- Housing Act. 

Statesman is a leading provider of life and 
property and casualty insurance products and 
services, and currently markets more annuities 
and other insurance products and services 
through savings and loan associations than 
through any other form of retail outlet. As 
described in the Business Plan, Statesman would 
intend to sell such insurance products and 
services through the facilities of SBS. Under this 
arrangement, insurance products and services 
would be sold on the premises and by the 
employees of SBS, and for these marketing 
efforts the institution and/or its employees would 
receive payments from the Statesman insurance 
affiliates at some contractually determined rate. 

Under the arrangement outlined above, mar- 
keting services would be provided by SBS and its 
employees on behalf of Statesman. Such market- 
ing arrangements would not violate the affiliate- 
transaction provisions of section 408(d) of the 
National Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 1730(d), and 
regulations thereunder, nor violate the affiliated- 
party regulations found in subchapter D of the 
Board’s regulations because Statesman and its 
insurance affiliates would provide no services to, 


220 


and receive no payments for services to, SBS. 
Likewise, while Statesman and its subsidiaries 
would intend to deposit funds in SBS, such 
deposits cannot violate the prohibitions against 
maintaining compensating balances because the 
deposits will be made in, rather than by, SBS. 
Statesman requests formal confirmation of these 
conclusions. 

In addition, section 408(p) of the National 
Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 1730(p), as recently added 
by the Competitive Equality Banking Act of 
1987, generally forbids an insured institution 
which is a subsidiary of a diversified savings and 
loan holding company from offering or marketing 
products or services of an affiliate that are not 
permissible for bank holding companies to pro- 
vide under section 4(c)(8) of the Bank Holding 
Company Act, or from offering or marketing its 
own such products or services through an 
affiliate. Under the recently re-enacted section 
408(m) of the National Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 
1730a(m), the FSLIC with certain exceptions is 
permitted to authorize a supervisory acquisition 
“notwithstanding any provisions of the laws or 
constitution of any State or any provision of 
Federal law.” The exceptions do not include the 
cross-marketing prohibitions of section 408(p). 
Consequently the FSLIC is authorized under 
section 408(m) to provide relief from the section 
408(p) prohibitions. 

e. The acquisitions will occur pursuant to 
FSLIC authorization under section 408(m) of the 
National Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 1730a, and the 
FSLIC will affirmatively find that such authori- 
zation pre-empts the laws of any state that would 
limit or preclude the affiliation of SBS and 


221 


Statesman or any of its insurance affiliates or 
would, by reason of such affiliation, prohibit the 
sale of insurance by Statesman, its insurance 
affiliates or agents. 

Statesman’s principal business is insurance 
which is provided by companies organized in 
several jurisdictions and sold by agents located in 
some 47 states. Various states prohibit the 
affiliation of insurance companies and depository 
institutions or prohibit the sale of insurance by 
agents of companies with such affiliations. The 
federal pre-emption of such states limits is a 
necessary prerequisite to Statesman’s ability to 
continue to conduct its insurance business. The 
FSLIC’s authority in this regard under section 
408(m) was recently clarified by Congress. See 
H.R. Rep. No. 261, 100th Cong., Ist Sess. 140. 

f. The acquisition will occur pursuant to au- 
thorization under section 408(m) of the National 
Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 1730a, and that the 
FSLIC will find that the affiliation of SBS and 
Statesman’s broker/dealer subsidiary, R.G. 
Dickinson & Co. is not prohibited by section 
408(r) of the National Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 
1730a(r). In this connection, subsection 
408(r(2D) provides an exception from the 
prohibition for an affiliation that results from an 
acquisition under section 408(m) of an institution 
(such as SBS) with total assets of $500 million or 
more at the time of the acquisition. FSLIC will 
assure Statesman that it can continue to simul- 
taneously own SBS and R.G. Dickinson & Co. 

g. The FSLIC will require that the acquisition 
of the shares of the newly converted institution, 
SBS (and therefore the acquisition of the shares 
of its parent companies, SSHC and Statesman), 


222 


be subject to the limitations found in section 
563b.3(i)(3) of the FSLIC’s conversion regula- 
tions, 12 C.F.R. 563b.3(i(3), for at least 18 
months. 

The FSLIC’s conversion regulations specifi- 
cally provide that the restriction of section 
563b.3(i(3) on the shares of a converted thrift 
will be applied to the stock of a thrift organized 
to acquire an insolvent thy in a voluntary 
supervisory conversion. 12 C.F.R. 563b.10(c)(2). 
The Board has indicated that restrictions would 
likewise be applicable to the parent of the 
resulting thrift. See 51 Fed. Reg. 40137 (1986). 
The regulation provides that section 563b.3(i)(3) 
shall be applicable for eighteen months in the 
case of the acquisition of an insolvent institution 
with assets of between $500 and $750 million, 
such as SBS. In the case of the involuntary 
conversion of an insolvent institution under 
section 408(m) of the National Housing Act, the 
FSLIC may require, as a condition of its 
authorization, that similar limits be imposed on 
the shares of SBS, and thereby SSHC and 
Statesman. 

h. For regulatory purposes, currently or subse- 
quently scheduled items or classified assets will 
be excluded in the calculation of capital or ratio 
requirements of SBS for a period of five years. 

i. FSLIC shall make available a line of credit 
for a period of two years for liquidity protection 
for SBS. 

j. For a period of two years following the 
acquisition date, in determining whether a 
penalty shall be assessed against or paid by SBS 
pursuant to Section 523.12 (Deficiencies and 
Penalties) of the Rules and Regulations for the 


223 


Federal Home Loan Bank System (“Bank Reg- 
ulations”), the liquidity requirements of Section 
523.11 (Liquidity Requirements) of the Bank 
Regulations as applied to SBS shall be reduced 
by (1) the amount of SBS’s liquidity deficiencies 
existing at the acquisition date and (2) any 
aggregate net withdrawals (excess of with- 
drawals over cash savings received) from SBS’s 
offices after the acquisition date. 

k. SBS will be entitled to establish additional 
branches in Florida and Iowa, including, but not 
limited to, Des Moines, Iowa, and to establish a 
consolidated headquarters office of SBS in Des 
Moines, Iowa. 

l. In prior discussions with FSLIC concerning 
a bid proposal, it was Statesman’s management’s 
understanding that the establishment of branches 
in one additional state would be favorably 
considered by FSLIC. Statesman requests and 
requires as part of this bid proposal the right to 
establish branches of SBS in the State of 
Missouri. 

m. Recognition by FSLIC that the preferred 
stock of Statesman owned by SBS will be a 
qualifying direct investment. 

n. SBS shall be given 3 years, to phase out, 
dispose of or discontinue in an orderly manner, 
any acquired investments, activities, or opera- 
tions inconsistent with the Home Owners’ Loan 
Act of 1933 as amended (HOLA), provided that 
such action is taken at the earliest possible date 
without material loss to SBS. 

o. SBS shall not for a period of 3 years, be 
deemed in violation of any of the provisions of 
Section 545.74 (Service Corporations) of the 
Rules and Regulations for the FSLIC by reason 


224 


that any activities of a service corporation 
acquired as part of the merger(s), at or before 
the effective date(s), are not approved or author- 
ized by the Home Owners’ Loan Act of 1933, as 
amended. 

p. For a period of five years after the 
acquisition, for purposes of calculating SBS’s 
building investment under Section 545.77 (Real 
Estate for Office and Related Facilities) of the 
Federal Regulations, all building investments in 
the acquired associations may be excluded. 

q. The Board will provide the certification that 
the grounds set forth in section 1464(d)(b)(A)- 
(iii), or (iii) of Title 12, United States Code, 
exist with respect to the Associations, or will 
exist in the near future in the absence of action 
by the Board. 

6. Exclusion of Certain Leases. With the 
exception of a start-up period of not more than 
three months from the date of acquisition, 
Statesman will not assume or be responsible for 
the executive office lease of First Federated 
Savings Bank in Florida or of the office lease of 
Federated Financial Corporation in Atlanta, 
Georgia. 

7. Maintenance of Deposits. This bid proposal 
is submitted on the assumption that there are 
approximately $631 million of deposits in the 
combined associations. In the event that the 
combined deposits should decrease by more than 
10% by the actual data of acquisition, Statesman 
reserves the right to renegotiate its participation 
in the assumption of losses. 


V. Business Plan. 


Submitted herewith and incorporated by reference 
as part of the acquisition proposal is the Business 


225 


Plan of the SBS. This Business Plan provides a 
description of the proposed acquisition and is sup- 
ported by financial data of the combined associations, 
including three-year forecasts of quarterly volumes, 
rate/yield analysis, interest income/expense report, 
profit-loss report, ratio report, cashflow forecast, and 
a pro forma combined statement of condition as of 
December 31, 1987 of the four associations, as well as 
individual pro forma statements of condition of each 
association. This Business Plan, including the financial 
information submitted therein, forms the basis upon 
which the acquisition proposal is being made. 


VI. Management and Directorate of Acquirer. 


Attached hereto as Appendix B is a listing of the 
officers and directors of Statesman. Attached hereto 
as Appendix C is a listing of the officers and directors 
of American Life. No selection has yet been made of 
prospective directors for SBS. However, it is antici- 
pated that one or more of the directors of Statesman 
and American Life would serve as directors of SBS 
and its holding company, and other directors would be 
selected on the basis of their experience and 
expertise regarding savings institutions, as well as 
their general proven business capabilities.

At a meeting of the Board of Directors of 
Statesman * id on November 11, 1987, D. J. Noble 
was auth sized on behalf of Statesman to enter into 
discussions and negotiations with FSLIC for the 
possible acquisition of federally-chartered savings 
institutions. Attached hereto as Appendix D is a

grave, Selvy, Kelly & Eddy, counsel for Statesman, to 


226 


the Regional Director and Deputy Director of FSLIC 
setting forth this authority. 


VIII. Accountants’ Statement. 


Submitted herewith as Appendix E is the requested 
statement from Ernst & Whinney, the independent ac- 
countants for Statesman, regarding the application of 
generally accepted accounting principles to the pro- 
posed transaction (purchase vs. pooling of interests), 
the estimated amount of unidentified intangible assets 
resulting therefrom, and the estimated amortization 
periods of such intangibles as presented in the 
accompanying Business Plan. 


IX. Tax Considerations and Benefits. 


Based upon the method of taxation of insurance 
organizations limiting utilization of non-life insurance 
operating losses, and the present loss carryforward 
position of the non-life insurance entities reflected in 
the consolidated Statesman tax return, loss car- 
ryovers of the acquired (non-life) associations may 
provide no tax benefits to Statesman. However, it is 
agreed that Statesman will cause an annual review of 
its tax position to be made through a with/without 
computation, and to the extent any tax benefits are 
actually realized in cash by Statesman directly from 
inclusion of the associations in the consolidated 
Statesman tax return, Statesman agrees to share 50% 
of those tax recoveries or payment reductions with 
FSLIC. 


X. Other Considerations. 


Statesman is the 60% owner of the holding company 
of Statesman Mortgage Company, which is located in 


227 


Des Moines, Iowa. It is anticipated that the mortgage 
business activities now being conducted by Federated 
Financial Corporation, the subsidiary of First Fede- 
rated Savings Bank, will be transferred to Statesman 
Mortgage Company. Therefore, the financial projec- 
tions contained in the Business Plan do not include 
future income and expense factors related to the 
business of Federated Financial Corporation. 

The foregoing Acquisition Proposal and accompany- 
ing Business Plan is submitted by The Statesman 
Group, Inc. this 8th day of January, 1988. 


The Statesman Group, Inc.

D.J. Noble 
Chairman of the Board 
and President

INTRODUCTION 


The Statesman Group, Inc. (“Statesman” or “Ac- 
quirer”) is submitting this Business Plan (“Plan”) in 
connection with its proposal to the Federal Home 
Loan Bank Board (“Board”), the Federal Savings and 
Loan Insurance Corporation (“FSLIC”), the Federal 
Home Loan Bank of Des Moines and the Federal 
Home Loan Bank of Atlanta for the acquisition of 
First Federated Savings Bank, West Palm Beach, 
Florida; First Federal Savings Bank, Waterloo, Iowa; 
Peoples Federal Savings and Loan Association, 
Waterloo, Iowa; and The Perpetual Savings and Loan 
Association, Waterloo, Iowa. 

The Plan assumes the acquisition is consummated 
on December 31, 1987 and that Statesman will acquire 
a unitary savings and loan holding company which 
will own a federally chartered savings bank, into 
which the assets and liabilities of the four associations 
are placed. The Plan and financial projections are 
presented for the resulting savings bank, hereinafter 
also referred to as “Statesman Bank for Savings, 
FSB” or “SBS”. 

The Plan projects a capital infusion in cash and 
preferred stock by Statesman, and/or its existing 
subsidiaries, initial financial assistance from FSLIC in 
the form of cash and an amortizing note receivable 
and continuing financial assistance in the form of cash 
from FSLIC. This assistance consists of spread and 
yield maintenance and guarantees of recovery by 
FSLIC of losses on certain assets (“Covered Assets”). 
The plan assumes that losses on other assets 
(“Uncovered Assets”) will be assumed by SBS. 


232 


The Plan also assumes the acquisition will be 
accounted for using the purchase method of account- 
ing, applied by the “push down” method, as of 
December 31, 1987. Purchase accounting is the 
method of recording a business acquisition by adjust- 
ing assets and liabilities to fair market value and 
setting net worth equal to the purchase price. “Push 
down” refers to recording the adjustments on the 
books of the acquired company. 

In connection with the preparation of the Plan, 
Statesman engaged Ferguson & Company, consult- 
ants to the savings and loan industry, to assist in the 
preparation of the Plan and to identify improvement 
opportunities and make recommendations for further 
action. The findings and recommendations of Fer- 
guson & Company are incorporated into the Plan, and 
such findings and recommendations have been re- 
viewed and approved by Statesman. 


DESCRIPTION OF THE ACQUIRER 


Statesman is a financial services holding company 
which, through its subsidiaries, engages primarily in 
the business of life insurance, including annuities, and 
to a lesser extent, in the business of investment 
banking and retail brokerage and property-casualty 
insurance. 

Although Statesman markets products in 47 states, 
the majority of its insurance business is conducted in 
the Midwest, California and the Southeast. Other 
financial services are provided primarily in the 
Midwest, Southwest and Western states through a 
regional investment banking and retail brokerage 
subsidiary. Statesman also has a mortgage banking 
subsidiary. Following is Statesman’s condensed his- 
torical financial information for the periods indicated 
(dollars in thousands):

CAPITAL INFUSION BY ACQUIRER 


Statesman intends to capitalize SBS through a 
transfer of assets from the savings and loan holding 
company of $8.4 million in cash and $12.6 million of 
preferred stock of Statesman, the combined total 
being approximately 3% of projected total liabilities 
of the combined associations at December 31, 1987. 
The Plan also projects that Statesman will incur 
approximately $26 million in goodwill and other 
intangibles in the transaction. 


FSLIC ASSISTANCE 


The acquisition of the associations will require 
FSLIC assistance. At September 30, 1987, the 
associations reported a combined deficit in excess of 
$82 million. 


234 


The Plan projects FSLIC will initially contribute 
cash and a note receivable bearing interest at the 
Eighth District cost of funds plus 200 basis points and 
equal to the combined deficit, adjusted for (1) the 
write-off of recorded goodwill, other intangibles, 
deferred losses and appraised equity capital; (2) 
commercial real estate valuation allowances (exclud- 
ing valuation allowances on single-family residential 
property or loans) adjusted for uncollectable accrued 
interest receivable; and (3) equity in the losses of 
Federated Financial Corporation, the wholly owned 
mortgage banking subsidiary of First Federated 
Savings Bank. The total initial FSLIC contribution is 
projected to be in excess of $115 million at December 
31, 1987, of which thirty percent shall be paid in cash 
and seventy percent in the form of a note amortized 
over a period of five years. 

The Pian also projects continuing FSLIC assistance 
in the form of quarterly cash payments equal to 93% 
of the losses on Covered Assets, which is assumed to 
be forty million dollars of the asset values for the 
Plan, plus spread and yield maintenance on Covered 
Assets equal to the Eighth District cost of funds plus 
225 basis points. Covered Assets include all real 
estate owned, all investment real estate, all invest- 
ments in and loans to subsidiaries and all nonperform- 
ing loans, each excluding first lien positions in one to 
four family residential dwelling units or loans, all as 
more fully set out in the proposal. For purposes of 
the Plan, Covered Assets are projected to be 
liquidated ratably over five years. 


[Tab 7] 


MONITORING OF THE BUSINESS PLAN 


Statesman will prepare comprehensive operating 
budgets based upon the Plan and such budgets will be 
reviewed and approved by the Board of Directors. 
The actual operating results will be compared with 
the budgets and such reports will be reviewed by the 
Directors on a quarterly basis. This procedure will 
allow the Board to take corrective action pertaining 
to variances between budgeted and actual items and 
make necessary revisions to operating strategies to 
insure compliance with the Plan. 

Monitoring of the Plan in its entirety will be 
evidenced in the minutes of the Board of Directors. A 
report of such review, together with an explanation of 
and justification for any proposed or material 
changes, will be submitted to the appropriate regula- 
tory authority. 


REVISION OF THE BUSINESS PLAN 


Variances between planned and actual results may 
be temporary or permanent. Temporary variances 
may arise from the inexact process of measuring 
expectations of the future timing of transactions. 
These differences are not expected to require a 
revision of the Plan. 

Permanent differences may arise from situations in 
which SBS has some control. During the process of 
the Board’s review of the Plan and its comparison to 
actual operating results, the underlying assumptions 
used in the projections will be reviewed for validity 


236 


with current economic conditions, strategies and 
market expectations. Should revisions be required, 
the Board will authorize appropriate changes to the 
Plan. Any material or significant changes authorized 
by the Board will be reviewed with and approved by 
the Supervisory Agent. 


METHOD OF ACCOUNTING 


The projected financial statements have been 
prepared with purchase accounting being applied by 
use of the “Push Down” method. Inasmuch as the 
acquisition does not give rise to continuity of 
ownership, Statesman believes that application of the 
purchase method is appropriate and is in accordance 
with generally accepted accounting principles. 

After the application of purchase accounting, the 
projected financial statements have been prepared as 
though the acquisition occurred on December 31, 
1987. 


REGULATORY CONSIDERATIONS 


The Plan is based upon the assumption that certain 
regulatory forbearances and considerations will be 
granted. These are requested by Statesman as a 
result of dispensations necessary to consummate the 
transaction, along with the current financial condition 
of the associations. The Plan is based on the granting 
of the requested forbearances and considerations as 
itemized in or incorporated by reference in the bid 
proposal. 

The financial data and projections submitted here- 
with form the economic basis upon which the proposal 
and the Business Plan were formulated.

NOTES TO PROJECTED FINANCIAL 
STATEMENTS 


(1) Significant accounting policies 


The accounting and reporting policies of Statesman 
Bank for Savings, FSB (“Bank”) conform to generally 
accepted accounting principles and to general prac- 
tices within the savings and loan industry. 


(2) Assumptions and estimates 


The projected financial statements are based on 
assumptions and estimates concerning future events 
and circumstances which were determined by the 
proposed purchaser. 

The assumptions disclosed herein are those be- 
lieved to be significant to the projections and/or are 
key factors upon which the financial results of the 
Bank depend and, unless otherwise indicated, are 
based on the circumstances and conditions that 
existed at the time the projections were prepared. 
Some assumptions will not materialize and unantici- 
pated events and circumstances may occur subse- 
quent to December 31, 1987, the date of this 
projection. Therefore, the actual results achieved 
during the periods covered by the projections will 
vary from the projections and such variations may be 
material. There are significant inherent risks in using 
outdated projections because events subsequent to 
their issuance may invalidate some or all of the 


238 


underlying assumptions and estimates. The assump- 
tions used are listed in “Significant Projection 
Assumptions” included elsewhere in this Plan. 


Cash and investments: 

The projected cash and investment balances at the 
end of each projected period are the residual balances 
that result, in part, from application of the following 
assumptions and estimates and, in part, from ex- 
pected efficiencies resulting from applications of 
improved cash management procedures. 


Loans receivable: 

Assumptions regarding mortgage loans and non- 
mortgage loans are listed in “Significant Projection 
Assumptions.” This projected other data provides a 


listing of projected originations, origination rates and 


renewal rates, if applicable, for each respective loan 
classification. Interest income on loans receivable is 
calculated by the projection model with respect to the 
activity and rates listed in “Significant Projection 
Assumptions.” 


Federal Home Loan Bank stock: 

FHLB stock is maintained in accordance with 
regulatory requirements over the projected period. 
The assumed dividend rate of FHLB stock during the 
three annual projection periods is listed in “Signifi- 
cant Projection Assumptions.” 


Property and equipment: 

Depreciation expense has been projected based 
upon acquired amounts, adjusted to include the 
projected equipment purchases. Depreciation has 
been calculated on the straight-line method over the 
estimated useful lives of the assets. 


239 


Savings deposits: 

All assumptions regarding transaction accounts, 
short term certificates of deposit, intermediate term 
certificates of deposit (over 6 months through 18 
months), long term certificates of deposit (more than 
18 months) and jumbo accounts are listed in “Signifi- 
cant Projection Assumptions.” This projected other 
data provides a listing of the projected changes and 
average cost of each respective savings deposit 
classification. Interest expense on savings deposits is 
calculated by the projection model with respect to the 
activity and rates as summarized in “Significant 
Projection Assumptions.” 


Loan origination fees: 

Loan origination fees are projected as a function of 
loan volume as outlined in “Significant Projection 
Assumptions.” All fees are calculated, as appropriate, 
by the projection model at 1% and deferred and 
amortized by the level yield mentod over the loan life. 


Other income and expense: 

All other items of income and expense were 
projected from analyses of the actual respective 
amounts (per books) for 1987 with increases or 
decreases appropriate to the Plan. 


Federal income taxes: 

Federal income taxes have not been provided since 
permanent differences, primarily nontaxable FSLIC 
assistance, offset pretax accounting income. 


Interest rate scenario: 

The interest rate scenario included in the Plan is 
based upon the September 1987 Savings and Loan 
Industry Assumptions prepared by the Federal Home 


240 


Loan Bank Board, adjusted for local economic 
conditions. The interest rates are listed in “Projected 
Interest Rates, Net Interest Income Analysis and 
Spread Analysis” presented elsewhere in this Plan. 


(3) Accounting for the acquisition 


The accompanying projected financial statements 
have been prepared assuming purchase accounting is 
applied to record the acquisition of the Bank and that 
purchase accounting is applied by the “push down” 
method as of December 31, 1987. Purchase accounting 
is the method of recording a business acquisition by 
adjusting assets and liabilities to market value and 
eliminating net worth. “Push down” refers to record- 
ing the adjustments on the books of the acquired 
company. 

Goodwill of approximately $25.8 million arising 
from the acquisition is being amortized over 12 years 
on the level yield method.

Il. INFORMATION REQUIRED BY ITEMS 
A. GENERAL BACKGROUND 
Item 1. Summary of Proposed Acquisition 


The proposed acquisition and any related transac- 
tions should be briefly summarized in a form that 
makes the acquisition and transactions readily 
understandable. 

The Statesman Group, Inc., (“Applicant”) headquar- 
tered in Des Moines, Iowa, proposes to acquire (the 
“Acquisition”), through a supervisory acquisition 
assisted by the Federal Savings and Loan Insurance 
Corporation (“FSLIC”), substantially all of the assets 
and liabilities of First Federated Savings Bank, West 
Palm Beach, Florida; First Federal Savings Bank, 
Waterloo, lowa; Peoples Federal Savings and Loan 
Association, Waterloo, Iowa; and The Perpetual 
Savings and Loan Association, Waterloo, Iowa. (The 
four institutions are collectively referred to herein as 
the “Associations.”) A copy of the acquisition proposal 
submitted to the FSLIC (the “Acquisition Proposal”) 
is attached as Exhibit A. The Acquisition Proposal, a 
proposed assistance agreement, and other matters are 
now under negotiation. 

As the first step in the Acquisition, Applicant 
intends to establish and incorporate Statesman Sav- 
ings Holding Corp., a unitary savings and loan 
holding company (the “Holding Company”). The 
initial capital infusion into the Holding Company will 
be $21 million, of which 60% ($12.6 million) will be 
represented by preferred stock issued by Applicant to 
the Holding Company in exchange for preferred stock 
issued by the Holding Company to Applicant. The 
other 40% ($8.4 million) will be in the form of cash 
contributed by American Life and Casualty Insurance 


244 


Company (the “Insurance Company”), a wholly-owned 
subsidiary of Applicant, in exchange for voting 
common stock issued by the Holding Company to the 
Insurance Company. As a result, the Insurance 
Company will have voting control of the Holding 
Company. 

As the second step in the Acquisition, the Holding 
Company will establish and incorporate Statesman 
Bank for Savings, FSB, a wholly-owned subsidiary 
federal savings bank (the “Institution”). The Holding 
Company will contribute all of the preferred stock 
issued by Applicant and all of the cash acquired from 
the Insurance Company to the Institution in exchange 
for all of the common stock of the Institution, thereby 
capitalizing the Institution in the amount of $21 
million. 

The third step of the Acquisition will be the 
purchase and assumption by the Institution of 
substantially all of the assets and liabilities of First 
Federated Savings Bank. The fourth step will be the 
conversion of each of the remaining three Associa- 
tions and its merger into the Institution. 

The Acquisition will be effected on a “turnkey” 
basis, with the FSLIC providing at its own cost for 
the establishment and incorporation of the Institution, 
transfer of assets and liabilities to the Institution, 
conversion and merger of the remaining Associations 
into the Institution, and recording and filing of all 
documents in accordance with various state and 
federal requirements. 

Applicant has submitted a business plan (the 
“Business Plan”), enclosed separately as Exhibit B, in 
connection with the Acquisition Proposal. The Busi- 
ness Plan assumes the acquisition is consummated on 
December 31, 1987, and presents financial projections 
for the Institution. The Business Plan projects a 


245 


capital infusion in cash and preferred stock by 
Applicant and/or its existing subsidiaries, initial 
financial assistance from FSLIC in the form of cash 
and an amortizing note receivable, and continuing 
financial assistance in the form of cash from FSLIC. 
This assistance consists of spread and yield mainte- 
nance and guarantees of recovery by FSLIC of losses 
on certain assets. The Business Plan assumes that 
losses on other assets will be assumed by the 
Institution. 

The Acquisition Proposal is subject to a number of 
specific terms and conditions, among which are 
certain regulatory forbearances and considerations to 
be granted or obtained by the FSLIC. These 
forbearances and considerations are listed at pages 
7-16 of the Acquisition Proposal which is attached as 
Exhibit A, and are hereby incorporated by reference 
into this Application. Applicant wishes to emphasize 
the importance of the requested forbearances and 
considerations to the completion of the Acquisition, 
and the consequent need for the appropriate offices 
within the FSLIC to review the relevant issues at an 
early stage. Without in any way affecting the 
requested forbearances and considerations as set 
forth in the Acquisition Proposal and incorporated by 
reference herein, a summary of the forbearances and 
considerations requested is given below: 

(a) For regulatory purposes with respect to the 
books and records of the Institution, push-down 
accounting shall be used to record the purchase of the 
Institution’s capital stock by the Holding Company. 
Twenty one million dollars of the cash contribution to 
be made to the Institution pursuant to an assistance 
agreement to be entered into between the FSLIC and 
Applicant is to be a credit to the Institution’s 


—_— al 


246 


regulatory capital; therefore, for regulatory account- 
ing purposes, the Institution will book such contribu- 
tion as a direct addition to its net worth. 

(b) The FSLIC will permit Applicant and the 
Holding Company to enter into a capital maintenance 
agreement for the Institution that is limited to: (i) an 
agreement to infuse capital in order to maintain the 
regulatory capital of the Institution at a level 
consistent with that required by 12 C.F.R. § 563.13(b) 
as now or hereinafter in effect, provided that the 
aggregate amount of this obligation shall not exceed 
$4 million; and (ii) an agreement that in the event the 
Institution’s tatio of regulatory capital to total 
liabilities at the end of any given calendar quarter 
falls below 2 percent, without prompt remedy, the 
FSLIC will have the right to vote the securities 
acquired by Applicant and the Holding Company, 
remove the Institution’s board of directors, and/or 
dispose of any or all of the voting securities of the 
Institution. 

(c) The FSLIC will give permanent and continuing 
approval to the issuance of debt by Applicant and its 
affiliates (or waive the debt requirement under 
section 408(m) of the National Housing Act), so long 
as the regulatory capital of the Institution meets the 
amount required at the time the debt is issued. 

(d) The FSLIC will authorize the acquisition under 
section 408(m) of the National Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. 
§ 1730a, will determine under such authority that 
Applicant and its affiliates are exempt from the 
operation of section 408(p) of the National Housing 
Act, 12 U.S.C. § 1730(p), with respect to the cross- 
marketing of products or services between the 
Institution and Applicant, its subsidiaries or its 
affiliates, and will confirm that such sales, as 
described herein, are consistent with section 408(d) of 
the National Housing Act. 


247 


(e) The acquisition will occur pursuant to FSLIC 
authorization under section 408(m) of the National 
Housing Act, 12 U.S.C. § 1730a, and the FSLIC will 
affirmatively find that such authorization preempts 
the laws of any state that would limit or preclude the 
affiliation of the Institution and Applicant or any of 
its insurance affiliates or would, by reason of such 
affiliation, prohibit the sale of insurance by Applicant, 
its insurance affiliates or agents. 

(f) The acquisition will occur pursuant to authoriza- 
tion under section 408(m) of the National Housing 
Act.

MEMORANDUM February 26, 1988 
Re: First—Peoples—Perpetual Page Two 


I. GENERAL RECOMMENDATION 


institutions’ financial condition and the acceptable 
acquisition proposal from Statesman, an FSLIC- 
assisted acquisition at this time will minimize the 


First Federal Savings Bank (“First Federal”) and 
Peoples Federal Savings and Loan Association 
(“Peoples”) are FSLIC-insured, federally chartered 
mutual institutions with home offices in Waterloo, 
lowa. The Perpetual Savings and Loan Association 
(“Perpetual”) is an FSLIC-insured, state chartered 
mutual institution with its home office also in 
Waterloo, Iowa. First Federal has one branch each 
in Oelwein, LaPorte City, Dubuque and Waterloo, 
and two branches in Cedar Falls. Peoples has one 
branch each in Manchester, New Hampton, 
Sumner, Traer, Union, Waterloo and Elkader, and 
two branches in Cedar Falls. Perpetual has one 
branch each in Parkersburg, Waterloo and Jesup, 
and two branches in Waverly. 


On December 11, 1987, these three institutions 
were bid nationwide on an assisted basis along 
with First Federated Savings Bank, North Palm 
Beach, Florida. One bid was received by the 
January 20, 1988, deadline, from the Statesman 
Group, Inc. (“Statesman”), Des Moines, Iowa. Their 
bid is less than the cost of liquidation of the three 
Waterloo institutions along with the North Palm 
Beach institution. | 


As reflected in current financial data of the three 
individual Waterloo institutions, each has a nega- 
tive net worth. First Federal has a continuing 
trend of monthly operating losses. Peoples and 
Perpetual have generated nominal monthly operat- 
ing income, resulting in an annualized return on 
assets of 0.1% for both institutions. Given the three 


eventual cost of resolution to the FSLIC. Accord- 
ingly, we recommend that the FHLBB approve the 
proposed F'S|.IC-assisted acquisition of First Fed- 
eral, Peoples and Perpetual by Statesman. 


The proposed transaction is expected to be accom- 
plished as follows: 


The Bank Board would allow a supervisory 
merger of the four institutions; approve the 
establishment and incorporation of Statesman 
Savings Holding Corp. and Statesman Bank of 
Savings, FSB; transfer the assets and lia- 
bilities of the combined institutions to States- 
man Bank of Savings, FSB; and permit 
Statesman Bank of Savings, FSB, to establish 
branches in the State of Missouri in addition to 
the branching rights associated with the 
acquisition of the Iowa and Florida institu- 
tions. The FSLIC would provide cash (30%) 
and promissory note (70%) contribution total- 
ing approximately $115,000,000; pay 93% of 
losses on covered assets for five years from 
the date of acquisition; provide yield mainte- 
nance on covered assets; indemnify Statesman, 
its subsidiaries, affiliates, officers, directors 
and employees for five yeas; and grant certain 
regulatory forbearances and considerations. 


Il. FINANCIAL STATUS OF PROBLEM 
INSTITUTIONS 


A. First Federal 


The institution’s most recent quarterly re- 
port dated December 31, 1987, indicated 


252 


average monthly net operating deficits of 
$666,667. Total regulatory net worth was a 
negative $44.4 million. 


As of December 31, 1987, it is estimated that 
the institution has unbooked losses of $11.3 
million. The majority of these losses, approx- 
imately $9.3 million, are the result of the 
institution’s investments in large commercial 
real estate loans. 


During late 1982, the prior Board and Presi- 
dent decided to have the institution “outgrow” 
the problem of its old, underwater loan 
portfolio. The institution failed to operate 
profitably while growing over 90% in 20 
months. The institution invested its proceeds 
from growth into commercial real estate loans 
on -a nationwide basis. These consisted pri- 
marily of construction and AD&C loans. Be- 
cause of the Board’s and President’s unsafe 
and unsound practices, they were replaced on 
November 5, 1987. 


Since the August 31, 1987, supervisory ex- 
amination, First Federal has been virtually 
unsuccessful in working out its problem assets. 
The new Board and President provide the 
institution with a good personnel base to 
successfully work out some of the problem 
loans and real estate owned.

B. Peoples 


The institution’s December 31, 1987, quarterly report 
indicated a deficit regulatory capital of $2.4 million. 
Although the institution reported nominal income of 
$96,000 for the three month period ended December 
31, 1987, losses, excluding a $4.3 million loan loss 
reserve in June 1987, have average $20,000 a month 
for the 12-month period ended December 31, 1987. An 
estimated 20 percent of assets are in delinquent local 
1-4 family mortgage loans, and local 1-4 family REO 
and REJ. Combining these assets with the continued 
poor condition of the local economy will preclude this 
institution from sustaining periods of profitability or 
returning to solvency in the near future. 


In addition, due to the continuing poor condition of 
the local economy, this institution may have unre- 
served losses on loans in excess of $2.5 million.
