D. Condition of Industry in Des Moines Bank 
District 

There are 188 insured institutions in the Eighth 
Federal Home Loan Bank Board (“FHLBB”) District. 


Assets of these insured institutions totaled $54.7 
billion as of December 31, 1987. 


As of December 31, 1987, it is projected that twenty 
(20) Eighth District insured institutions, representing 
assets of $4.1 billion or 7.5 percent of total Eighth 
District insured assets, will be insolvent within the 
next 12 months. 


Out of the 50 member institutions in Iowa, eight (8) 
are currently insolvent with $1.1 billion in assets, 
comprising 12.1 percent of total lowa member assets. 
The average net worth of Iowa institutions equaled 
1.8 percent of assets as of December 31, 1987. In 
addition, 30 Iowa member institutions reported net 
worth levels in excess of 3 percent as of December 31, 
1987. 


256 


Ill. SUPERVISORY HISTORY 
A. First Federal 


First Federal was incorporated in 1878 as a 
state chartered savings and loan association, 
became a bank member in 1934, converted to 
a federally chartered savings and loan associ- 
tion and received insurance of accounts in 
1953, and converted to a federally chartered 
savings bank in 1983. 


In 1981 and 1982 First Federal had operating 
losses of $1,654,002 and $3,108,302, respec- 
tively. The institution’s mismatch of asset and 
liability maturities during this rising interest 
rate period caused the bulk of these losses. 
Also, during this same time period, First 
Federal’s lending area became generally de- 
pressed with little sign of improvement. In 
order to reverse these operating losses and 
counteract the Waterloo economy, First Fed- 
eral undertook a total reorganization in 
September of 1982. The institution planned to 
grow from $140 million to $500 million in 
assets by December 31, 1987. The proposed 
growth strategy was to be achieved by 
incresing business thrust (e.g., financial inter- 
mediary, mortgage banking—multi-locations, 
real estate development, and expanding other 
services); diminishing the negative effect of 
old assets by acquiring new assets at a 
positive spread; buying mortgage banking 
servicing income streams; discounting GNMA 
or other favorable accounting strategies; 
using purchase method accounting where 
applicable as mergers occur; and buying out- 
of-state loans. 


257 


From September 1982 to June 1984 First 
Federal’s assets increased from $140 million 
to $238 million. A large percentage of this 
increase was initially funded through bor- 
rowed money and brokered deposits and 
invested in large loans purchased in the 
secondary market. The institution increased 
their senior management staff by one person 
in December of 1982 to accommodate this 
growth. The new senior manager specialized 
in large real estate loans. His goal, at the 
direction of his superiors, was to raise $1.4 
million in loan fees during 1983. 


On January 6, 1984. First Federal consum- 
mated an unassisted supervisory merger with 
Oelwein Federal Savings and Loan Associa- 
tion (“Oelwein Federal”), Oelwein, Lowa. This 
merger was approved by the FHLBB and the 
Supervisory Agent (“SA”). Oelwein Federal 
had $13 million in assets and approximately 
$100,000 in net worth as of the merger date. 
First Federal was the surviving institution. 


The December 9, 1983, supervisory examina- 
tion revealed a continued mismatch in asset/ 
liability maturities; but, more importantly, a 
lack of proper underwriting procedures (in- 
cluding inadequate appraisals) on loans pur- 
chased in the secondary market, and minimal 
analysis of loans and participations purchased. 
In addition, scheduled items increased signifi- 
cantly and it appeared that the Waterloo 
economy was worsening. 


The SA met with First Federal’s board of 
directors on March 2, 1984, to discuss the 
December 9, 1983, examination. It was noted 


258 


during the meeting that a follow up to this exam 
would begin during the coming weeks to 
determine if the institution has taken corrective 
actions regarding deficient lending practices. 


The March 14, 1984, supervisory examination 
noted that during the 14 months ended Febru- 
ary 29, 1984, $79 million of loans and participa- 
tions were purchased. This activity provided 
management with the loan fee income needed to 
meet its $1.4 million goal. During this time 
period, the institution had inadequate resources 
to devote to this lending activity. On June 18, 
1984, First Federal executed a Supervisory 
Agreement regarding loan underwriting at the 
request of the SA. 


On November 1, 1984, the SA met with First 
Federal’s board of directors to discuss the 
September 14, 1984, supervisory examination 
report. The SA was concerned with the institu- 
tion’s rapid growth and investments in commer- 
cial real estate, combined with a decreasing net 
worth ratio. The SA requested that the institu- 
tion revise its business plan to disallow the 
granting of any additional AD&C loans or other 
types of new commercial real estate construction 
loans. The SA also requested that the institution 
submit for his review a written detailed quar- 
terly status report for each loan cited in the 
examination report. Finally, the board of direc- 
tors were reminded of their fiduciary responsi- 
bilities. 


The high degree of risk First Federal assumed 
in the loans and participations purchased during 
1983 and 1984 took their toll on the institution’s 


259 


net worth following the March 2, 1985, super- 
visory examination. Continued severe operating 
deficiencies caused by excessive nonperforming 
assets and nearly $3.6 million in indicated loan 
losses more than depleted the $1.9 million net 
worth as of February 28, 1985. 


As a result of the March 2, 1985, examination 
report findings, the SA held a meeting with the 
institution’s board of directors on April 30, 1985. 
At that meeting the SA requested that the 
board execute a Consent Resolution. The board 
signed the Consent Resolution on May 22, 1985. 


First Federal was shopped in accordance with 
standard Federal Home Loan Bank Board pro- 
cedures in June of 1985. No institutions or 
investor groups were interested on an unassisted 
basis. A bid packet was sent to interested 
parties on September 13, 1985, requesting bid 
proposals to be submitted by October 7, 1985. A 
bid was submitted by First Financial Savings 
Bank (“First Financial”), Des Moines, lowa, and 
Midland Financial Savings and Loan Association 
(“Midland”), Des Moines, Iowa. Midland’s bid 
was unacceptable upon receipt and First Finan- 
cial’s bid was deemed unacceptable by FSLIC 
during June of 1986. Also, on June 17, 1986, First 
Financial withdrew its merger applications to 
acquire First Federal on an assisted basis. 


The February 3, 1986, supervisory examination 
indicated an asset portfolio heavily weighted 
with loans secured by major commercial real 
estate projects. These loans were secured by 
properties in Texas, Oklahoma, Colorado, Flor- 
ida, Nevada, Washington, Hawaii, Minnesota, 


260 


and lowa. On many of the participations, First 
Federal is a minor participant in the loan and, 
thus, has virtually no control over the asset. 
Possible losses on these major projects totaled 
$15 million. Sixty-eight percent of the $105.5 
million in large commercial real estate loans 
purchased or originated by First Federal were 
subject to adverse comment at this exam. In 
addition, the institution’s operating losses 
reached $1.0 million per month in 1986. The SA 
met with the institution’s board of directors on 
June 18, 1986, to discuss the February 3, 1986, 
examination. 


The SA met with the institution’s board of 
directors on May 1, 1987, to discuss its current 
financial condition. 


During the early part of 1987 the SA began 
interviewing individuals to replace the current 
board of directors and President at First 
Federal. On May 21, 1987, FSLIC indemnifica- 
tion was requested for five prospective directors 
and a prospective President to replace the 
current board and President at First Federal. 
An indemnification agreement was obtained from 
the FSLIC on September 21, 1987. The SA met 
with First Federal’s board of directors on 
November 5, 1987, and discussed with them the 
need to bring on new personnel as board 
members and President of the institution. During 
that meeting, all members of the board and the 
President resigned. The new board and Presi- 
dent were appointed at the same meeting. 


At the second meeting of the new board, 
November 19, 1987, the SA met with the 


261 


members to discuss the June 16, 1987, super- 
visory examination report. The new board was 
also presented with a new Consent Agreement 
for their review and execution. The new board 
signed the new Consent Agreement on January 
21, 1988.

B. Peoples 


Peoples was incorporated as a state chartered 
mutual savings and loan association on January 
25, 1879. The institution became a member of the 
Federal Home Loan Bank of Des Moines on 
March 1, 1956, and received insurance of accounts 


262 


on February 28, 1956. Peoples was a state 
chartered institution (Peoples Mutual Savings 
and Loan Association) until it merged with 
Peoples Federal Savings and Loan Associetion, 
Cedar Falls, lowa, and became a federal charter 
at that time. 


This institution received composite ratings of “2” 
and “3” up until the annual examination of 
December 1, 1984, at which time the institution 
received composite ratings of “4” and “3.” Prior 
to this, although ratings were not particularly 
adverse, examinations did note significant losses, 
resulting from inadequate net interest margins. 
Peoples suffered the normal post deregulatory 
spread problems, the result of fixed rate loans 
yielding well below the institution’s increasing 
cost of funds. In addition, this institution’s 
normal lending territory was increasingly becom- 
ing depressed due to agricultural difficulties, the 
result of which was an increasing amount of non- 
earning assets. All of these factors led up to a 
somewhat weak capital position, prior to Peoples’ 
more serious supervisory concerns. 


Peoples’ December 1, 1984, examination disclosed 
a composite rating of “4.” This rating was due 
primarily to a capital position which had been 
reduced to $1.7 million, losses of $967,000 for the 
period, and scheduled items which were noted to 
be $13.6 million or 8.1% of assets. The losses 
were due primarily to reserves for uncollected 
interest and appraised losses on nonearning 
assets. As a result of these findings, the SA 
requested to meet with this board to discuss 
methods of alleviating the above concerns. Items 
discussed by the SA were merger proposals, 


263 


possible capital infusion and branch sales. Cor- 
rective measures were properly addressed in 
response to the SA. 


Another meeting was held with the board of this 
institution on November 21, 1985. High sched- 
uled items, further losses, and inadequate 
spreads were discussed. The SA requested a net 
worth action plan at this meeting so as to 
document this institution’s plan for a return to 


capital adequacy. 


The December 31, 1985 examination disclosed 
continuing problems created by the weak econ- 
omy in the Waterloo area. Scheduled items had 
increased to 15.2% of assets and were comprised 
entirely of loans on 1-4 family type properties. 
Although net worth had increased slightly, it 
was due primarily to nominal profits which 
occurred due to softening interest rates. As a 
result of these findings, the SA advised the 
institution that a return to sustained periods of 
profitability and adequate net worth were not 
likely and the institution should immediately 
seek a merger partner or some sort of capital 
stock conversion to alleviate these concerns. This 
letter also discussed the institution’s net worth 
action plan requested earlier. The SA notified 
the institution that the plan was not adequate in 
that it did not display a return to capital 
adequacy. The board was instructed to continue 
working on the action plan and explore new 
areas of restructuring. 


The SA again requested to meet with this board 
in December of 1985. The meeting, held January 
27, 1986, checked the progress of the institution 


264 


in terms of net worth action plans, stock 
conversions, decreasing size, and branch sales. 
Many of these items did not occur because the 
institution was unable to effectuate any of the 
events in a proper manner. Although a net worth 
action plan was in place, little progress was 
noted toward capital improvement as the econ- 
omy was far too weak for improvement. 


The next course of supervisory action at this 
institution was a Supervisory Agreement, which 
was obtained on August 20, 1986, and was the 
result of continued losses and a_ projected 
insolvency of less than one year. The Agreement 
was standard and covered such items as lending, 
investment and other operating criteria. The 
institution has complied with this Agreement. 


In March of 1987, the SA met again with this 
board to discuss the institution’s business plan 
and progress thereto. We notified the board that 
the upcoming examination would likely result in 
the depletion of all of the institution’s net worth 
due to asset classifications. Previous to this, the 
institution had not been subject to classification. 
In addition, continued operating losses were 
being caused by a high level of nonearning 
assets. We also discussed a proposed merger and 
capital conversion plan organized by Peoples and 
Perpetual. A proposed investor was to inject 
several million dollars into the combined institu- 
tion as well as obtain several million dollars in 
FSLIC assistance. The Agreement did not occur 
as the feasibility of the combined institution was 
questionable. 


265 


The next item of supervisory concern at this 
institution was dated April 25, 1987, and con- 
cerned a memo from the examiners to the 
Principal Supervisory Agent. An examination 
which commenced March 23, 1987, would require 
Doubtful and Loss classifications of $8 million 
and, in turn, loss reserves of $4.2 million. This 
exhausted all of Peoples’ net worth. Total 
classified assets amounted to $30 million or 18.2 
percent of assets. The institution at this time 
was given a “5” rating. The exam noted that the 
institution’s primary problems were due to 
severe economic conditions and that management 
had failed to take aggressive steps to reduce the 
level of REO and problem assets. The exam also 
noted concerns with the institution’s level of 
interest rate risk, business plan, and a year end 
operating loss of $711,000. 


A supervisory letter sent May 28, 1987, re- 
quested corrective action with regard to all 
concerns as well as a loss reserve of $4.2 million. 
The reserve rendered the institution insolvent 
and, thus, a Consent Agreement dated June 17, 
1987, was obtained. The Consent Agreement 
prohibits the institution from any lending, except 
certain residential mortgage and consumer loans, 
and other operational matters without prior 
written approval of this office. 


The final supervisory matter concerning this 
institution was a special limited examination 
completed October 19, 1987. The examination 
was conducted to determine compliance with the 
Consent Agreement. It was reported that in 
several instances the institution was not in 
compliance with the Agreement; however, Su- 
pervision determined that the violations were 


266 


inadvertent and have not occurred subsequent to 
the examination. 


On December 23, 1987, Peoples submitted a 
capital forbearance application which will be 
denied due to the lack of sufficient earnings to 
restore capital to the minimums required by 
Section 563.13 within a reasonable period of 
time. In conjunction with the forbearance ap- 
plication, a proposed merger plan between 
Peoples and Perpetual was also submitted. The 
proposed merger would combine the two insol- 
vent institutions with the intention of achieving 
operating economies of scale. The proposal would 
not result in the resolution of the two FSLIC 
cases as does Statesman’s acquisition proposal. 


The following table provides information regard- 
ing Peoples’ examination report findings.

March 10, 1938, and received insurance of 
accounts on December 26, 1950.

to adversely impact the institution’s capital 
position. 

In October 1982, the institution decided to 
refinance a large amount of Federal Home Loan 
Bank advances, incurring prepayment penalties 
of approximately $2 million. The institution had 
incorrectly believed that such losses could be 
deferred and amortized over time. The subse- 
quent examination, as of December 27, 1982, 
disclosed concern over the impact of the con- 
tinued operating losses and prepayment penal-

C. Perpetual deterioration in asset quality with significant 


Perpetual was incorporated as a state chartered 
mutual savings and loan association on April 1, 
1890. The institution became a member of the 
Federal Home Loan Bank of Des Moines on 


increases in real estate owned and past due 
loans. As a result, an examination commenced 


October 27, 1984. Operating lossed continued to 
drain the institution’s net worth which, as of 


268 


September 20, 1984, represented 1.1 percent of 
total assets. Also of concern was the increasing 
level of scheduled items (3.9 percent of total 
assets) and a high level of interest rate risk. 
During the examination, the examiners dis- 
covered that the institution had funded a $1 
million purchase of second mortgage loans from 
First American Mortgage Company, Inc. 
(“FAM”), and issued a commitment to purchase 
an additional $9 million of this type of loan which 
was to be funded in November 1984. The 
underlying loans were not available for review at 
this examination; however, the review of a 
sample loan revealed a high degree of risk, with 
borrowers having poor credit histories and 
questionable repayment ability. The borrowers 
were charged annual percentage rates of up to 
30 percent. The loans were allegedly 100 percent 
insured against default by Rockwood Insurance 
Company, and the institution’s investment was 
dependent on the insurance rather than the 
individual borrowers. The institution’s composite 
rating at this examination was lowered to a “4.” 


On January 9, 1985, a meeting with the board of 
directors was held to discuss the results of the 
October 1984 examination and the quality of the 
FAM loans. 


An examination was performed on March 20, 
1985, to review the status of the FAM loans. It 
was determined that, as underwritten, the $10 
million in FAM loans represented a commercial 
loan, and an excessive concentration of credit to 
one borrower. The institution was requested to 
divest itself of the investment to within the level 
provided by the commercial loans-to-one- 


269 


borrower regulation. The composite rating re- 
mained a “4.” 


A meeting was held with representatives of 
FAM on June 11, 1985, to discuss the operations 
of FAM and the problems that the regulators 
had with the FAM loans. The lack of adequate 
documentation and the failure of the institution 
to include this type of loan in their underwriting 
guidelines were the two primary items of con- 
cern which led to the examiners’ classification of 
these loans as commercial loans to FAM. How- 
ever, the institution was notified on September 
10, 1985, that the loans could be considered real 
estate loans if the two criteria, previously 
mentioned, were met. Correspondence from the 
institution on September 13, 1985, advised this 
office that the board adopted a resolution to 
include this type of loan in its underwriting 
standards and they would pursue FAM to obtain 
needed documentation and delinquency reports. 


At the November 7, 1985, examination, the 
viability of the institution was questioned due to 
the low level of net worth and problems 
associated with the FAM loans. A law suit was 
filed against FAM by E.F. Hutton, a loan 
purchaser, alleging fraud and breach of contract 
in FAM’s loan servicing and misappropriation of 
funds by FAM and its chairman. Also-_of concern, 
regarding the collectibility of these loans, was 
the failure of the institution to have the 
individual mortgages and loss payee provisions 
of the surety bond assigned to them. A new 
servicer was hired and began reviewing the files 
to determine the delinquency status of the 
individual loans. Scheduled items, which con- 
tinued to increase due to the depressed local 


270 


economy of Waterloo, Iowa, represented 6 
percent of total assets at this review. 


A meeting with the board of directors was held 
on January 15, 1986, at the request of the 
institution, to discuss director liability and other 
issues of concern, which included the FAM loans 
and the November 1985 examination report. 
Lawsuits had been filed against FAM by 
individual borrowers in two states seeking relief 
due to unconscionable interest and points. The 
institution’s eligibility for a supervisory conver- 
sion to stock was also discussed. 


The May 13, 1986, examination report disclosed 
further deterioration in the condition of the 
institution. The delinquency report on the FAM 
loans showed 47 percent of the loans were past 
due 90 days or more, and a loss of 
$875,000-$1,283,000 was estimated by the new 
servicer. Litigation against FAM continued in 
Alabama and South Carolina with potential 
losses on loans in these states of 100 percent and 
40 percent, respectively. The institution’s com- 
posite rating was lowered to “5” at this 
examination. 


The board of directors signed a Consent Agree- 
ment on July 16, 1986, due to the anticipated 
insolvency of the institution. 


A proposal for a merger between Perpetual and 
Peoples was submitted to this office for review 
in October 1986. The proposal stated that an 
investor would infuse up to $9 million of fresh 
capital, if the FSLIC would infuse $13 million. 
The proposal was rejected because 1) it repre- 
sented the merger of two weak institutions 


271 


which was against FSLIC policy, 2) the investor 
never committed the funds, and 3) an extensive 
shopping procedure would have to precede such 
a plan. 


An examination performed as of October 20, 
1986, disclosed an insolvent institution with 
serious earnings problems and a high level of 
scheduled items (11.9 percent of total assets). 
The institution incurred a net loss of $2,281,000 
for fiscal year end September 1986. The majority 
of the loss, $1,594,000, was due to specific loss 
reserves established for FAM loans. Concern 
over management and the board’s willingness 
and ability to comply with the Consent Agree- 
ment was also noted, as several deficiencies were 
disclosed during the examination. The institu- 
tion’s composite rating remained a “5.” 


The case was transferred to the FSLIC on 
February 26, 1987. 


A meeting with the board of directors was held 
on March 18, 1987, to discuss the October 1986 
examination and the status of the FAM loans. 
The institution had in excess of $7.8 million of 
FAM loans outstanding, with loss reserves of 
$1.5 million recorded and additional reserves of 
$1.2 million to be made. 


On December 23, 1987, Perpetual submitted a 
capital forbearance application which was subse- 
quently denied due to the lack of sufficient 
earnings to restore capital to the minimum level 
required by Section 563.13 within a reasonable 
period of time. In conjunction with the for- 
bearance application, a proposed merger plan 


272 273

VILL. 


274 
SERVICE CORPORATION ISSUES 


There are no significant service corporation 
issues at Peoples related to this transaction. 


One of First Federal’s second tier service 
corporation, Combined Mortgage, Phoenix, Ari- 
zona, is jointly owned with Occidental/Nebraska 
Federal Savings Bank, Omaha, Nebraska. There 
are no other significant service corporation 
issues at First Federal related to this transac- 
tion. One of Perpetual’s three services corpora- 
tions, Perpetual Services Corporation, engages 
in real estate sales. This activity, which is 
permitted under the Iowa regulations governing 
states chartered institutions, is not permitted 
under Federal Regulations (Section 545.74). If 
the resulting institution is federally chartered, 
this issue will need to be addressed. 


FIDELITY BOND/D&O POLICY 


First Federal’s fidelity bond and D&O coverage 
was canceled on August 20, 1985. A discovery 
bond was purchased at that time for a three 
month period. The institution has not been able 
to obtain a fidelity bond or D&O coverage since 
the cancellation. 


Peoples’ fidelity bond expired November 22, 
1987, and the institution has been unable to 
obtain coverage elsewhere. 


275 


Perpetual’s surety bond expired in April 25, 
1987, and the institution has been unable to 
obtain coverage elsewhere.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 


ISSUE 


Whether the Board should approve the FSLIC- 
assisted acquisitions of First Federal SB, Waterloo, 
[A (“First”), Peoples FS&LA, Waterloo, IA (“Peo- 
ples”), Perpetual S&LA, Waterloo, IA (“Perpetual”), 
and First Federated, North Palm Beach, FL (“Fede- 
rated”) by the Statesman Group, Inc. (“Statesman”). 


278 


BACKGROUND 


First attempted to grow out of it’s early 1980’s loan 
difficulties by engaging in a policy of nationwide 
commercial lending. The association grew over 90 
percent in 20 months, but the operations of First 
remained unprofitable. The association is currently 
carrying on the books numerous nonperforming 
commercial and acquisition, development and con- 
struction loans. 


Peoples’ difficulties are attributable to the high level 
of delinquent 1-4 family mortgages, real estate owned, 
and real estate in judgment. The economy in the 
Waterloo area is weak resulting in a high level of 
unemployment and a decrease in housing values. In 
addition, unreserved loan losses currently exceed $2.5 
million. 


Perpetual has agreed to establish a loan loss reserve 
of $2.7 million in connection with problem secondary 
mortgage loans purchased from the now defunct First 
American Mortgage Company. Furthermore, the 
association is located in a depressed area and 
scheduled items are quite high. 


Federated’s problems stem primarily from the man- 
ner in which the mortgage banking subsidiary was 
operated. The subsidiary’s mortgage servicing port- 
folio was comprised largely of servicing rights on high 
coupon loans purchased at significant premiums. The 
prepayment of the underlying loans has resulted in 
significant writedowns of the abovementioned pre- 
miums. The association has also experienced manage- 
ment and lending difficulties. 


279 


TRANSACTION 


The Statesman Group, Inc. (“Statesman”) will issue 
$12.6 million in preferred stock to Statesman Savings 
Holding Company (“SSHC”) which will be used as 
partial capitalization of SSHC. The other portion of 
the capitalization of SSHC will be cash of $8.4 million 
from American Life and Casualty Company in 
exchange for common stock. SSHC will in turn issue 
$12.6 million in preferred stock to the Statesman and 
pass $8.4 million in cash, $12.6 million in preferred 
stock, and $5 million in subordinated debentures 
through to Statesman Bank for Savings, SB (“SBS”). 
SBS will then purchase substantially all the assets 
and liabilities of First, Peoples, Perpetual, and 
Federated. 


FORBEARANCES 


It is proposed that the following forbearances be 
granted in connection with the acquisition: 


1. Where scheduled items are referred to in the 
regulation, assets of First, Peoples, Perpetual, and 
Federated that are scheduled items or become 
classified assets shall be excluded for a period of 
five years following the acquisition from any 
computation which requires the inclusion of such 
assets. 


2. For a period not to exceed five years following the 
acquisition, FSLIC will not exercise its authority 
under Section 563.13 for a failure to meet the 
regulatory capital requirements due to the ac- 
quired scheduled items. 


280 


. For one year following the acquisition, liquidity 
penalties will not be assessed if the deficiency 
results from a liquidity deficiency existing at the 
time of the acquisition or from aggregate net 
withdrawals from the existing offices of First, 
Peoples, Perpetual, or Federated. 


. It is the FSLIC’s intention that $21 million of the 
cash contribution to be made to SBS pursuant to 
an assistance agreement to be entered into 
between the FSLIC and SBS is to be a credit to 
SBS regulatory capital; therefore, for regulatory 
accounting purposes, SBS may book such contribu- 
tion as a direct addition to its regulatory capital. 


- SBS shall be given a reasonable period of time, but 
not to exceed 3 years, to phase out, dispose of or 
discontinue in an orderly manner, any acquired 
investments, activities, or operations inconsistent 
with the Home Owners’ Loan Act of 1933, as 
amended. 


. SBS shall not for a period not to exceed 3 years, 
be deemed in violation of any of the provisions of 
Section 545.74 (Service Corporations) for invest- 
ments acquired as part of the merger. 


. The Bank Board shall forbear from taking action 
due to SBS’ violations of the holding company 
regulations regarding the acceptance and holding 
of specified securities of its parent holding com- 
pany. In addition, the Bank Board will forbear 
from taking any supervisory/enforcement action 
due to the institution’s violation of the equity risk 
investment limitations due to its acceptance and 
holding of the specified securities of its parent 
holding company. 


281 


AFFILIATED TRANSACTIONS 


The applicant has requested waivers for numerous 
affiliated transactions such as, the servicing of SBS’ 
loans through Statesman’s mortgage subsidiary, 
providing adjustable rate loans and a line of credit to 
the mortgage subsidiary, and allowing Statesman to 
borrow from the institution and provide management 
services to the same. The applicant has requested 
that SBS be allowed to purchase GNMA and other 
securities from Statesman’s mortgage company. 


To allow for such affiliated transactions this Office 
recommends several restrictions and guidelines: 


1. Statesman and SBS must maintain documentation 
to justify the affiliated transactions and ensure that 
they are fair to and in the best interest of the 
institution. 


2. The PSA may revoke any approved affiliated 
transactions that are judged not to be fair or in the 
best interest of the institution. 


3. Affiliated transactions are to be limited to existing 
lines of business. 


4. The institution shall submit in advance an annual 
report to the PSA indicating the actual types and 
volumes of affiliated transactions. 


CROSS-MARKETING 


Statesman has requested the ability to market a 
variety of insurance products through the institution’s 
branches and to utilize Statesman’s and SBS’ 
customer bases for direct and personal solicitation. In 
addition, the applicant proposes to set up brokerage 
services within the branches and utilize the broker to 
trade for SBS’ account. 


Oe ee, ete 6 te, Ditee Ie,» sr 2h 


282 


ORPOS does not object to the cross-marketing as 
long as the purchasers of the insurance products are 
made aware that there is no link between the 
insurance products and FSLIC insurance. Further- 
more, the products to be marketed will be restricted 
to existing products being offered by Statesman 
unless approved by the PSA. 


DEBT APPROVAL 


Pursuant to be applicant attaining non-diversified 
status, there will be a Proposed Debt Budget filed 
with the PSA by the applicant to cover any 
contemplated debt for a given fiscal year. 


LOANS 


Loans aggregating $30 million, a $20 million loan and 
a $10 million loan to the Employee Stock Ownership 
Plan which is guaranteed by Statesman, currently in 
affect with Bank One, can be place on SBS’ books 
with the same rates, terms, restrictive covenants, and 
collateralization. A $17 million loan will also be 
allowed to cover payments to the Government 
National Mortgage Association for three months. At 
the end of the three months that loan will be reduced 
to $15 million for a period not to exceed 12 months in 
total duration. 


RECOMMENDATIONS 


The Supervisory Agent’s recommendation as well as a 
more detailed history of the disappearing institutions 
is contained in the attached memorandum. This Office 
has no objection to the Supervisory Agent’s recom- 
mendation for approval.

Housing Act, as amended (“NHA”), 12 U.S.C. 
$ 1729%a), (b)(1)(A), (c)(1)(B) and (e)(3)(B) (1982). 

2. It is deemed to be in the best interests of the 
members of the MERGING ASSOCIATION and the 
sole stockholder of SBS that such institutions effect a 
merger upon the terms and conditions stated in this 
Agreement (“Merger”). 

3. It is believed that the Merger will enable SBS to 
establish and maintain savings, mortgage and other 
services. 

4. The Merger is authorized by $5 of the Home 
Owners’ Loan Act of 1933, as amended (“HOLA”), 12 
U.S.C. § 1464 (1982), and the rules and regulations 
promulgated under and pursuant to the HOLA and 
the NHA by the FEDERAL HOME LOAN BANK 
BOARD (“BANK BOARD”).

consideration of the mutual promises contained in this 
Agreement.

same were possessed, held and enjoyed by the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION prior to the Merger. The 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall be deemed to be 
and shall be a continuation of the entity and identity 
of SBS. All of the rights and obligations of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION shall remain unimpaired, 
and the RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall, on the 
Effective Date, succeed to all of such rights and 
obligations and the duties connected with them. 

§ 11 Stock. Upon the Effective Date, each share of 
SBS capital stock outstanding immediately prior to 


295 


such date shall remain outstanding as a share of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION’s stock. Upon the 
Effective Date, all voting rights of borrowers or 
accountholders of the MERGING ASSOCIATION 
shall cease, and all voting rights in the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION shall be vested in and exercisable by 
the sole stockholder of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION. No stock of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
shall be received or distributed in connection with the 
Merger. 

$12 Effective Date of Merger. The transactions 
contemplated by this Agreement shall be effective 
only when approved by the BANK BOARD. The 
Effective Date of the Merger shall be a date 
determined by the BANK BOARD in accordance with 
its resolution approving the Merger. 

$13 Further Actions. All of the transactions 
contemplated by this Agreement have been or will be 
authorized by all necessary corporate action of each 
institution. Both institutions by their proper officers 
shall execute and deliver all instruments, certificates 
and other documents as may be necessary or 
incidental to the performance of this Agreement. 

§$ 14 Condition of the MERGING ASSOCIATION. 
The RESULTING ASSOCIATION acknowledges 
that it is familiar with the present financial condition 
of the MERGING ASSOCIATION and of the deterio- 
ration of the assets, business and operations of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION. 

$ 15 Counterparts, Modifications, Headings. 

(a) This Agreement may be executed in any 
number of countervarts, all of which taken together 
shall constitute one and the same instrument, and any 
party may execute this Agreement by signing any 
such counterpart.

MERGER AGREEMENT 
AND 
PLAN OF MERGER 


THIS MERGER AGREEMENT is made and 
entered into as of the 11th day of March, 1988, by and 
between THE PERPETUAL SAVINGS AND LOAN 
ASSOCIATION, Waterloo, lowa (“MERGING ASSO- 
CIATION”), and STATESMAN BANK FOR SAV- 
INGS, FSB, Des Moines, Iowa (“SBS” or “RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION”). 


RECITALS 


1. SBS has been organized and chartered as a 
federal stock savings bank pursuant to § 406(a), 
(b) 1A), (c)1)(B) and (c)(3)(B) of the National 
Housing Act, as amended (“NHA”), 12 U.S.C. 
§ 1729(a), (b)(1)(A), (e)(1)(B) and (c)(3)(B) (1982). 

2. It is deemed to be in the best interests of the 
members of the MERGING ASSOCIATION and the 
sole stockholder of SBS that such institutions effect a 
merger upon the terms and conditions stated in this 
Agreement (“Merger”). 

3. It is believed that the Merger will enable SBS to 
establish and maintain savings, mortgage and other 
services. 

4. The Merger is authorized by § 5 of the Home 
Owners’ Loan Act of 1933, as amended (“HOLA”), 12 
U.S.C. § 1464 (1982), and the rules and regulations 
promulgated under and pursuant to the HOLA and 
the NHA by the FEDERAL HOME LOAN BANK 
BOARD (“BANK BOARD”). 

5. The following agreement is entered into in 
consideration of the mutual promises contained in this 
Agreement. 


299 


AGREEMENT 


§ 1 Merger. The MERGING ASSOCIATION shall 
merge with and into SBS. 

§ 2 RESULTING ASSOCIATION. The RESULT- 
ING ASSOCIATION shall be STATESMAN BANK 
FOR SAVINGS, FSB, Des Moines, Iowa. 

§3 Name of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 
The name of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall 
be STATESMAN BANK FOR SAVINGS, FSB. 

$4 Home Office of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION. The home office of the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION shall be located at Fourth and Mulberry, 
Waterloo, Iowa. The present home office of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION shall become a branch 
office of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 

§5 Charter and Bylaws of the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION. After the Merger, the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION shall operate under the charter and 
bylaws of SBS. 

§ 6 Directors of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 
The number of directors of the RESULTING ASSO- 
CIATION shall be 7. The names and addresses of 
those persons who shall serve as directors of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION and the expiration 
dates of their terms are listed on Exhibit A actached 
hereto, which constitutes a part of this Agreement. 

§ 7 Officers. The existing officers of SBS shall be 
the officers of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 

§ 8 Branch Offices of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION. After the Merger, the branch offices of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall include the pres- 
ent home and branch offices of the MERGING 
ASSOCIATION. The addresses of the offices that will 
be branch offices of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION are listed on Exhibit B attached hereto, which 
constitutes a part of this Agreement. 


300 


$9 Accounts. Upon the effective date of the 
Merger (“Effective Date”), each accountholder of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION shall receive, without 
payment, a withdrawable account or accounts in the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION equal in withdrawal 
value to the account or accounts held in the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION on such date, featuring 
the same rate, maturity and other terms, except that 
holders of such accounts shall not be members of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION, shall have no voting 
rights in the RESULTING ASSOCIATION and shall 
have no rights to participate in the equity of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION upon liquidation or 
otherwise. 

$10 Transfer of Assets and Liabilities upon 
Merger. Upon the Effective Date, all of the assets and 
property of every kind or character, real, personal 
and mixed, tangible and intangible, choses in action, 
rights and credits, then owned by the MERGING 
ASSOCIATION, or that would inure to it, shall 
immediately by operation of law and without the 
necessity of any instrument of conveyance or transfer 
and without any further act or deed be vested in and 
become the property of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION, which shall have, hold, and enjoy the same in 
its own right as fully and to the same extent as the 
same were possessed, held and enjoyed by the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION prior to the Merger. The 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall be deemed to be 
and shall be a continuation of the entity and identity 
of SBS. All of the rights and obligations of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION shall remain unimpaired, 
and the RESULTING ASSOCIATION shall, on the 
Effective Date, succeed to all of such rights and 
obligations and the duties connected with them. 

§ 11 Stock. Upon the Effective Date, each share of 
SBS capital stock outstanding immediately prior to 


301 


such date shall remain outstanding as a share of the 
RESULTING ASSOCIATION’s stock. Upon the 
Effective Date, all voting rights of borrowers or 
accountholders of the MERGING ASSOCIATION 
shall cease, and all voting rights in the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION shall be vested in and exercisable by 
the sole stockholder of the RESULTING ASSOCIA- 
TION. No stock of the RESULTING ASSOCIATION 
shall be received or distributed in connection with the 
Merger. 

$12 Effective Date of Merger. The transactions 
contemplated by this Agreement shall be effective 
only when approved by the BANK BOARD and the 
Superintendent of Savings and Loans, State of Iowa. 
The Effective Date of the Merger shall be a date 
determined by the BANK BOARD in accordance with 
its resolution approving the Merger. 

$13 Further Actions. All of the transactions 
contemplated by this Agreement have been or will be 
authorized by all necessary corporate action of each 
institution. Both institutions by their proper officers 
shall execute and deliver all instruments, certificates 
and other documents as may be necessary or 
incidental to the performance of this Agreement. 

§ 14 Condition of the MERGING ASSOCIATION. 
The RESULTING ASSOCIATION acknowledges 
that it is familiar with the present financial condition 
of the MERGING ASSOCIATION and of the deterio- 
ration of the assets, business and operations of the 
MERGING ASSOCIATION. 

$15 Counterparts, Modifications, Headings. 

(a) This Agreement may be executed in any 
number of counterparts, all of which taken together 
shall constitute one and the same instrument, and any 
party may execute this Agreement by signing any 
such counterpart. 


302 


(b) No amendment or modification of this Agree- 
ment shall be binding unless executed in writing by 
the parties or their successors. 

(c) Section headings are not to be considered part 
of this Agreement, are solely for convenience of 
reference, and shall not affect the meaning or 
interpretation of this Agreement or any of its 
provisions. 

§$ 16 Entire Agreement, Severability. 

(a) This Agreement, together with any inter- 
pretation or understanding agreed to in writing by 
the parties, constitutes the entire agreement between 
the parties pertaining to the subject matter hereof 
and supersedes all prior agreements and understand- 
ings of the parties in connection with it. 

(b) If any provision of this Agreement is invalid 
or unenforceable, all of the remaining provisions of 
this Agreement shall remain in full force and effect 
and shall be binding upon the MERGING ASSOCIA- 
TION and the RESULTING ASSOCIATION. 

$17 Governing Law. This Agreement and the 
rights and obligations under it shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Iowa to the extent that 
federal law does not control. Nothing in this Agree- 
ment shall require any unlawful action or inaction by 
either party.

in this Agreement is intended or shall be construed to 
give any person other than the RESULTING 
ASSOCIATION or the MERGING ASSOCIATION 
any legal or equitable right, remedy, or claim under, 
or in respect to, this Agreement, it being the 
intention of the parties that this Agreement, the 
assumption of obligations and statements of respon- 
sibilities under it, and all of its conditions and

provisions are for the sole benefit of the RESULT-

TION and for the benefit of no other person. 

$19 Acknowledgment. Each party to this Agree- 
ment, by the execution of this Agreement, acknowi- 
edges and affirms that its board of directors has, by 
at least a two-thirds vote, approved this Agreement 
and the Merger, authorized the execution of this 
Agreement, empowered its signatories to execute this 
Agreement, and authorized the filing of this Agree-

$20 Conditions. This Agreement is conditioned 
upon the execution of an Assistance Agreement 
among the Federal Savings and Loan Insurance 
Corporation, the RESULTING ASSOCIATION, The

ACQUIs.TION AGREEMENT 


THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this 
llth day of March, 1988, by and between the 
FEDERAL SAVINGS AND LOAN INSURANCE 
CORPORATION, in its capacity as Receiver for 
FIRST FEDERATED SAVINGS BANK, West Palm 
Beach, Florida (respectively, “RECEIVER” and 
“CLOSED ASSOCIATION”), and STATESMAN 
BANK FOR SAVINGS, FSB, Waterloo, Iowa (“AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION”). 


RECITALS 


A. The CLOSED ASSOCIATION is a stock savings 
bank organized under the laws of the State of Florida, 
the accounts of which are insured by the FEDERAL 
SAVINGS AND LOAN INSURANCE CORPORA- 
TION in its capacity as a corporate instrumentality of 
the United States (“CORPORATION”). 

B. The FEDERAL HOME LOAN BANK BOARD 
(“BANK BOARD”), by its Resolution No. 88, dated 
March 11, 1988, duly appointed the CORPORATION 
as RECEIVER for the CLOSED ASSOCIATION, 
and the RECEIVER’s appointment has become 
effective. 

C. The RECEIVER has taken possession of the 
CLOSED ASSOCIATION and by operation of law 
has succeeded to all the rights, titles, powers, and 
privileges of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION. 

D. The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION is a stock 
savings bank organized under the laws of the United 
States, the accounts of which are insured by the 
CORPORATION. 

E. It has been mutually agreed between the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION and the RECEIVER, 


307 


and the BANK BOARD has determined that it is in 
the best interests of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION, 
its savers, and the CORPORATION, that substan- 
tially all of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s assets, all 
of its deposits or withdrawable accounts, and substan- 
tially all of its other liabilities be immediately 
transferred to and assumed by the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION, as provided in this Agreement. 

F. In consideration of the foregoing and of the 
mutual covenants and agreements contained herein, 
the RECEIVER and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION enter into the following agreement. 


AGREEMENT 


$1 Purchase of Assets. The RECEIVER hereby 
sells to the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, and the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION hereby purchases from 
the RECEIVER, all of the RECEIVER’s right, title, 
and interest in and to all of the CLOSED ASSOCIA- 
TION’s assets that the RECEIVER owns or holds 
and any of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s assets 
hereafter acquired by the RECEIVER, excluding, 
however: (a) any known or unknown claim, demand, 
cause of action, or judgment (i) against the CLOSED 
ASSOCIATION’s or any of its subsidiaries’ present 
or former employees, agents, or controlling persons, 
directors, officers, or other persons, directly or 
indirectly exercising a controlling influence over the 
management or policies of the CLOSED ASSOCIA- 
TION or any of its subsidiaries, (ii) against any 
stockholder of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION, (iii) 
against any appraiser, accountant, auditor, attorney, 
investment banker or broker, loan broker, deposit 
broker, securities dealer, or other professional indi- 
vidual or entity performing services for the CLOSED 


308 


ASSOCIATION, and (iv) against any administrator, 
executor, personal representative, heir, assign, direc- 
tor or officer of any person or entity identified in 
$ l(a)i), (ii) or (iii), arising out of any act(s) or 
omission(s) of such person or entity prior to the 
effective date with respect to the CLOSED ASSO- 
CIATION or its property, or any of its subsidiaries or 
the subsidiaries’ property; (b) any known or unknown 
claim, demand, cause of action or judgment against a 
surety for any person or entity identified in § 1(a)(i), 
(ii), (iii) or (iv), including any insurer who may be 
liable for indemnification for the loss caused by the 
action or inaction of any such person or entity; (¢) any 
claim against counsel for the CLOSED ASSOCIA- 
TION for recovery of sums paid by the CLOSED 
ASSOCIATION to challenge the receivership or 
contemporary or subsequent actions of the BANK 
BOARD or the CORPORATION; and (d) such limited 
items, parts, or portions of the CLOSED ASSOCIA- 
TIQN’s books and records as are specifically identi- 
fied by the RECEIVER, provided that the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION consents to the RECEIVER’s 
retention of such records (the “excluded papers”). 
$2 Assumption of Liabilities. The ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION hereby expressly assumes and 
agrees to pay, perform, and discharge all of the 
CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s liabilities, including, but 
not limited to, those shown on the CLOSED 
ASSOCIATION’s books and records at such time as 
the RECEIVER takes possession of the CLOSED 
ASSOCIATION, but excluding, however, obligations 
under any compensation or employment contract 
between the CLOSED ASSOCIATION and any of its 
employees, officers, or directors, other than obliga- 
tions to honor or satisfy and right already vested 
under such contracts. The term “liability” as used in 


309 


this section does not refer to any obligation of the 
CLOSED ASSOCIATION to its stockholders arising 
out of or related in any way to their stock holdings 
(including claims for damages or rescission arising 
from the purchase or sale of such stock holdings) or 
any obligation to indemnify controlling persons, 
directors, officers or other persons as a result of suits 
arising from claims described in $1, and the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION specifically does not 
assume by virtue of this Agreement any such 
obligations to the stockholders or for indemnification 
of the controlling persons, directors, officers or other 
persons. For the purposes of this Agreement, “stock- 
holder” means a holder of stock; and “stock” includes 
a nonwithdrawable account, as defined by 12 C.F.R. 
§ 561.42 (1987), stock, permanent stock, guarantee 
stock, common stock, preferred stock, and subordi- 
nated debt issued by the CLOSED ASSOCIATION. 
$3 Records. 

(a) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION acknowl- 
edges that the RECEIVER is delivering to it, among 
the assets sold and purchased under $1 of this 
Agreement, all of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s 
books and records, except the excluded papers, if any. 

(b) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION agrees to 
preserve and keep safe all of the CLOSED ASSO- 
CIATION’s books and records that it acquires 
pursuant to this Agreement, to permit the RE- 
CEIVER at any reasonable time to inspect, make 
extracts from or copies of any of such books and 
records, and to cooperate fully with the RECEIVER 
in the preparation of the inventory described below. 

(c) As a condition of this Agreement, the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION agrees, at its own 
expense, either to segregate and keep separate or, in 
the alternative and at the option of the ACQUIRING 


310 


ASSOCIATION, to reconstruct, as of the date of this 
Agreement, the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s books 
and records transferred to it until the period in which 
the CLOSED ASSOCIATION may file an action for 
removal of the RECEIVER pursuant to law has 
expired, or, if such action is filed, until the dismissal 
of such action or the issuance of an order determining 
an appeal from any order of a court directing the 
removal of the RECEIVER. 

$4 Inventory. As soon as is practicable after the 
effective date of this Agreement, the RECEIVER 
shall make, or cause to be made, an inventory of the 
CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s assets and a record of its 
liabilities, as directed by the BANK BOARD. The 
RECEIVER shall furnish a copy of the inventory of 
the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s assets and record of 
its liabilities to the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
upon their completion. 

§ 5 Purchase Price. The purchase price of all assets 
sold to the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION pursuant to 
§ 1 shall be the assumption of liabilities provided for 
in § 2. 

$6 Duties With Respect to Savers. 

(a) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION agrees to 
pay, in accordance with the provisions of applicable 
law and the individual contracts governing the 
CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s deposits or withdrawable 
accounts, all properly drawn and presented with- 
drawal requests by the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s 
depositors or withdrawable accountholders whose 
deposits or accounts are assumed by the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION pursuant to § 2 of this Agree- 
ment, to the extent that such deposits or withdraw- 
able account balances are sufficient to permit such 
payments and in compliance with the contractual 
terms of such deposits and accounts, and in all other 


311 


respects to discharge, in the usual course of business, 
the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s duties and obliga- 
tions with respect to its depositors or accountholders, 
provided, however, that the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION does not assume any special or unusual duties 
of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION to such depositors 
or accountholders unless the terms of such duties are 
disclosed in the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s records. 
Further, in accordance with applicable law and the 
individual contracts governing such deposits or 
accounts, the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION agrees 
to pay interest on all of the CLOSED ASSOCIA- 
TION’s deposits and withdrawable accounts that are 
assumed pursuant to § 2 of this Agreement. 

(b) If any such depositors or withdrawable 
accountholders decline to accept the obligation of the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION to pay the CLOSED 
ASSOCIATION’s deposit or account liabilities as- 
sumed pursuant to § 2 of this Agreement and asserts 
a claim against the RECEIVER for any part of such 
assumed deposit liabilities, the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION shall provide the RECEIVER, upon 
demand, with an amount of money sufficient to enable 
it to pay the claims of such depositors or ac- 
countholders, less any applicable early withdrawal 
penalties, not exceeding the amount credited to such 
persons on the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION’s rec- 
ords at the time such demand is made, and, upon 
paying the RECEIVER as so demanded, the AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION shall be discharged from 
any further liability for such claim under this 
Agreement. 

(c) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall given 
written notice, in a form approved by the RE- 
CEIVER or its counsel, to the CLOSED ASSOCIA- 
TION’s depositors and withdrawable accountholders 


312 


whose deposits or accounts are assumed by the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION pursuant to § 2 of this 
Agreement of its assumption of liability for such 
deposits or accounts. 

§ 7 Office Space for the RECEIVER. The AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION agrees to provide the 
RECEIVER, without charge and, if its duties so 
require, for a period of one year from the date of this 
Agreement, adequate space, including vault space and 
furnishings, for the RECEIVER’s use in connection 
with the transfers and transactions required by this 
Agreement and with the exercise and discharge of its 
powers and duties as receiver of the CLOSED 
ASSOCIATION. After such period, the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION shall continue to provide to the 
RECEIVER, at a reasonable rental to be agreed 
upon by the parties, such space and furnishings for 
such period as the RECEIVER may request. 

§ 8 Litigation; Power of Attorney. In the event that 
any action at law or in equity in which the 
RECEIVER has an interest is instituted by any 
person against the CLOSED ASSOCIATION, the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, or both of them, or 
against the RECEIVER and either or both of the 
foregoing as co-defendants, or in which action the 
RECEIVER joins or is joined as co-defendant, the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION agrees to file, or to 
join with the RECEIVER in filing, a petition to 
remove the action to an appropriate court, and hereby 
authorizes and appoints, as its attorney for the 
purpose of effecting such removal, any attorney 
designated or approved by the RECEIVER to act in 
that capacity. 

$9 Rights and Forbearance. The rights, powers, 
and remedies given to the parties by this Agreement 
shall be in addition to all rights, powers, and remedies 


313 


given by any applicable statute or rule of law. Any 
forbearance, failure, or delay by either party in 
exercising or partially exercising any right, power, or 
remedy shall not preclude the further exercise of such 
right, power, or remedy. 

§$ 10 Sole Benefit. It is the intention of the parties 
that this Agreement, the assumption of obligations 
and statements of responsibilities under it, and all 
conditions and provisions of it, are for the sole benefit 
of the RECEIVER and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION and for the benefit of no other person. Nothing 
expressed or referred to in this Agreement is 
intended to or shall be construed to give any person 
other than the RECEIVER or the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION any legal or equitable right, remedy, 
or claim under or with respect to this Agreement or 
any of its provisions. 

$11 Swecessors and Assigns. All the terms and 
provisions of this Agreement shall be binding upon 
and inure to the benefit of the parties, their 
respective transferees, successors, and assigns, but 
this Agreement may not be assigned nor may any 
rights under it be transferred to or vested in any 
other party, through merger, consolidation, or other- 
wise, without the prior written consent of the 
CORPORATION. 

§ 12 Notices. Any notice, request, demand, or other 
communication to either of the parties shall be 
deemed given when received and shall be given in 
writing and delivered in person or sent by first class 
mail in a prepaid envelope to such party at its 
address set forth below or at such other address as 
such party shall hereafter furnish in writing:

§ 13 Accounting Principles. Except as otherwise 
provided in this Agreement, any computations made 
for the purposes of this Agreement shall be governed 
by generally accepted accounting principles as applied 
in the savings and loan industry, except that where 
such principles conflict with the terms of this 
Agreement, any assistance agreement or agreements 
to which the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION and the 
CORPORATION are parties, including, but not 
limited to, an agreement to make a contribution or 
loan, an agreement to purchase subordinated debt, or 
an agreement to issue and purchase Income Capital 
Certificates, applicable regulations of the BANK 
BOARD or the CORPORATION, or any resolution or 
action of the BANK BOARD or CORPORATION 
approving, or adopted concurrently with, this Agree- 
ment, then this Agreement, such assistance agree- 
ment, such regulations, and such resolutions or 
actions shall govern. In the case of any ambiguity in 
the interpretation or construction of any provision of 
this Agreement, such ambiguity shall be resolved in a 
manner consistent with such assistance agreement, 
such regulations, and the BANK BOARD’s or the 
CORPORATION ’s resolutions or actions. If there is a 
conflict between this Agreement and such assistance 


315 


agreement or such regulations or reslutions or 
actions, then such assistance agreement or such 
regulations or resolutions or actions shall govern. 

§$ 14 Governing Law. To the extent that federal law 
does not control, this Agreement and the rights and 
obligations under it shall be governed by the law of 
the State of lowa. Nothing in this Agreement shall 
require any unlawful action or inaction by either 


y. 
$15 Effective Date. This Agreement, and the 
transfer of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s assets and 
liabilities provided for by this Agreement, shall 
become effective upon its execution by the parties. 
$16 Entire Agreement; Severability. 

(a) This Agreement, together with any inter- 
pretation of it or any understanding agreed to in 
writing by the parties, constitutes the entire agree- 
ment between the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION and 
the CORPORATION as RECEIVER, but not in its 
corporate capacity, in connection with the transac- 
tions contemplated by this Agreement, and super- 
sedes all prior agreements and understandings of the 
parties, excepting only any resolutions or letters 
approved or adopted contemporaneously with this 
Agreemer* by the BANK BOARD or the 
CORPORATION. 

(b) If any provision of this Agreement is invalid 
or unenforceable, then, to the extent possible, all of 
the remaining provisions of this Agreement shall 
remain in full force and effect and shall be binding 
upon the parties. 

§$ 17 Counterparts; Modification; Headings. 

(a) This Agreement may be executed in any 
number of counterparts, each of which shall be an 
original but all of which shall together constitute one 
and the same instrument, and either party may 


316 


execute this Agreement by signing any such 
counterpart. 

(b) No modification of this Agreement shall be 
binding unless executed in writing and signed by the 
parties or their successors. 

(c) Section headings ure not to be considered part 
of this Agreement, are solely for convenience of 
reference, and shall not affect the meaning or 
interpretation of this Agreement or any of its 
provisions. 

$18 Warranties. 

(a) The RECEIVER warrants and represents 
that it has authority to enter into this Agreement and 
that, as RECEIVER, it has full power and authority 
to transfer the CLOSED ASSOCIATION’s assets and 
liabilities as provided by this Agreement. 

(b) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION warrants 
and represents that all of the transactions contem- 
plated by this Agreement have been or will be 
authorized by all necessary corporate actions and that 
it, by its proper officers or agents, has executed this 
Agreement and shall execute and deliver all instru- 
ments, certificates, and other documents that may be 
necessary or incidental to the performance of this 
Agreement. 

(c) These warranties shall survive the execution, 
performance, and termination of this Agreement. 

$19 Continuing Cooperation. The RECEIVER 
agrees, upon the request of the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION, to execute and deliver such further 
instruments and documents of conveyance as shall be 
necessary or proper to vest in the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION the RECEIVER’s full legal or 
equitable title to the property transferred to the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION pursuant to this 
Agreement.

ASSISTANCE AGREEMENT 


THIS AGREEMENT, dated March 11, 1988, is 
entered into by and among THE STATESMAN 
GROUP, INC., a Delaware corporation (“STATES- 
MAN”), AMERICAN LIFE AND CASUALTY IN- 
SURANCE COMPANY, a North Dakota corporation 
wholly owned by STATESMAN (“AMERICAN 
LIFE”), STATESMAN SAVINGS HOLDING CORP., 
Des Moines, lowa, a savings and loan holding company 
incorporated under the laws of the State of Iowa and 


‘owned by STATESMAN and AMERICAN LIFE 


(“ACQUIRER”), STATESMAN BANK FOR SAV- 
INGS, FSB, Waterloo, lowa, a federal stock savings 
bank wholly owned by the ACQUIRER (“ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION”), and the FEDERAL SAV- 
INGS AND LOAN INSURANCE CORPORATION 
(“FSLIC”), in its capacity as a corporate instrumen- 
tality and agency of the United States 
(“CORPORATION”). 


RECITALS 


A. The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION has been 
organized and chartered as a federal stock savings 
bank pursuant to § 406(a), (b)(1)(A), (c)(1)(B) and 
(c(3(B) of the National Housing Act, as amended 
(“NHA”), 12 U.S.C. § 1729(a), (b)(1)(A), (c)(1)(B) and 
(c(3)(B) (1982), and the ownership of the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION to the ACQUIRER and the 
ownership of the ACQUIRER by STATESMAN and 
AMERICAN LIFE have been authorized by the 
Federal Home Loan Bank Board (“Bank Board”) by 
Resolution No. 88-169, dated March __., 1988, in 
accordance with § 408(e) and (m) of the NHA, 12 
U.S.C § 1730a(e) and (m) (1982). 


326 


B. On March 11, 1988, the FSLIC was appointed 
receiver (“Receiver”) for First Federated Savings 
Bank, West Palm Beach, Florida, a stock savings bank 
chartered under the laws of the State of Florida 
(“CLOSED ASSOCIATION”). The Bank Board, by 
Resolution No. 88-169, dated March 11, 1988, has 
authorized the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION to pur- 
chase and assume substantially all of the assets and 
liabilities, respectively, of the CLOSED ASSOCIA- 
TION from the Receiver (“Acquisition”) pursuant to 
the Acquisition Agreement (“Acquisition Agreement”) 
of even date herewith between the Receiver and the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION. 

C. The Bank Board, by Resolution No. 88-169, dated 
March __., 1988, has authorized the supervisory 
conversion of First Federal Savings Bank, Waterloo, 
Iowa, a _ federally-chartered mutual savings bank 
(“First Federal”), and the merger of First Federal 
with and into the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
(“First Federal Merger’), pursuant to the Merger 
Agreement and Plan of Merger of even date herewith 
between First Federal and the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION (“First Federal Merger Agreement”). 

D. The Bank Board, by Resolution No. 88-169, dated 
March  __., 1988, has authorized the supervisory 
conversion of The Perpetual Savings and Loan 
Association, Waterloo, lowa, a mutual savings and loan 
association chartered under the laws of the State of 
lowa (“Perpetual”), and the merger of Perpetual with 
and into the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION (“Per- 
petual Merger”), pursuant to the Merger Agreement 
and Plan of Merger of even date herewith between 
Perpetual and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
(“Perpetual Merger Agreement”). 

E. The Bank Board, by Resolution No. 88-169, dated 
March __, 1988, has authorized the supervisory 


327 


conversion of Peoples Federal Savings and Loan 
Association, Waterloo, Iowa, a_ federally-chartered 
mutual savings and loan association (“Peoples”), and 
the merger of Peoples with and into the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION (“Peoples Merger”), pursuant to the 
Merger Agreement and Plan of Merger of even date 
herewith between Peoples and the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION (“Peoples Merger Agreement”). 

F. First Federal, Perpetual and Peoples may be 
sometimes referred to herein individually as a “MER- 
GED ASSOCIATION” and collectively as the “MER- 
GED ASSOCIATIONS”. The MERGED ASSOCIA- 
TIONS and the CLOSED ASSOCIATION may be 
sometimes referred to herein individually as an 
“ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION” and collectively as the 
“ACQUIRED ASSOCIATIONS”. The First Federal 
Merger, the Perpetual Merger and the Peoples Merger 
may be sometimes referred to herein individually as a 
“Merger” and collectively as the “Mergers”, and the 
First Federal Merger Agreement, the Perpetual 
Merger Agreement and the Peoples Merger Agree- 
ment may be sometimes referred to herein individually 
as a “Merger Agreement” and collectively as the 
“Merger Agreements”. 

G. The Bank Board, by Resolution No. 88-169, dated 
March ___., 1988, has certified for purposes of Section 
lillegible}(aX3)(D) of the Internal Revenue Code that 
the ground set forth in 12 U.S.C. § 1464(d)(6)(A)(i) 
exists with respect to each ACQUIRED ASSOCIA- 
TION in that each ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION is 
insolvent. 

H. The CORPORATION insured the accounts of the 
CLOSED ASSOCIATION prior to the appointment of 
the Receiver and insured the accounts of each 
MERGED ASSOCIATION prior to the Mergers, and 
the CORPORATION will insure the accounts of the 


328 


ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION after the Acquisition 
and the Mergers, in accordance with § 403 of the 
National Housing Act, as amended (”"NHA”), 12 U.S.C. 
§ 1726 (1982). 

I. The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, pursuant to 
the Acquisition Agreement, will succeed to substan- 
tially all of the rights, obligations, duties and liabilities 
of the CLOSED ASSOCIATION, and substantially all 
of the assets and property of every kind and character 
belonging to the CLOSED ASSOCIATION will be 
vested in and become the property of the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION. 

J. The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, pursuant to 
the Merger Agreements, will succeed to all of the 
rights, obligations, duties and liabilities of the MER- 
GED ASSOCIATIONS, and all of the assets and 
property of every kind and character belonging to the 
MERGED ASSOCIATIONS will be vested in and 
become the property of the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION. 

K. A condition to the obligation of the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION to consummate the Acquisition pur- 
suant to the Acquisition Agreement, a condition to the 
obligations of the respective parties to the Mergers to 
consummate the Mergers pursuant to the Merger 
Agreements, and a condition of the ACQUIRER to 


acquire the common stock of the ACQUIRING — 


ASSOCIATION, is that the CORPORATION, 
STATESMAN, AMERICAN LIFE, the ACQUIRER 
and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION enter into an 
agreement in the form of this Agreement, pursuant to 
which the CORPORATION will provide financial 
assistance and indemnification to the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION, the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and 
AMERICAN LIFE. 

L. The CORPORATION has decided, pursuant to 
§ 406(f)(1)-(4) of the NHA, 12 U.S.C. § 1729(f)(1)-(4) 


329 


(1982), to provide financial assistance and indemnifica- 
tion as set forth in this Agreement, having determined 
that each ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION is in danger of 
default and that the amounts payable pursuant to such 
assistance and indemnification would be less than the 
losses the CORPORATION would sustain upon the 
liquidation of the ACQUIRED ASSOCIATIONS 
through receiverships accompanied by payments of 
insurance of accounts. 

M. In consideration of the mutual promises con- 
tained in this Agreement, the parties hereto enter into 
the following agreement. 


AGREEMENT 


Section 1. Definitions. For the purposes of this 
Agreement, the following terms have the indicated 
meanings: 

(a) “Acquired Association Claim”: Any Claim that 
any MERGED ASSOCIATION or the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION as its successor has against (1) any 
person who is or was, on or prior to the Effective 
Date, a director, officer, controlling person (including 
any person who directly or indirectly exercised a 
controlling influence over the management or policies 
of any MERGED ASSOCIATION or any subsidiary 
thereof), employee or agent of any MERGED ASSO- 
CIATION or any subsidiary thereof, or an auditor, 
accountant, attorney, appraiser, broker, loan broker, 
deposit broker, securities dealer, investment banker, 
financial advisor or other professional, individual or 
entity that has performed services for any MERGED 
ASSOCIATION or any subsidiary thereof, or an 
employee, agent or other person acting for or in 
concert with any such person, provided that an action 


330 


or inaction of any such person on or prior to the 
Effective Date gave rise, in whole or in part, to such 
Claim; (2) an insurer who may be liable for indem- 
nification of the loss caused, in whole or in part, by the 
action or inaction of any person identified in § 1(a)(1) 
above; (3) an officer, director, agent, administrator, 
executor, personal representative, heir or assign of any 
person identified in § l(a)(1) above; or (4) any other 
person or persons, partnership, corporation, or legal 
entity, and any officer, director, partner, agent, 
administrator, executor, personal representative, heir 
or assign of any such third party, provided that the 
Claim against any such third party is related to a 
Claim against a party referred to in § 1(a)(1), (2) or (3) 
above. No Acquired Association Claim shall include 
any claim of the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
against a party referred to above to collect on a loan 
made by any MERGED ASSOCIATION on which loan 
such party is an obligor. 

(b) “Affiliate”: Affiliate, as that term is defined in 
$ 561.25 of the Insurance Regulations. 

(c) “Appraised Equity Capital”: Appraised equity 
capital as that term is defined in § 563.13(c) of the 
Insurance Regulations. 

(d) “Bank”: The Federal Home Loan Bank of Des 
Moines. 

(e) “Bank Board”: The Federal Home Loan Bank 
Board. 

(f) “Book Value”: When used in connection with a 
Related Claim or an Acquired Association Claim, and 
(except as set forth in § 1(f(2) and (3) below) when 
used with respect to a Covered Asset, Book Value 
shall mean the value reflected on the books and 
records immediately prior to the Effective Date of the 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION that owned such Claim 
or Covered Asset immediately prior to the Effective 


331 


Date (excluding any special reserve or valuation 
allowance for such Covered Asset on the books of such 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION immediately prior to the 
Effective Date), as adjusted by the Initial Audit and 
as thereafter adjusted or determined in accordance 
with the accounting principles required by this 
Agreement. 

(1) Except as set forth in § 1(f)(2) and (3) below, 
the following shall be included in the determination of 
Book Value: 

(A) With respect to any property or interest 
herein which secures a Covered Asset and which is 
acquired by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION after 
the Effective Date by Foreclosure, 

(i) The outstanding principal amount of the 
indebtedness secured by such property (excluding any 
specific loss reserve or valuation allowance relating to 
such indebtedness); 

(ii) Any taxes or hazard insurance premiums 
due and payable or accrued as of the Effective Date 
that are subsequently paid by the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION; 

(iii) The costs, fees, and expenses of Fore- 
closure, provided that any costs, fees, and expenses 
incurred incident to the acceptance of a deed in lieu of 
foreclosure, or in connection with the repossession of 
property sold subject to a sales contract or loan which 
is in default, shall be included in Book Value only if 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION complies with 
§ 14(c) of this Agreement; 

(iv) The amount, if approved in advance by 
the CORPORATION, paid for the satisfaction or 
settlement of a lien upon a Covered Asset or security 
for a Covered Asset, provided that such amount has 
not been reimbursed under another provision of this 
Agreement; 


332 


(v) Any unamortized premium paid or cap- 
italized by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION; and 

(vi) Any other costs, fees, and expenses 
associated with the acquisition of any property or 
interest therein that secures a Covered Asset, 
provided that such costs, fees, and expenses have not 
been reimbursed under another provision of this 
Agreement and have been approved in advance by the 
CORPORATION. 

(B) Properly capitalized expenses or assess- 
ments, if approved in advance by the CORPORA- 
TION, for the repair, rehabilitation, or completion of 
property that is a Covered Asset, or security for a 
Covered Asset; provided that such expenses and 
assessments are within the limits permitted by § 13 of 
this Agreement, have not been reimbursed under 
another provision of this Agreement, and are deter- 
mined in accordance with the accounting principles 
required by this Agreement; 

(2) The following shall not be included in the 
determination of Book Value for Covered Assets: 

(A) Any amount of specific reserves and 
valuation reserves of any ACQUIRED ASSOCIA- 
TION as of the Effective Date, as adjusted by the 
Initial Audit; 

(B) Any amount of accrued and uncollected 
interest or late charges on the principal amount of the 
indebtedness related to a Covered Asset that has been 
accrued through the Effective Date; 

(C) The undisbursed portion of any loan in 
process account, provided, however, that disburse- 
ments made after the Effective Date pursuant to a 
legally binding commitment of the ACQUIRED ASSO- 
CIATION that owned such Covered Asset which 
exists immediately prior to the Effective Date, or 
pursuant to the prior written consent of the CORPO- 
RATION, shall be included in Book Value; 


333 


(D) The amount of any deferred income related 
to a Covered Asset, including, without limitation, 
unearned discounts on loans purchased, unearned 
profit on real estate sold, deferred loan fees or 
acquisition credits, unearned discounts on installment 
loans, deferred gains on securities, and other unearned 
discounts; 

(E) With respect to any Covered Asset re- 
flected on an ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION’s books 
and records immediately prior to the Effective Date, 
any adjustment to the value of such Covered Asset 
made solely as a result of the accounting treatment of 
the Acquisition or the Mergers as a purchase of assets: 
| (F) The amount of any net operating income or 
loss determined in accordance with the accounting 
principles required by this Agreement and attributable 
to Covered Assets on the books and records of any 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION immediately prior to the 
Effective Date or acquired as a result of Foreclosure 
with respect to a Covered Asset; and 

(G) The cost of appraisals required by the 
CORPORATION pursuant to this Agreement. 

(3) Book Value, when used with respect to a 
Covered Asset that becomes such pursuant to § 1(h)(4) 
of this Agreement, shall mean the value of the 
Covered Asset on the books and records of the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION as of the consummation 
of the transaction consented to pursuant to said 
$ 1(h)(4), as such value may be adjusted in accordance 
with the accounting principles required by this 
Agreement.

(g) “Claim”: Any claim, demand, or cause of action. 

(h) “Covered Asset”: Except as provided in § 1(h)(5), 
the term “Covered Asset” means the following: 

(1) Any loan which is on the books of any 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION immediately prior to the 
Effective Date that is contractually delinquent 90 days 
or more as of the Effective Date or that becomes 
contractually delinquent 90 days or more at any time 
within two years after the Effective Date; provided 
that, with respect to loans other than Participation 
Interests, Foreclosure is formally instituted within the 
later of 18 months from the Effective Date or 90 days 
after the date on which such loan has become a 
Covered Asset; and provided further that, if Fore- 
closure is delayed or barred by an order or ruling of 
any court of proper jurisdiction or by operation of law, 
then the applicable period within which Foreclosure 
must be formally instituted shall be extended until a 
reasonable time following the date on which Fore- 
closure is no longer delayed or barred. 

(2) Real Estate Owned on the books of any 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION immediately prior to the 
Effective Date or acquired after the Effective Date as 
a result of a Foreclosure of any loan constituting a 
Covered Asset. 

(3) Real Estate in Judgment and Real Estate Held 
for Investment on the books of any ACQUIRED 
ASSOCIATION immediately prior to the Effective 
Date. 

(4) A loan, contract, or investment made by the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION in connection with or to 


335 


facilitate the sale of, or to salvage, a Covered Asset, or 
any property or interest therein received in exchange 
for a Covered Asset, if, prior to the making of any 
such loan, contract, investment, or exchange of a 
Covered Asset the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION has 
obtained the CORPORATION ’s written consent to the 
transaction and to the treatment as a Covered Asset 
of such loan, contract, investment, or property or 
interest received in exchange for a Covered Asset; 
provided that the CORPORATION’s consent shall not 
be required if such Covered Asset is a Participation 
Interest and if, notwithstanding the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION’s best efforts to oppose such loan, 
contract, investment or exchange, the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION (as a consequence of being the 
Successor to a contractual or other legal obligation 
incurred by one of the ACQUIRED ASSOCIATIONS 
or as a consequence of entering into a contractual or 
other legal obligation which has been approved in 
writing by the CORPORATION) is contractually or 
otherwise legally obligated to make such loan, contract 
or investment or accept such exchange. 

(5) Notwithstanding anything herein to the con- 
trary, Covered Assets shall not include: 
| (A) Loans secured by valid and enforceable first 
liens or first mortgages on one to four family dwelling 
units; 

(B) Loans secured by valid and enforceable first,
