balance of $50,000 or less, provided that a participation 
loan is not excluded unless the total outstanding 
principal balance of the participation loan, not just the 
portion held by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, is 
$50,000 or less; 

(C) Any one to four family dwelling units; 

(D) Any office buildings and facilities of any 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION, any real estate or

Proceeds Received exceeds the Book Value as written 
down.

(1) “Debit” or “Debit Item”: An amount charged or

Section 3. Special Reserve Account. The ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION for itself and the ACQUIRER, 


346 


STATESMAN and AMERICAN LIFE shall establish 
as of the Effective Date a single memorandum 
account, with an opening balance of zero, to be called 
the Special Reserve Account, and shall maintain such 
account solely for the purpose of, and in accordance 
with, the provisions of this Agreement, without 
limitation of its duty to maintain its books and records 
in accordance with proper accounting procedures and 
the Insurance Regulations. 

(a) Debits. The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, for 
itself and the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and 
AMERICAN LIFE, following the Effective Date, may 
charge as Debits to the Special Reserve Account 
amounts equal to each of the following items: 

(1) Covered Asset Losses. Ninety-three percent 
(93%) of each Covered Asset Loss; 

(2) Appraisal Costs. The cost of any independent 
appraisal required by the CORPORATION pursuant 
to this Agreement and paid for by the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION; provided, that the appraiser selected 
by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION and the cost of 
appraisal have been approved in advance by the 
CORPORATION, and provided further that the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall instruct the ap- 
praiser to deem both the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION and the CORPORATION as its client and to 
conduct the appraisal in accordance with standards 
approved by the Bank Board; 

(3) Indemnification. The amounts owed by the 
CORPORATION under §7 of this Agreement pur- 
suant to its obligations to indemnify the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION, the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and 
AMERICAN LIFE; 

(4) Claim Expenses. The amount of the reasonable 
costs and expenses incurred by the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION, the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and 


347 


AMERICAN LIFE, where appropriate, in pursuit of a 
Related Claim or Acquired Association Claim, and 
reimbursable by the CORPORATION pursuant to § 7 
or §8 of this Agreement; 

(5) Covered Asset Purchase. The amount of the 
purchase price of a Covered Asset purchased by the 
CORPORATION in accordance with §§ 13 or 15 of this 
Agreement; 

(6) Payment of Credits. The amount of any 
payments made by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, 
for itself and the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and 
AMERICAN LIFE, to eliminate a Credit balance in 
the Special Reserve Account; 

(7) Expenses to Cure. The amount of other 
payments or costs incurred with the CORPORA- 
TION’s prior written approval in order to cure or 
salvage any Covered Asset or security for a Covered 
Asset or prevent a substantial deterioration in value of 
a Covered Asset, provided that such payments or costs 
are not appropriately capitalized under the accounting 
principles required by this Agreement; 

(8) Assignment of Related Claim and Acquired 
Association Claim and Excess Recoveries. The Book 
Value reflected on the books and records of an 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION immediately prior to the 
Effective Date of a Related Claim or Acquired 
Association Claim assigned to the CORPORATION 
pursuant to § 8(b) of this Agreement and any excess 
recovery on any such claim to be debited to the 
Special Reserve Account pursuant to § 8(e) of this 
Agreement. 

(9) Uncollected Interest. The amount of the 
ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION’s uncollected interest on 
any Covered Asset accrued prior to the Effective Date 
that remains uncollected subsequent to the Effective 
Date, at such time as such accrued and uncollected 


348 


interest is determined by the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION, with the written concurrence of the 
CORPORATION, to be uncollectible and is written off, 
provided, that this amount shall not include any 
amounts paid by the CORPORATION to the AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION pursuant to § 6(a)(1) and 
(2) of this Agreement, including any amounts with 
respect to which general loss reserves, specific loss 
reserves or valuation allowances were established 
prior to the Effective Date; and provided further, that 
the amounts of such interest that are subsequently 
collected shall be credited to the Special Reserve 
Account upon collection. 

(10) Yield Subsidy. 

(A) For any given Quarter during the first five 
years of this Agreement, with respect to all Covered 
Assets which have not been liquidated at the 
beginning of such Quarter, the excess, if any, of (i) the 
Guaranteed Yield Amount, as computed below over (ii) 
the actual yield, whether positive or negative, during 
such Quarter on such Covered Assets, which shall 
include all interest and other income received or 
accrued which as attributable to such Covered Assets, 
including, specifically, with respect to Real Estate 
Owned, Real Estate Held for Investment, and Real 
Estate in Judgment, any net operating income or net 
operating loss; provided, that for any Quarter during 
the term of this Agreement in which the actual yield 
exceeds the Guaranteed Yield provided for hereunder, 
there shall be a Credit to the Special Reserve Account 
for the amount of such excess. 

Net operating income or operating loss on Real 
Estate Owned, Real Estate Held for Investment and 
Real Estate in Judgment shall be determined by 
deducting all expenses directly attributable to such 
real estate (including all items appropriately expensed 


349 


under GAAP, but specifically excluding depreciation 
and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION’s administra- 
tive and overhead expenses), from the gross income 
attributable to such real estate. 

(B) The Guaranteed Yield Amount for any 
Quarter constituting a full calendar quarter shall be 
computed by multiplying the Average Book Value of 
such Covered Assets for such quarter (determined for 
a given Quarter by adding the Book Value of all 
unsold Covered Assets on the first day of the Quarter 
and the Book Value of all unsold Covered- Assets on 
the last day of each calendar month within the Quarter 
and dividing the sum by four) by the Guaranteed Yield 
Percentage. The Guaranteed Yield Percentage for all 
Covered Assets other than Participation Interests 
shall be equal to one-quarter of a rate that is equal to 
the cost of funds for savings and loan institutions 
operating in the Bank Board’s Eighth District, as most 
recently reported at the end of a Quarter by the Bank 
(“Eighth District COF”), plus 225 basis points until 
the second anniversary of the Effective Date, shall 
then decline by 50 basis points and shall decline 
further by an additional 25 basis points on the third 
anniversary of the Effective Date and on each 
anniversary thereafter until the termination of the 
CORPORATION’s obligations under this Agreement. 
The Guaranteed Yield Percentage for Participation 
Interests shall remain fixed at one-quarter of Eighth 
District COF plus 225 basis points. 

(C) If the first or last Quarter during the term 
of this Agreement does not constitute a full Quarter, 
the Guaranteed Yield Amount for such Quarter shall 
be the product of 

(i) the Average Book Value of such Covered 
Assets for such Quarter or portion thereof (determined 
by adding the Book Value of all unsold Covered Assets 


350 


on the first day of the Quarter and the last day of each 
calendar month ending within the Quarter or portion 
thereof and dividing the sum by the number of such 
first and last days) multiplied by a fraction (the 
numerator of which shall be the number of days during 
which this Agreement was in effect in suc Quarter 
and the denominator of which shall be 90), and 
(ii) the Guaranteed Yield Percentage. 

(D) In the event of reinstatement of a loan to 
income-producing status and collection of delinquent 
interest so that such ioan ceases to be a Covered 
Asset, the Guaranteed Yield Amount for any previous 
Quarter that included such loan in the determination of 
Average Book Value for all or any portion of such 
quarter shall be recalculated excluding such loan, and 
the sum of the differences between the Guaranteed 
Yield Amount as recalculated and as_ originally 
calculated for each such Quarter shall be a Credit to 
the Special Reserve Account. The calculation of the 
Credit to be made to the Special Reserve Account, if 
any, pursuant to this § 3(a)(10)(D) shall be made at the 
end of each Quarter for all loans which cease to be 
Covered Assets during such Quarter. 

(E) With respect to loans that become contrac- 
tually delinquent 90 days or more after the Effective 
Date, such loans shall be deemed to be Covered 
Assets, for purposes of this § 3(a)(10), from the time 
they first become contractually delinquent. At the end 
of each Quarter during which a loan becomes 
contractually delinquent 90 days or more, the Guaran- 
teed Yield Amount for the previous Quarter (if such 
loan became contractually delinquent in such prior 
Quarter) shall be recalculated including such loan as of 
the day it first became contractually delinquent, and 
the difference between the Guaranteed Yield Amount 
as recalculated and as originally calculated for such 


351 


prior Quarter shall be added to the Guaranteed Yield 
Amount calculated for such current Quarter. 

(F) For any Quarter that has more than one 
Guaranteed Yield Percentage applicable to portions of 
such Quarter, the Guaranteed Yield Percentage to be 
applicable to the entire Quarter shall be determined by 
multiplying each Guaranteed Yield Percentage by the 
number of days in such Quarter to which such 
Guaranteed Yield Percentage applies, adding the 
results of such multiplications. and dividing such sum 
by the totai number of days in the Quarter. 

(11) Recoveries on Purchased Covered Assets. Any 
amount permitted to be debited to the Special Reserve 
Account pursuant to § 15(d).

Section 4. Write-Down of a Covered Asset. 

(a) Approval of Write-Down. The ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION shall not write down any Covered 
Asset without first obtaining the CORPORATION’s 
written consent pursuant to the terms of this § 4, and 
shall undertake to liquidate each Covered Asset in 
accordance with the terms of this Agreement prior to 
applying to the CORPORATION for such consent. In 
the event that the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
proposes to write down a Covered Asset, the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall first apply to the 
CORPORATION for permission to write down a 
Covered Asset by submitting to the CORPORATION 
in writing the terms, including the amount, of the 
proposed write-down, and the grounds for the AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION’s opinion that the Covered 
Asset should be written down, supported by such 
information or documents as the CORPORATION may 
reasonably request. 

(b) Determination of Write-Down. With respect to 
any Covered Asset, any write-down shall be deter- 
mined as follows: 

(1) The CORPORATION may require the AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION to write down the Book 
Value of any Covered Asset; and such amount of the 
resulting Covered Asset Loss shall be debited to. the 
Special Reserve Account as provided in § 3(a)(1); or 

(2) Within thirty (30) days of the CORPORA- 
TION’s receipt for an application from the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION setting with the grounds for the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION’s opinion that the value 
of a Covered Asset is less than its Book Value, and 
such information or documents as the CORPORA- 
TION may request, the CORPORATION shall: 


360 


(A) approve the application, with or without 
modifications or conditions, and permit the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION to write down the Covered 
Asset’s Book Value and Debit such amount of the 
resulting Covered Asset Loss to the Special Reserve 
Account as provided in § 3(a)(1); 

(B) deny the application (the basis for such 
denial shall not be unreasonable), in which case the 
CORPORATION shall direct the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION to take such action with respect to the 
Covered Asset as, in the exercise of reasonable 
judgment, the CORPORATION deems necessary or 
appropriate; or 

(C) require the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
to submit additional information, including, but not 
limited to, an independent appraisal. Within thirty (30) 
days after the CORPORATION’s receipt of any 
additional information requested, the CORPORATION 
shall approve, with or without modifications or 
conditions, or deny such application, in which case the 
CORPORATION shall direct action by the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION as provided by paragraph (B) 
above. 

(c) Required Sale. The CORPORATION may at any 
time require the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION to sell 
any Covered Asset to any person or entity the 
CORPORATION designates, including the CORPO- 
RATION, at a price specified pursuant to § 13 of this 
Agreement, provided, that the CORPORATION can 
not require the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION to 
provide financing to such person or entity. 

(d) Adjustment at Termination. As of the Termina- 
tion Date of this Agreement, the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION shall adjust, if necessary, the Book 
Value of each Covered Asset remaining on its books to 
the value of such asset as determined by agreement 


361 


between the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION and the 
CORPORATION or by an appraisal acceptable to both 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION and the CORPO- 
RATION. Such agreement may be made or such 
appraisals may be obtained at any time beginning on 
the date which is four years and six months from the 
Effective Date. The amount of any adjustment of the 
Book Value of any Covered Asset pursuant to this 
§ 4(d) shall be deemed, for purposes of this Agree- 
ment, a Covered Asset Loss or a Covered Asset 
Recovery, respectfully, prior to the Termination Date. 
An appraisal pursuant to this § 4(d) shall be performed 
by an appraiser selected by the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION and acceptable to the CORPORATION. In 
the event either party objects to an appraisal prepared 
by such an appraiser, such party shall submit its 
objections in writing to the other party. Such 
objections shall be specific and shall be based upon the 
Bank Board’s appraisal policies then in effect. Follow- 
ing any such objections, the parties can request the 
appraiser to revise his appraisal in light of such 
objections or can select a new appraiser in the same 
manner as the initial appraiser was selected; provided 
that any new appraisal requested by the Acquiring 
Association shall be performed at the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION’s expense. Any revised or new ap- 
praisal shall be subject to approval by both parties on 
the same basis. 

Section 5. Initial Audit and Inventory. As soon as 
is practicable after the Effective Date, the CORPO- 
RATION with the assistance of the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION’s personnel, whose salaries and ex- 
penses shall be paid by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION without reimbursement from the CORPORA- 
TION, shall cause to be prepared and completed by 
Coopers & Lybrand. 


362 


(a) an Initial Audit of each ACQUIRED ASSOCIA- 
TION as of immediately prior to the Effective Date, to 
be performed in accordance with generally accepted 
auditing standards, except that the scope of the Initial 
Audit shall be determined by the CORPORATION 
consistent with this Agreement and shall not include a 
revaluation of any ACQUIRED ASSOCIATION’s 
assets, other than single family residential properties 
constituting REO (selected on a sampling basis), or a 
determination whether accrued but uncollected inter- 
est, if any, is collectible; and 

(b) an inventory of all assets and liabilities required 
by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION as of the Effec- 
tive Date under the Acquisition Agreement and the 
Merger Agreements. Such inventory shall be limited 
to such assets and liabilities as are shown on the books 
and records of the ACQUIRED ASSOCIATIONS 
immediately prior to the Effective Date, or which are 
reasonably ascertainable during the preparation of the 
inventory. A copy of the Initial Audit and the 
inventory, in such forms as the CORPORATION shall 
determine, shall be furnished to the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION upon completion and approval by the 
CORPORATION. 


Section 6. Payments and Contributions. 


(a) Initial Contributions by the CORPORATION. 

(1) Cash Contribution. If the conditions set 
forth in §2 of this Agreement have been satisfied, 
within five business days after the Effective Date, the 
CORPORATION shall wire transfer to the account of 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION at the Bank imme- 
diately available funds in an amount equal to $60 
million or approximately fifty-eight percent (58%) of 
the estimated combined Negative Regulatory Capital 
of all of the ACQUIRED ASSOCIATIONS imme- 
diately prior to the Effective Date, which the parties 


362a 


agree equals $103 million (“Estimated Negative Reg- 
ulatory Capital”).

other than reports or financial statements prepared for 
reporting or disclosure purposes that are required to 
be governed by GAAP, $26 million of the contribution 
made under this § 6(a)(1) shall be credited to the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION’s regulatory capital ac- 
count and shall constitute regulatory capital (as 
defined in § 561.13 of the Insurance Regulations). The 
parties understand that the preceding sentence is not 
intended to address in any way the accounting 
treatment of contributions from the CORPORATION 
that must be reflected in any filing that the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATIONS may make, whether 
to the Bank Board (for reporting or disclosure 
purposes) or otherwise, that requires the submission of 
financial statements prepared in accordance with 
GAAP.

§ 2 of this Agreement have been satisfied, on the 
Effective Date the CORPORATION shall deliver to

executed by the CORPORATION, the principal 
amount which shall be equal to the difference between 
the Estimated Negative Regulatory Capital and the 
amount of cash contributed to the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION within five business days after the 
Effective Date pursuant to § 6(a)(1) above. Promptly

(1) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall pay 
to the FSLIC- 9% of its Gross Book Income for the 
first two fiscal years following the Effective Date and 
18% of its Gross Book Income for the fiscal years and 
any portion thereof from and including the third fiscal 
year through the 10th anniversary of the Effective 
Date. Any payments due hereunder shall be credited 
to the Special Reserve Account if the Gross Book 
Income is determined prior to the termination of this 
Agreement-and shall be paid in immediately available 
funds if the Gross Book Income is determined after 
the termination of this Agreement.

the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION’s Gross Book In- 
come for any year equals to exceeds $5 million, then

the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall assign and

Preferred Stock having an initial book value of $2.0 
million for such a year, and the transfer of Preferred

obligations to make any credits, payments or transfers 
pursuant to this § 6(d). 

(4) Credits, payments or transfers pursuant to 
this § 6(d) shall be made as soon after the end of the 
applicable fiscal year or, in the case of the portion of a 
fiscal year ending on the tenth anniversary of the 
Effective Date, as soon after the end of such portion, 
as the Gross Book Income for such fiscal year or 
portion thereof, but in no event later than 90 days 
after the end of such fiscal year or portion thereof. 

(5) For purposes of this § 6(d), the term Gross 
Tax Income shall mean net income in accordance with 
GAAP but without any deduction for any payments 
made on the subordinated debenture issued to the 
CORPORATION pursuant to § 6(e). For purposes of 
this § 6(d) “Statesman Preferred Stock” shall mean 
1988 Series I Convertible Preferred Stock of STATES- 
MAN bearing a dividend rate of 8%. 

(e) Issuance of Subordinated Debenture. On the

(f) Reimbursement of Credit Balances. 

(1) Within thirty (30) days after the CORPORA-

pursuant to § 6(a) of this Agreement, the ACQUIR-

QUIRER, remit to the CORPORATION an amount

Balance of the Special Reserve Account as of the last 
day of the Quarter reported in the Quarterly Report; 
provided, that if such amount exceeds the total of the 
contributions made by the Corporation to date and not 
repaid, then the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall 
remit to the Corporation only so much of such amount 
as equals the total of the contributions, and the excess, 
if any, shall remain as a credit balance in the Special 
Reserve Account. 

(2) Within ninety (90) days after rendering its 
final accounting required under § 11(c) of this Agree- 
ment, the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall, for 
itself and the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and 
AMERICAN LIFE, remit to the CORPORATION an 
amount equal to the aggregate net Credit balance in 
the Special Reserve Account as of the Termination 
Date; provided, that the amount so remitted shall not 
exceed the total contributions made by the CORPO- 
RATION under this Agreement and not repaid. 

(g) Optional Purchase of Covered Assets. In the 
event that the CORPORATION exercises its right to 
purchase a Covered Asset pursuant to § 13 or § 15 of 
this Agreement, the excess, if any, of the purchase 
price paid by the CORPORATION over the fair 
market value of such asset shall be deemed to be a 
contribution to the CORPORATION. Fair market 
value shall be determined, at the election of the 
CORPORATION, by agreement of the CORPORA- 
TION and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, or by 


367 


an independent appraisal accepted by the CORPORA- 
TION and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION. 

(h) Adjustments. Any contribution or payment by 
the CORPORATION or the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION pursuant to this Agreement, any Credit or 
Debit to the Special Reserve Account, or any 
accounting or report furnished by the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION under this Agreement shall be subject 
to adjustment if it subsequently is determined that 
such contribution, payment, Credit, Debit, accounting, 
or report was based upon error or upon a tax 
determination that has been subsequently adjusted, or 
was not computed in accordance with the accounting 
principles prescribed in this Agreement, or, without 
the approval of the CORPORATION and the AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION, was otherwise inconsist- 
ent with the terms of this Agreement. 

(i) Repayment. In the event that a court of 
competent jurisdiction directs the ACQUIRING AS- 
SOCIATION to divest itself of the assets it acquired 
and the liabilities it assumed from any ACQUIRER

(c) Covered Asset Restrictions. The ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION shall not, without the CORPORA-

receipt of a request for such consent) unless the

(1) lease any Covered Asset for a fixed term of 
more than one year; 

(2) engage the services of any consultant, at- 
torney, accountant, agent, or other professional in 
connection with the management or administration of 
any Covered Asset the cost of whose services may be 
debited to the Special Reserve Account pursuant to 
§ 3(a); and 

(3) extend, modify, or sell at a loss any loan or 
contract that is a Covered Asset, or extend or modify 
any loan or contract that could become a Covered 
Asset and result in a Debit to the Special Reserve 
Account; provided that, at the direction of the 
pate the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
Shall sell any such loan or contract; provided that the 
CORPORATION’s consent shall not be required if 
such Covered Asset is a Participation Interest and if 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION used its best 
efforts to oppose any such action identified in 
subsections (1), (2), or (3) above. 

(d) Covered Asset Requirements. The ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION shall, unless the CORPORATION 
waives any such requirement in writing: 


390 


(1) submit to the CORPORATION upon request 
an independent appraisal, acceptable to the CORPO- 
RATION, of any Covered Asset; 

(2) promptly institute Foreclosure proceedings, as 
provided in § 14 of this Agreement, with respect to 
any loan that is a Covered Asset (other than a 
Participation Interest), unless the CORPORATION 
has given written approval for an alternative action; 

(3) proceed promptly, within those procedures 
provided for in § 14 and in accordance with prudent 
business practice, with the Liquidation of any Real 
Estate Owned that is a Covered Asset (other than a 
Participation Interest); 

(4) retain all written offers to purchase any 
Covered Asset pending inspection by the CORPORA- 
TION and not dispose of any such offer without the 
CORPORATION’s prior written approval; 

(5) use its best efforts, in accordance with the 
management standard described in § 12(a)(1) of this 
Agreement, to cause each parcel of Real Estate 
Owned, each Covered Asset or the property securing 
each Covered Asset, to be insured against loss or 
damage by fire (extended coverage), flood and any 
other such hazards that may normally be encountered 
with respect to such property and, to the extent 
applicable, in accordance with § 571.4 of the Insurance 
Regulations, or any successor regulation. Where 
appropriate, all insurance contracts shall contain the 
standard mortgage loss payable clause in favor of the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION; and

for a Covered Asset in accordance with the ACQUIR-

except as may otherwise by provided pursuant to 
§ [illegible)(a) of this Agreement.

Section 13. Sale and Repair of Covered Assets. 

(a) Unless otherwise permitted by the CORPORA- 
TION, compliance with the terms of this section shall 
be a condition precedent to the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION’s right to Debit to the Special Reserve 
Account, pursuant to §3 of this Agreement, the 
amount of any loss on the Covered Asset, or to add to 
the Book Value of any Real Estate Owned that is a 
Covered Asset, any expenditure to repair, rehabilitate, 
or complete Real Estate Owned that is a Covered 
Asset, or portion thereof, that exceeds the applicable 
limitation of § 13(e) of this Agreement; provided that 
the terms of this section shall not apply to Participa- 
tion Interests. 

(b) As soon as practicable and not less than forty- 
five (45) days prior to the offering for sale of Real 
Estate Owned that is a Covered Asset as of the Effec- 
tive Date, and otherwise within forty-five (45) days 
after the completion of a Foreclosure, including any 
applicable redemption period, of a loan that constitutes 
a Covered Asset and results in the acquisition of Real 
Estate Owned, the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
shall provide to the CORPORATION a schedule of 
each such Covered Asset, setting forth a description of 
the Covered Asset, its Book Value, its estimated 
market value, the recommended listing sales price, the 
minimum sales price, the proposed terms of sale, and 
the estimated selling costs, and, unless waived by the 


394 


CORPORATION, an appraisal acceptable to the 
CORPORATION of each such Covered Asset. 

(c) Within thirty (80) days after receipt of a 
schedule and an appraisal required by § 13(b), the 
CORPORATION shall: 

(1) approve the proposed minimum sales price 
and the terms of sale for each Covered Asset, which 
may then be sold at less than such approved minimum 
sales price, upon the approved sales terms, without 
further notice to the CORPORATION; or 

(2) disapprove the proposed minimum sales price 
of the terms of sale for any Covered Asset and 
establish a minimum sales price, which shall in no 
event be more than ninety (90%) percent of the value 
established by the appraisal, plus one hundred (100%) 
percent of the estimated selling costs, and the terms 
of sale. Thereafter, the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION 
may sell such asset, at not less than the approved 
price and upon the approved sales terms, without 
further notice to the CORPORATION; 

(3) purchase such Covered Asset at the lesser of 
Book Value, ninety (90%) percent of the value 
established by the appraisal, and the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION’s proposed minimum sales price 
(minus estimated selling costs). Purchases by the 
CORPORATION pursuant to this § 13 shall be treated 
as purchases by third parties for the purposes of 
§§ 3(a)(1) and 3(c)(1) of this Agreement. The amount 
payable by the CORPORATION in connection with 
such a purchase shall be debited to the Special 
Reserve Account as of the purchase date if the 
purchase occurs prior to the Termination Date, and 
shall be paid to the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION by 
the CORPORATION in immediately available funds 
no later than ten (10) days following conveyance to the 
CORPORATION of the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION’s rights, title, and interest in such Covered 


395 


— if the purchase occurs after the Termination 
ate. 

(d) The CORPORATION shall evaluate the market- 
ing efforts made by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION, all written offers to purchase, current condi- 
tion, and all other pertinent factors with respect to 
any Covered Asset on which a sales contract has not 
been accepted within ninety (90) days from the date 
on which the CORPORATION approved its sales 
price. The CORPORATION thereafter shall advise 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION in writing concern- 
ing a revised sales price or additional marketing 
efforts. 

(e) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall not: 


(1) expend for the repair, rehabilitation, or 
completion of any Real Estate Owned that is a 
Covered Asset an amqunt win excess of Ten 
Thousand Dollars ($10,000.00) for a residential 
property, or in excess of Five Thousand Dollars 
($5,000.00) per family unit plus Twenty Thousand 
Dollars ($20,000.00) for common areas, up to a 
maximum of Forty Thousand Dollars ($40,000.00) 
for a multi-family building, or in excess of 
Twenty Thousand Dollars ($20,000.00) for a 
commercial property; or 

(2) expend any sum for the repair, rehabilita- 
tion, or completion of any Covered Asset thet is 
not Real Estate Owned, 


without the prior written consent of the CORPORA- 
TION, unless the CORPORATION expressly waives 
this requirement in writing or unless an emergency 
exists, such as a deficiency of the asset that violates a

Section 14. Foreclosures. 

(a) Prior to the Foreclosure of any instrument 
securing a loan that is or may become a Covered 
Asset (other than a participation Interest), the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall, upon the COR- 
PORATION’s request, inspect and evaluate or ap- 
praise the security property to determine its recover- 
able value, taking into consideration all circumstances 
affecting marketability of title, including the existence 
of any mechanic’s or materialman’s liens or other 
encumbrances on the security property. 

(1) Except as provided in § 14(a)(2) below, if, the 
opinion of the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, the 
security property has recoverable value and the Book 
Value of the related covered Asset is less than One 
Hundred Thousand Dollars ($100,000.00), the AC-

ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, within ten (10) days

CORPORATION, of the amount to be bid at the 
foreclosure sale. 

(b) The ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall advise 
the CORPORATION monthly of all completed Fore- 
closures subject to 12(a), citing the Book Value, 
appraised value, and the amount of the successful bid 
in each case. In addition, if the successful bid, 
whether by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, or 
another party, results in a deficiency, the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION shall advise the CORPORA- 
TION concerning the likelihood of securing a judg- 
ment against the defaulting mortgagor. 

(c) A deed in lieu of Foreclosure or any equivalent 
instrument may not be obtained and no consideration 
shall be paid to the debtor under such deed without 
the written consent of the CORPORATION. 

(d) In the case of any instrument securing a 
nonmortgage loan that is or may become a Covered 
Asset, the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall follow 
up on such procedures and take such actions as the 
CORPORATION may request. 

(e) Unless otherwise permitted by the CORPORA- 
TION, compliance with the provisions of this section 
by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall be a 
condition precedent to the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION’s right to Debit the Special Reserve Account 
with respect to any loss resulting from a Foreclosure. 

Section 15. CORPORATION’s Right to Purchase 
Covered Assets. 

(a) At any time during the term of this Agreement 
and until the later of ninety (90) days after the 
Termination Date, or termination of the CORPORA- 
TION’s obligation to provide assistance pursuant to 
$19 of this Agreement, the CORPORATION shall 


400 


have the right to purchase without recourse against 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION any or all Covered 
Assets that have not been liquidated prior to the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION’s receipt of the COR- 
PORATION’s notice that it shall exercise its right to 
purchase, at a purchase rice as stated in § 13(c)(3) of 
this agreement. Purchases by the CORPORATION 
pursuant to this § 15 shall be treated as purchases by 
third parties for the purposes of §§ 3(a)(1) and 3(¢)(1) 
of this Agreement. 

(b) The purchase price shall be debited to the 
Special Reserve Account as of the purchase date if 
the purchase occurs prior to the termination of this 
Agreement, or shall be paid to the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION by the CORPORATION in imme- 
diately available funds within ten (10) days following 
conveyance to the CORPORATION of the ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION’s right, title, and interest in 
such asset if the purchase occurs after the termina- 
tion of this Agreement. The excess, if any, of the 
purchase state over the fair market value of such 
asset shall be deemed to be a contribution by the 
CORPORATION as provided in §6 of this 
Agreement. 

(c) In order to facilitate such sale to the CORPO- 
RATION, the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall, at 
the CORPORATION’s request: | 

(1) Provide the CORPORATION with such 
information as the CORPORATION deems necessary 
regarding the nature or condition of the Covered 
Asset and documenting the Book Value of such 
Covered Asset; 

(2) Execute and deliver such instruments of sale, 
grant, conveyance, or transfer, and make endorse- 


401 


ments in such term and manner and at such time as 
the CORPORATION shall require; and 

(3) Execute and deliver all such other instru- 
ments together with all insurance policies, title 
policies, and title certificates which the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION possesses and are necessary or 
requisite, in the opinion of the CORPORATION, to 
complete such sale, grant, conveyance, or transfer. 

(d) The Net Proceeds Received by the CORPORA- 
TION as a result of Liquidation of any Covered Asset 
purchased pursuant to 13 or 15 of this Agreement in 
excess of the purchase price and by the CORPORA- 
TION shall be retained by the CORPORATION to 
the extent it has made contributions under this 
Agreement that have not been repaid, and the 
remainder, if any, shall be paid to the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION. 

Section 16. Sale of Assets of the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION. In the event of a Sale (as defined 
below), within seven (7) years following the Effective 
Date, by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION of any of 
its assets, or any of the capital stock of any 
subsidiary or Affiliate of the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION formed to conduct any of the operations, or 
hold any of the assets, of the ACQUIRING ASSO- 
CIATION, the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION shall 
pay promptly to the CORPORATION a percentage to 
be agreed upon at such time, but in no event greater 
than fifty percent (50%), of the net proceeds of such 
Sale; provided that no such sale shall be consummated 
without the prior written approval of the CORPORA- 
TION; and provided further that in determining the 
amount of such net proceeds, an appropriate amount 
thereof (based upon the ratio of the assets being sold 
to the total assets of the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA-

TION) shall be allocated as a return of the invest- 
ment of the ACQU'TRER in the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION. For the purposes of this § 16, the 
term “Sale” shall mean any and all sales consum- 
mated or agreed to during any 90 day period having 
an aggregate sales price of $20 million. If a Sale shall 
occur within five (5) years following the Effective 
Date without the approval of the CORPORATION, 
which approval may be granted or withhold at the 
sole discretion of the CORPORATION, notwithstand- 
ing the provisions of § 19 of this Agreement, this 
Agreement and all of the CORPORATION’s obliga- 
tions under it shall terminate immediately after the 
consummation of the sale. Notwithstanding anything 
herein to the contrary, the term “Sale” shall not 
include the transfer to an Affiliate of the AC- 
QUIRER the stock or any or all of the assets and 
liabilities of Federated Financial, Combined Mortgage 
Corporation, Homestead Mortgage Corporation or 
Perpetual Financial Corporation. 

Section 17. Accounting Principles. Except as 
otherwise provided, any computations made for 
purposes of this Agreement shall be governed by 
GAAP, except that where such principles conflict 
with the terms of the Agreement, applicable regula- 
tions in the Bank Board or the CORPORATION, or 
any resolution or action in the Bank Board approving 
or relating to the Acquisition, the Mergers or to this 
Agreement, then this Agreement, such regulations, or 
such resolution or action shall govern. In the case of 
any ambiguity in the interpretation or construction of 
any provision of this Agreement, such ambiguity shall 
be resolved in a manner consistent with such 
regulations and the Bank Board’s resolution or action 


403 


relating to the Acquisition, the Mergers or of this 
Agreement. If there is a conflict between such 
regulations and the Bank Board’s resolution or action 
relating to the Acquisition, the Mergers or to this 
Agreement, the Bank Board’s Resolution or action 
shall govern. For purposes of this section, the 
accounting principles and governing regulations shall 
be those in effect on the Effective Date, as 
subsequently changed, amended, clarified or inter- 
preted by the Bank Boad (as proposed, for example, 
at 52 Fed. Reg. 39112 et seq. (October 20, 1987)), its 
staff, or the Financial Accounting Standards Board 
(“FASB”), or any successor organization of the 
American Institute of Certified Public Accountants 
(“AICPA”). If there is a conflict between what is 
required by the FASB or the AICPA ‘and what is 
required by the Bank Board, the interpretation of the 
Bank Board shall govern. The valuation of real estate 
assets shall be in accordance with the Bank Board’s 
proposed regulations published on October 20, 1987 
(52 Fed. Reg. 39070) or as those regulations are 
finally adopted by the Bank Board, or any successor 
regulations then in effect.

Section 19. Termination of Agreement. 

(a) Except as otherwise specifically provided in § 16 
elsewhere in this Agreement, this Agreement shall 
terminate five (5) years following the Effective Date 
or on such other date which the parties or their 
Successors agree in writing, provided that if a 
receiver is appointed for the purpose of liquidating 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, or if the AC- 
QUIRING ASSOCIATION ceases to be an associa- 
tion the accounts of which are insured by the 
CORPORATION, this Agreement and all of the 
CORPORATION’s obligations under it shall termi- 
nate upon the effective date of such occurrence. 

(b) Upon the Termination Date, the net Credit or 
Debit balance of the Special Reserve Account, if any, 
shall be paid or distributed in accordance with the 
provisions of § 6 of this agreement. 

(c) Nothwithstanding termination of this Agree- 
ment as prescribed in § 19a), the CORPORATION 
shall indemnify the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, 
the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN or AMERICAN 
LIFE, as the case may be, for any loss arising from 
liabilities and Claims to the extent described in § 7 of 
this Agreement, including the reasonable costs and 
expenses of related litigation actually incurred and 
payable by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, the 
ACQUIRER, STATESMAN or AMERICAN LIFE 
and shall indemnify for the reasonable costs and 
expenses actually incurred and payable by the 
ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, the ACQUIRER, 
STATESMAN or AMERICAN LIFE, where appro- 
priate, in pursuit of a Related Claim or Acquired 
Association Claim pursuant to §$7 or 8 of this 
Agreement, provided, that any such liability or Claim 


406 


is in litigation on the Termination Date; provided 
further, that the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, the 
ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and AMERICAN LIFE 
continue to observe after the Termination Date all 
covenants, agreements of cooperation, reporting re- 
quirements, and agreements for the use of best 
efforts set forth in this Agreement; provided further, 
that any recovery by the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION, and ACQUIRER, STATESMAN or AMERI- 
CAN LIFE, where appropriate, on any Related 
Claims or required Association Claims shall be paid 
over to the CORPORATION, subject to the limita- 
tions of §§ 7 and 8; and provided further, that the 
obligation of the CORPORATION stated in this 
§ 19%(c) shall terminate as stated in the proviso to 
§ 19a). 

(d) Notwithstanding § 19(a) of this Agreement, the 
CORPORATION shall consider and act upon, in 
accordance with this Agreement, all reports, applica- 
tions, requests, and other submissions that are 
subject to the CORPORATION’s approval, consent, 
or other action, and that are actually received by the 
CORPORATION prior to or on the Termination 
Date, and the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION, the 
ACQUIRER, STATESMAN and AMERICAN LIFE 
shall cooperate in the resolution of all such pending 
submissions as contemplated in this Agreement. The 
final accounting required by § 11(c) of this Agreement 
shall take account of all actions taken and determina- 
tions made by the CORPORATION pursuant to this 
§ 19d) and any related provisions of this Agreement. 

Section 20. Compliance with Law. The ACQUIR- 
ING ASSOCIATION shall, and shall cause its 
subsidiaries, Affiliates, and service corporations to, 


407 


comply in all material respects with all appli 
statutes, regulations, orders of, and Be 
imposed by the United States or any state, munici- 
pality, or other political subdivision, or by any agency 
of the foregoing public units, regarding the ownership 
of its properties and conduct of its business, includ- 
ing, without limitation, all applicable statutes, regula- 
tions, orders, and _ restrictions relating to equal 
employment opportunities, employment retirement 
income security, and environmental standards and 
controls; provided, however, that nothing contained in 
this § 20 shall require the ACQUIRING ASSOCIA- 
TION, or any of its subsidiaries, Affiliates, or service 
corporations, to comply with any law so long as the 
applicability thereof shall be contested in good faith 

Section 21. Rights and Forbearances. The rights, 
powers, and remedies given to the parties by this 
Agreement shall be in addition to all rights, powers 
and remedies given by any applicable statute or rule 
of law. Any forbearance, failure, or delay by either 
party in exercising or partially exercising any such 
right, power, or remedy shall not preclude its further 
exercise. 

Section 22. Governing Law. This Agreement and 
the parties’ rights and obligation under it shall be 
governed by the law of the State of Iowa, to the 
extent that Federal law does not control. Nothing in 
this Agreement shall require any unlawful action -or 
inaction by either party. 

Section 23. Entire Agreement, Severability. 


(a) This Agreement, together with any resolutions 


adopted by the Bank Board and documents delivered 
at closing which contain written interpretations or 
understandings of the parties, constitutes the entire 


ae eS ee 


408 


agreement between the parties and supersedes all 
prior agreements and understandings of the parties in 
connection with it, excepting only the Acquisition 
Agreement, the Merger Agreements and any resolu- 
tions or letters concerning the Acquisition, the 
Mergers or this Agreement issued by the Bank Board 
or the CORPORATION in connection with the 
approval of the Acquisition, the Mergers and this 
Agreement, provided, however, that in the event of 
any conflict, variance, or inconsistency between this 
Agreement and the Acquisition Agreement or the 
Merger Agreements, the provisions of this Agree- 
ment shall govern and be binding on all parties 
insofar as the rights, privileges, duties, obligations, 
and liabilities of the parties are concerned. 

(b) If any provision of this Agreement is invalid or 
reinforceable, all of the remaining provisions of this 
Agreement shall nevertheless remain in full force and 
effect and shall be binding upon all the parties hereto. 

Section 24. Counterparts, Modifications, Headings. 

(a) This Agreement may be executed in any 
number of counterparts, each of which shall be an 
original but all of which shall constitute one and the 
same instrument, and any party may execute this 
Agreement by signing any such counterpart. 

(b) No amendment or modification of this Agree- 
ment shall be binding unless executed in writing by 
the parties or their successors. 

(c) Section headings are not to be considered part 
of this Agreement, are solely for convenience of 
reference, and shall not affect the meaning or 
interpretation of this Agreement or any of its 
provisions. 


409 


Section 25. Successors and Assigns. All the terms 
and provisions of this Agreement shall be binding 
upon and inure to the benefit of the parties and their 
respective transferees, successors, and assigns, but 
this Agreement may not be assigned to any party nor 
may any rights or obligations under it be transferred 
or delegated to or vested in any other party, through 
merger, consolidation, or otherwise, without the prior 
written consent of the CORPORATION. 

Section 26. Sole Benefit. It is the intention of the 
parties that this Agreement, the assumption of 
obligations and statements of responsibilities under it 
and all of its conditions and provisions are for the sole 
benefit of the parties hereto and for the benefit of no 
other person. Nothing expressed or referred to in this 
Agreement is intended or shall be construed to give 
any person other than the parties hereto any legal or 
equitable right, remedy, or claim under, or in respect 
to, this Agreement or any of its provisions. 

Section 27. Continuing Cooperation. It is the 
purpose of this Agreement to provide a means by 
which the failure of the ACQUIRED ASSOCIA- 
TIONS may be prevented, the depositors and other 
creditors of the ACQUIRED ASSOCIATIONS may 
be protected against losses, the thrift and financing 
needs of the communities in which the ACQUIRED 
ASSOCIATIONS’ offices are located may be served 
the ACQUIRING ASSOCIATION and the CORPO. 
RATION may receive the benefits and assume the 

risks contracted for, and expense to the CORPORA- 
TION may be reduced. The parties therefore agree 
that they shall in good faith, and with their best 
efforts, cooperate to carry out the purposes of this 
Agreement as described in this section. 


410 


Section 28. Brokerage Commission. The CORPO- 
RATION agrees to pay one half of any brokerage 
commission or finders fee that the ACQUIRING 
ASSOCIATION, whether in its own right or as 
successor to any of the ACQUIRED ASSOCIA- 
TION’s, the ACQUIRER, STATESMAN or AMERI- 
CAN LIFE, pays to an unaffiliated third party 
pursuant to a binding obligation incurred in connec- 
tion with the requisition or any of the Mergers, 
provided that the CORPORATION’s maximum lia- 
bility pursuant to this paragraph shall be $170,000.00, 
and provided further that the CORPORATION shall 
not be obligated under any other provision of this 
Agreement (including without limitation § 7 hereof) to 
reimburse or indemnify any party to this Agreement 
in connection with any such brokerage or finders fee. 
Any amount owed by the CORPORATION pursuant 
to this paragraph shall be debited to the Special 
Reserve Account.
